---

## What is RouteFlow?

RouteFlow is a multi-tenant SaaS platform for last-mile delivery operations. It has three user roles:

- **Operator (TENANT_ADMIN / OPERATOR):** manages routes, drivers, orders, invoices, and inventory via a web dashboard (Expo mobile-as-web on the audited URL; a separate Next.js web app exists but is not yet deployed).
- **Driver:** receives dispatched routes on a React Native mobile app, completes stops with proof-of-delivery (POD) photos/signatures, and records payments.
- **Buyer (CUSTOMER):** places orders, tracks delivery status, and manages invoices via a React Native buyer portal.

The backend is a NestJS API (PostgreSQL via Prisma, Redis for throttling and Socket.IO pub/sub, Firebase Admin SDK for push notifications). The mobile apps are Expo (React Native).

---

## Executive Summary

This audit covered API source code (W1–W20), live browser testing of the deployed production tenant (W7, W9, W15, W16, W17, W18, W21, W25, W27, W28, W30–W35), deep-dives into real-time/concurrency (W8, W10), security/IDOR (W11, W27), financial integrity (W12, W30), controller coverage (W13), upload security (W14, W27), mobile offline (W19), auth/scheduling (W20, W26), cross-role real-time (W21, W25), E2E cycles (W23), corner/abuse testing (W24, W28), cascading deletes and price-race (W31), bulk ops and accessibility (W32, W33), and full Phase 1/2 browser walk (W34, W35). It found **221 distinct findings** (after deduplication) across all surfaces. All workers W1–W35 are complete.

### Top findings (must-fix before any production traffic)

1. **RF-001 (P0) — RouteRun `tenantId: null`:** Every new dispatch is invisible to the driver it was assigned to. Dispatch is 100% broken for all tenants.
2. **RF-002 (P0) — No WebSocket client in frontend:** The real-time infrastructure (Socket.IO gateway, all emitters, all rooms) is fully implemented on the backend but the frontend has never been wired to receive events. Every real-time update path is dead code from the client's perspective.
3. **RF-003 (P0) — `completeStop` on SCHEDULED run:** A driver (or any valid JWT holder with a stop ID) can complete stops on a run that has never been started, producing falsified delivery records.
4. **RF-147 (P0) — `createInvoiceFromOrder()` stores `tenantId: null`:** Every invoice created from an order is invisible to buyers in the portal. The buyer finance flow is completely broken — buyers cannot list, view, or pay any invoice generated from their orders.
5. **RF-156 (CLEARED) — Cross-tenant invoice leak:** W29 verified — all 14 invoices in the ux-audit tenant carry only `tenantId: 8ee7bbf5`. No "Blue Dreamz" or affa-tenant records present. The W21 observation was prior data contamination, not a real isolation failure. `forTenant()` is working correctly on invoice queries.
6. **RF-157 (P0) — Stored XSS via SVG upload:** `POST /products/:id/images` and `POST /customers/:id/tax-documents` accept SVG files containing `<script>` tags and serve them back as `image/svg+xml`. Any authenticated user can inject and execute arbitrary JavaScript in other users' browsers. Three live XSS payloads are currently stored in the `ux-audit` test tenant and require cleanup.
7. **RF-176 (P0 CONFIRMED) — Missing `X-Tenant-Slug` header on login issues a `tenantId: null` JWT that bypasses tenant isolation:** When `POST /auth/login` is called without the `X-Tenant-Slug` header (but with valid credentials), the server returns HTTP 200 with a JWT carrying `tenantId: null` and `role: TENANT_ADMIN`. That null-tenant token was used to enumerate 20 products, 20 customers, 20 orders, and 14 users belonging to two completely foreign tenants — none belonging to the authenticated user's tenant. This is a complete tenant isolation bypass. Any credential holder who omits the header gets a skeleton key to all null-tenantId rows in the database.
8. **RF-197 (P0) — `DELETE /customers/:id` silently cascades to delete all linked orders, invoices, and returns without any confirmation or warning:** Deleting a customer is an irreversible destructive cascade — every historical order, invoice, and return for that customer is permanently lost. The UI presents no warning and the API returns 200 with no list of affected records.
9. **RF-203 (P0) — SYSTEMIC: Every "Create" form in the operator dashboard shows only a spinner and never renders:** All four core creation flows — `/routes/create`, `/customers/create`, `/products/create`, `/invoices/create` — are completely non-functional. Operators cannot create any of these entities through the web UI.
10. **RF-009 (P1) — 100% push notification failure:** Expo proxy tokens are fed directly to Firebase FCM. Every token is rejected with `invalid-registration-token` and self-deleted. Zero push notifications ever reach any user.
11. **RF-014 (P1) — Order number duplicate race:** `orders.service.ts` uses MAX+1 with no unique constraint; concurrent order creation silently produces duplicate order numbers.

### Overall release readiness verdict

**NOT READY FOR PRODUCTION.** Thirteen P0 findings include a broken dispatch flow (RF-001), a false delivery recording vector (RF-003), a complete absence of real-time updates (RF-002), buyers being unable to see any invoices from orders (RF-147), a stored XSS vulnerability via SVG upload (RF-157), a confirmed tenant isolation bypass via null-tenant JWT (RF-176 — any valid credential without an X-Tenant-Slug header gets read access across all tenant data), a silent cascading customer delete that permanently destroys all linked orders/invoices/returns (RF-197), and a systemic failure of all four operator "Create" flows — routes, customers, products, and invoices — which render only a spinner and never display a form (RF-203). RF-156 (cross-tenant invoice leak) has been cleared — confirmed as prior data contamination, no real isolation failure. Additionally, 55 P1 issues include 100% push notification failure, duplicate financial record numbers, broken cron tenant isolation, multiple financial integrity gaps, no brute-force protection on login (RF-160), permanent customer deletion without confirmation (RF-159), the driver "my runs" endpoint returning 404 for all drivers (RF-167), the buyer standing orders API returning 404 (RF-180), the buyer catalog API returning no stock availability data (RF-200), the invoice "Voided" filter always returning zero results due to an enum mismatch (RF-204), and the route "Optimize stops" button navigating away from the page instead of optimizing (RF-205). The product as audited cannot be shipped without resolving at minimum all P0 and the P1 issues marked "core flow broken."

## P0 — Blockers

### RF-001 — `RouteRun` created with `tenantId: null`; driver cannot see dispatched runs

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** driver-pod / dispatch
- **Surface:** API / mobile-driver
- **Source:** W9-002
- **Issue:** `routes.service.ts::createRun()` creates the `RouteRun` record without including `tenantId` in the `data` object. Every Prisma query uses `forTenant()` which appends `WHERE tenantId = '{tenantId}'`, excluding all null-tenantId rows. All new route runs are invisible to drivers and to tenant-scoped operator queries.
- **Expected:** A newly dispatched `RouteRun` should carry the correct `tenantId` and appear in the driver's `GET /route-runs/my-runs` response immediately after dispatch.
- **Actual:** Every `RouteRun` is stored with `tenantId: null`. The `forTenant()` filter excludes it from all tenant-scoped queries, making 100% of dispatched runs invisible to drivers and operators.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as operator and call `POST /route-runs` to dispatch a new run.
  2. Authenticate as the assigned driver and call `GET /route-runs/my-runs`.
  3. Observe the run is absent from the response.
  4. Inspect the `RouteRun` record directly in the database — confirm `tenantId` is `null`.
- **Fix:** Add `tenantId: this.prisma.getTenantId()` to the `data` object in `tx.routeRun.create()` in `routes.service.ts::createRun()`. Run a one-time backfill migration to populate `tenantId` on existing null rows.
- **Prevention:** Add a DB-level `NOT NULL` constraint on `RouteRun.tenantId`. Add an integration test that creates a route run and then fetches it via the driver's `my-runs` endpoint, asserting it is present. Add a Prisma middleware check that throws if any create/update omits `tenantId` on tenant-scoped models.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

---

### RF-002 — Frontend never connects to the Socket.IO gateway; all real-time infrastructure is dead code from the client

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** real-time
- **Surface:** web-operator / mobile-buyer / mobile-driver
- **Source:** W9-001, W9-004 (client-side manifestation); W8 cross-reference; W21/13.1–13.9 (browser-confirmed: MutationObserver over 60–120s detected zero DOM changes on operator dashboard after buyer actions; all 5 cross-role scenarios showed "never auto-update" result)
- **Issue:** The backend implements a complete Socket.IO gateway (`routeflow.gateway.ts`) with per-tenant and per-user rooms, a Redis pub/sub adapter for multi-instance scaling, and typed emitter calls throughout `orders.service.ts` and other services. The frontend (both web and mobile) contains no `socket.io-client` import, no `io()` call, and no `EventSource` listener. Zero real-time events ever reach any client. All data updates are purely REST-on-navigate.
- **Expected:** Clients should connect to the Socket.IO gateway on login, receive real-time events (new orders, status changes, dispatch), and update the UI without requiring a page navigation or manual refresh.
- **Actual:** No WebSocket connection is ever established from any client. All real-time backend emissions are dropped. The UI only updates when the user navigates between tabs or performs a manual refresh.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator. Open browser DevTools → Network tab → WS filter.
  2. Wait 60 seconds — observe zero WebSocket connections are opened.
  3. Search the deployed frontend JS bundle for `socket.io`, `io(`, `EventSource` — none found.
  4. Have a buyer place an order. Observe the operator dashboard pending count does not update for the duration of the session.
- **Fix:** Add a `useSocket` hook or global app provider that connects `socket.io-client` to the API on login, joins the tenant room (`tenant:{id}:operators` or `tenant:{id}:customer:{userId}`), and calls `queryClient.invalidateQueries(...)` on incoming events. The backend is production-ready; only the client-side wiring is missing. As an immediate mitigation, add `refetchInterval: 30_000` on critical queries (pending orders, active runs).
- **Prevention:** Add an E2E test using a WebSocket client that verifies events are received by the frontend after relevant backend mutations. Include a smoke test in CI that connects to the deployed gateway and asserts the connection is accepted. Add a frontend lint rule or PR checklist item requiring that any new event emitted in the backend has a corresponding client-side handler.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 3**
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5**

---

### RF-003 — `completeStop` accepts stops on SCHEDULED (never-started) runs

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** driver-pod
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W4-001, confirmed P0 by L1-C
- **Issue:** `completeStop()` in `routes.service.ts` (lines 958–1037) never reads the parent `RouteRun.status`. Only `stop.status === "COMPLETED"` is checked. Any holder of a valid JWT and a known stop ID can submit a delivery completion for a run that has never been started, producing falsified delivery, payment, and POD records.
- **Expected:** The API should reject a stop-completion request with `400 Bad Request` if the parent `RouteRun.status` is not `IN_PROGRESS`.
- **Actual:** Stop completion succeeds regardless of the parent run's status. A stop on a `SCHEDULED` (never-started) run can be marked `COMPLETED`, creating falsified delivery and payment records.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as operator and call `POST /route-runs` to create a run (status `SCHEDULED`).
  2. Do NOT call `startRun` or otherwise advance the run to `IN_PROGRESS`.
  3. Call `PATCH /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete` with a stop ID from that run.
  4. Observe HTTP 200 response and the stop status is now `COMPLETED` despite the run never having started.
- **Fix:** Fetch the parent `RouteRun` before accepting the completion; throw `BadRequestException` if `run.status !== IN_PROGRESS`.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for `completeStop()` that asserts a `400` error is returned for stops on `SCHEDULED` runs. Add an E2E integration test covering the full dispatch lifecycle (create → start → complete). Add a PR checklist item: "Any new route-run state mutation must validate parent run status."
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

---

## P1 — High Priority

### RF-004 — Any authenticated driver can complete another driver's stop (missing authorization check)

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** driver-pod
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** NEW-W4-A, confirmed by L1-C
- **Issue:** `completeStop()` receives the calling user and resolves their driver record, but only to populate the `driverId` field on the mutation — it never verifies the caller is the driver assigned to the parent `RouteRun`. Any driver with a known stop ID can complete stops belonging to another driver's route.
- **Expected:** The API should return `403 Forbidden` when a driver attempts to complete a stop on a route run that is not assigned to them.
- **Actual:** Any driver with a valid JWT and a known stop ID can complete stops on any other driver's run, effectively fabricating delivery records for routes they never visited.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as driver_A and obtain a stop ID belonging to driver_B's run.
  2. Call `PATCH /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete` using driver_A's JWT.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 — the stop is marked COMPLETED under driver_A's name.
- **Fix:** After fetching the `RouteRun`, assert `run.driverId === callingDriver.id`; throw `ForbiddenException` otherwise.
- **Prevention:** Add an authorization unit test for `completeStop()` covering the cross-driver scenario (driver_A attempting driver_B's stop → 403). Add a security PR checklist item: "All driver-facing mutations must verify the requesting driver owns the resource."

---

### RF-005 — Non-atomic stop completion + payment: second call can fail silently

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** driver-pod
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W4-012, confirmed P1 by L1-C
- **Issue:** `payment.tsx` issues two sequential `await` calls: `completeMut.mutateAsync()` then `paymentMut.mutateAsync()`. The catch block explicitly accepts partial failure. A network hiccup between the two produces a DELIVERED stop with no payment record, requiring manual reconciliation.
- **Expected:** Stop completion and payment recording should succeed or fail together atomically — a DELIVERED stop must always have an associated payment record.
- **Actual:** If the network fails between the two sequential calls, the stop is marked DELIVERED but no payment record is created. The driver receives no error and has no way to re-submit the payment, leaving a revenue gap.
- **Repro:**
  1. Open the driver app on a stop with a cash/card payment.
  2. Using a network proxy (e.g., Charles), throttle/block traffic after the stop-complete call succeeds but before the payment call fires.
  3. Submit the stop completion — observe the stop is marked DELIVERED but no payment is recorded.
- **Fix:** Unify into a single atomic backend endpoint (`POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete-with-payment`), or enqueue both actions as a dependency-ordered pair in the offline queue so the payment retries until it succeeds.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test simulating a mid-flow network failure that asserts the DB contains either both records or neither. Add a DB-level trigger or application-level check that flags DELIVERED stops with no payment for reconciliation alerts.

---

### RF-006 — $0 cash/card payment silently skipped; stop still marked DELIVERED

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** driver-pod
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W4-004, confirmed P1 by L1-C
- **Issue:** `payment.tsx` (line 124–126): when method is Cash/Card/Cheque, `collected` defaults to `0`. The guard `if (invoiceId && collected > 0)` silently skips the payment record instead of blocking submission. A driver who forgets to enter the amount completes the stop with no revenue record and receives no warning.
- **Expected:** When a driver selects Cash/Card/Cheque as the payment method, the app should require a non-zero collected amount before allowing submission, and show an inline validation error if the field is empty or zero.
- **Actual:** Submitting with a $0 amount silently skips the payment record creation. The stop is marked DELIVERED with no revenue recorded and no feedback to the driver that the payment was omitted.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as driver and navigate to a stop requiring payment.
  2. Select "Cash" as the payment method.
  3. Leave the collected amount at the default (0).
  4. Tap Submit — observe the stop is marked DELIVERED and no error is shown.
  5. Verify in the operator dashboard that no payment record exists for that stop.
- **Fix:** Add a pre-submit validation that blocks `closeStop()` when `method !== "On account"` and `collected === 0`; show a required-field error to the driver.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the payment form component asserting that the submit button is disabled when method is Cash/Card/Cheque and amount is 0. Add a server-side validation rule on the payment endpoint that rejects `amount === 0` for non-account payment methods.

---

### RF-007 — Tab transition freeze: every bottom-nav tab unresponsive for 20–40 s

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-009 (W7/audit), confirmed P1 with root cause by L1-C
- **Issue:** `dispatch.tsx` fires `useAdminRoutes({ limit: 50 })` and `useAdminDrivers()` simultaneously on mount; `warehouse.tsx` fires four parallel product queries. All queries resolve simultaneously on Railway cold-start, triggering a cascade of state updates that freezes the JS thread for 20–40 s on every tab transition. Users perceive the app as crashed.
- **Expected:** Navigating to any bottom-nav tab should show a skeleton or loading indicator immediately and render the full content within 2–3 seconds, without freezing the UI.
- **Actual:** Every tab transition causes a 20–40 second JS thread freeze. The page appears unresponsive; users cannot interact with the UI or navigate away during this period.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator on the production URL.
  2. Click the Dispatch tab in the bottom navigation.
  3. Observe the page freezes for 20–40 seconds before rendering.
  4. Repeat for Warehouse tab — same freeze occurs.
- **Fix:** Introduce skeleton/loading states; stagger or paginate initial queries; use React Query's `enabled` flag to defer secondary queries until primary data lands.
- **Prevention:** Add a performance budget test that fails CI if the Time-to-Interactive on the dispatch or warehouse tab exceeds 3 seconds on a throttled (3G) network profile. Use React Query `suspense` + `ErrorBoundary` patterns with skeletons as the default data-fetching pattern for all list screens.

---

### RF-008 — Cron jobs run without per-tenant context: cross-tenant data access or daily silent crash

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** L1B-002, L1B-003
- **Issue:** `generateDailyOrders()` and `generateDueRecurringInvoices()` call `this.prisma.forTenant()` inside cron handlers that run outside any HTTP request scope. No `AsyncLocalStorage` tenant context is established. Depending on `PrismaService.forTenant()` fallback behavior, either all tenants' data is processed together without isolation (data confidentiality breach) or the cron throws and silently fails every day.
- **Expected:** Each cron handler should process records strictly scoped to one tenant at a time, iterating over all active tenants with proper ALS context set for each, so no cross-tenant data is ever read or written.
- **Actual:** Cron handlers run outside any tenant context. Depending on the `forTenant()` fallback, either cross-tenant records are processed together (data breach) or the handler throws and silently fails, meaning standing orders and recurring invoices are never generated.
- **Repro:**
  1. Inspect the cron handler implementations in `order-templates.service.ts` and `recurring-invoices.service.ts`.
  2. Confirm that no `AsyncLocalStorage` tenant context is set before calling `forTenant()`.
  3. Trigger the cron manually and observe either cross-tenant data access or a silent exception.
- **Fix:** In each cron handler, first fetch all active tenants, then iterate: set the ALS context per tenant, process only that tenant's records, clear context.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test for each cron handler that runs with two active tenants and asserts only the correct tenant's records were processed. Add a Prisma middleware or ALS guard that throws a loud error (not a silent fallback) when `forTenant()` is called without an established tenant context.

---

### RF-009 — 100% push notification failure: Expo proxy tokens sent to Firebase FCM directly

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** real-time
- **Surface:** API / mobile-buyer / mobile-driver
- **Source:** W8-004, confirmed P1 by L1-D
- **Issue:** `apps/mobile/lib/auth.ts` line 86 calls `Notifications.getExpoPushTokenAsync()`, which returns an `ExponentPushToken[...]` string (an Expo-managed proxy). `notifications.service.ts` lines 78–84 feeds these tokens directly into `firebase-admin`'s `sendEachForMulticast()`, which requires raw FCM registration tokens. FCM rejects every Expo token with `messaging/invalid-registration-token`. The server's cleanup code (lines 89–96) then deletes the token on first failure. All push notifications silently fail and the token table self-empties.
- **Expected:** Push notifications should be delivered to registered devices when order status changes, dispatch occurs, or other relevant events fire. The token registration table should persist valid tokens.
- **Actual:** Every push notification fails with `messaging/invalid-registration-token` because Expo proxy tokens are sent directly to Firebase FCM. The self-cleanup code then deletes the token, leaving the token table empty. Zero push notifications are ever delivered.
- **Repro:**
  1. Register a device (buyer or driver) and log in — a device token is stored.
  2. Trigger an order-confirmed notification from the operator side.
  3. Check the Firebase console — delivery status shows failure.
  4. Query the `DeviceToken` table — the row has been deleted by the cleanup code.
- **Fix (recommended):** Replace `firebase-admin` multicast with `expo-server-sdk` (`expo.sendPushNotificationsAsync()`). Alternative: switch mobile to `Notifications.getDevicePushTokenAsync()` with `google-services.json`/`GoogleService-Info.plist` configured per platform.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated smoke test after each deployment that registers a test token, fires a notification, and asserts delivery receipt from Expo's Push Receipts API. Add server-side logging for every push attempt (not just failures) so notification health is visible in production monitoring dashboards.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

---

### RF-010 — Credit note allows over-crediting against original invoice total, ignoring payments already made

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-003, confirmed P1 by L1-B
- **Issue:** `credit-notes.service.ts` (lines 51–62) validates the credit note amount against the original invoice total, not against `(invoice_total − payments_received)`. A $1,000 invoice with $600 already paid can still receive a $500 credit note, creating a $100 over-credit against a receivable that is already 60% collected.
- **Expected:** A credit note amount must not exceed the invoice's remaining unpaid balance (`invoice.total − sum(confirmed_payments)`). The API should return `400 Bad Request` if the proposed credit would exceed the outstanding amount.
- **Actual:** The validation compares only against the original invoice total. A partially-paid invoice can receive a credit note that, combined with prior payments, exceeds 100% of the invoice value, creating a negative AR balance.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice for $1,000.
  2. Record a confirmed payment of $600 against that invoice.
  3. Create a credit note for $500 against the same invoice.
  4. Observe HTTP 201 — the credit note is accepted despite payments + credit ($1,100) exceeding the invoice total ($1,000).
- **Fix:** Validate against remaining balance: `proposed_credit <= (invoice.total − sum(payments))`. Run this check inside a serializable transaction.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for `createCreditNote()` with a pre-paid invoice asserting rejection when credit + payments exceed total. Add a DB check constraint or application-level invariant: `SELECT sum(creditNotes.amount) + sum(payments.amount) <= invoice.total`.

---

### RF-011 — Duplicate invoice creation from an order-linked original creates double-billing risk

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-015, confirmed P1 by L1-B
- **Issue:** The duplicate-invoice endpoint (`invoices.service.ts` lines 863–899) does not check whether the source invoice was generated from an order (`orderId IS NOT NULL`). Duplicating such an invoice produces a second independent invoice for the same order with no guard preventing both from being sent to the customer.
- **Expected:** Attempting to duplicate an order-linked invoice should be blocked with a `400 Bad Request` explaining that order-linked invoices cannot be duplicated.
- **Actual:** The duplication succeeds, producing a second, independent invoice for the same order. Both invoices can be sent to the customer, resulting in double-billing.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an order and generate an invoice from it via `POST /invoices/from-order/:orderId`.
  2. Note the resulting invoice ID and its `orderId` field.
  3. Call `POST /invoices/:id/duplicate` on that invoice.
  4. Observe HTTP 201 — a second invoice for the same order exists.
  5. Send both invoices — the customer receives two invoices for one order.
- **Fix:** If `invoice.orderId` is not null, block duplication and return `BadRequestException`.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the `duplicateInvoice()` service method asserting it throws when `invoice.orderId` is non-null. Add the check as a named guard function (`assertInvoiceDuplicatable`) so it is reusable and cannot be omitted in future code paths that allow duplication.

### RF-012 — Invoice update with discount/shippingFee change does not recalculate total

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-004, L1B-005
- **Issue:** `invoices.service.ts` (lines 626–641) else-branch: when `PATCH /invoices/:id` is called with only `discount` or `shippingFee` (no `items`), the new values are persisted without recomputing `total` or `taxAmount`. Stored total is stale. Currently guarded by a DRAFT-only check, but any future refactor relaxing that guard will silently produce invoices with wrong totals.
- **Expected:** Any change to `discount` or `shippingFee` on an invoice should immediately trigger a recalculation of `total` and `taxAmount`, ensuring the stored values always reflect the current fields.
- **Actual:** The `discount` and `shippingFee` values are updated in the database, but `total` and `taxAmount` remain at their previous (now incorrect) values until the next full item-level update.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a DRAFT invoice with a total of $100.
  2. Call `PATCH /invoices/:id` with `{ "discount": 50 }` and no `items` field.
  3. Fetch the updated invoice — observe `total` is still $100, not $50.
- **Fix:** Extract a `recalculateTotals(invoiceId)` helper and call it from both update branches. Eliminate the divergence.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test asserting that `PATCH /invoices/:id` with only a `discount` change updates the `total` field. Enforce a single `recalculateTotals()` code path as the only way to persist invoice totals, so future changes cannot bypass recalculation.

### RF-013 — Cart not cleared on logout: items visible to next user on shared device

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W5-003, confirmed P1 by L1-B
- **Issue:** `buyer-auth.ts::buyerLogout()` deletes auth tokens but does not call `useCartStore.getState().clear()`. In-memory cart state persists for the app process lifetime. On a shared device or after a multi-account switch, User B sees User A's cart.
- **Expected:** Upon logout, all in-memory user state including cart contents should be cleared so that the next user who logs in starts with a clean session.
- **Actual:** After User A logs out, the cart retains User A's items in memory. When User B logs in on the same device, User B's cart screen displays User A's items as if they were their own.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as User A (buyer) and add items to the cart.
  2. Log out from User A's session.
  3. Log in as User B on the same device.
  4. Open the cart — User A's items are still present.
- **Fix:** Call `useCartStore.getState().clear()` immediately before or after token deletion in `buyerLogout()`.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for `buyerLogout()` that asserts the cart store is empty after the logout function completes. Add a logout integration test covering the full logout → login-as-different-user flow and asserting cart isolation. Make it a code review checklist item that any auth state clearing must also clear all user-scoped stores.

### RF-014 — Order number duplicate race: MAX+1 with no unique constraint

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W10-001 (L1-D), L1B-004 cross-reference
- **Issue:** `orders.service.ts` (lines 542–551) generates order numbers by fetching the last `ORD-NNNNN` and incrementing. No DB transaction or row lock wraps the read-then-write. `schema.prisma` has no `@@unique` constraint on `orderNumber` (confirmed absent at lines 828–836). Two concurrent order creations read the same `lastOrder`, compute the same `seq`, and both succeed — silently producing duplicate order numbers.
- **Expected:** Every order should receive a unique `orderNumber`. Concurrent order creation should never produce two orders with the same number.
- **Actual:** Under concurrent load, two simultaneous `POST /orders` requests can both read the same `lastOrder` sequence, compute the same next number, and both persist, silently creating duplicate order numbers with no error.
- **Repro:**
  1. Fire `Promise.all([POST /orders, POST /orders])` with two valid payloads simultaneously.
  2. Both return HTTP 201.
  3. Inspect the `orderNumber` fields of both created orders — they may be identical.
- **Fix:** Use a DB-level sequence or atomic counter table with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`, or add `@@unique([tenantId, orderNumber])` and implement retry-on-conflict.
- **Prevention:** Add `@@unique([tenantId, orderNumber])` to the Prisma schema immediately as a safety net (even before the full sequence fix). Add a concurrency integration test that fires 10 simultaneous order creates and asserts all resulting order numbers are unique.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

### RF-015 — Operator dispatch emits nothing to the driver (no WebSocket event, no push notification)

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** real-time / dispatch
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W8-001, confirmed by L1-D; NEW-W8-B (supporting)
- **Issue:** `routes.service.ts::createRun()` (lines 490–606) completes the transaction and order-linking with zero calls to `this.gateway.*` or `this.notifications.*`. The `driver:${userId}` WebSocket room is registered at connection time but no service ever emits into it (confirmed: none of the 9 gateway emitter methods target a driver room). `sendToDriver()` exists in `notifications.service.ts` but is never called from `routes.service.ts`.
- **Expected:** When an operator dispatches a route run, the assigned driver should immediately receive a push notification and/or a WebSocket event with the run details so they can begin navigation.
- **Actual:** No push notification is sent to the driver and no WebSocket event is emitted on dispatch. The driver has no way to know a run has been assigned without manually refreshing the app.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as operator and dispatch a run to a specific driver.
  2. Monitor the driver's device for a push notification — none arrives.
  3. Monitor WebSocket traffic on the driver's client — no event is received.
  4. Confirm: `grep -n 'sendToDriver\|emitRouteDispatched' routes.service.ts` returns zero results in the `createRun()` method.
- **Fix:** Add a `gateway.emitRouteDispatched(tenantId, { runId, driverId, routeName })` emitter targeting `tenant:X:driver:${userId}`. Call it from `createRun()` after the transaction, alongside `notifications.sendToDriver()`. (Note: depends on RF-001 fix being in place first.)
- **Prevention:** Add a `createRun()` integration test that asserts a WebSocket event is emitted to the driver room and a push notification call is made after successful dispatch. Create an architectural convention: every state-changing service method must have a corresponding notification/emission call — make this explicit in code review guidelines.

### RF-016 — `routes.service.ts::completeStop()` never auto-completes the RouteRun; dispatch deadlock

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** driver-pod / dispatch
- **Surface:** API / web-operator
- **Source:** NEW-W8-A, confirmed P1 by L1-D (promoted from P2 in initial write-up due to dispatch deadlock consequence)
- **Issue:** When an operator uses the web UI to complete the final stop of a run, `routes.service.ts::completeStop()` marks the stop COMPLETED but never checks whether all stops are done and never updates `RouteRun.status` to COMPLETED. The `orders.service.ts::completeStop()` path (lines 1332–1345) correctly performs this check. If an operator completes all stops via the web dashboard, the run remains `IN_PROGRESS` forever. `createRun()` (lines 530–541) blocks on an active run check, preventing dispatch of the next run for the same route indefinitely.
- **Expected:** When the final stop on a route run is marked COMPLETED (via any code path), the parent `RouteRun.status` should automatically transition to `COMPLETED`, freeing the route for the next dispatch.
- **Actual:** Completing all stops via the web operator UI leaves the `RouteRun.status` as `IN_PROGRESS` indefinitely. Any subsequent dispatch attempt for that route is blocked by the active-run check, creating a permanent deadlock unless a developer manually updates the status in the database.
- **Repro:**
  1. Dispatch a run with two stops via the operator web UI.
  2. Complete both stops via the web UI (not the driver mobile app).
  3. Attempt to dispatch a new run for the same route.
  4. Observe an error indicating a run is already active.
  5. Check `RouteRun.status` in the database — it remains `IN_PROGRESS`.
- **Fix:** After marking the stop COMPLETED in `routes.service.ts::completeStop()`, check if all sibling stops are COMPLETED; if so, update `RouteRun.status = COMPLETED`. Mirror the `allDone` logic from `orders.service.ts::completeStop()`.
- **Prevention:** Consolidate the two divergent `completeStop()` implementations into a single shared service method so the auto-complete logic cannot be omitted in one path. Add an integration test covering the full operator-UI stop-completion flow and asserting the run status is COMPLETED after the last stop.

### RF-017 — No stock check at order creation: buyers can order out-of-stock products

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W5-002 (confirmed P1 by L1-B), W9-003 (confirmed via live concurrent-order test), W10-004 cross-reference, W22/13.14 (browser-confirmed: `Promise.all` of two simultaneous buyer checkouts both returned HTTP 201 on stock=1 product)
- **Issue:** `orders.service.ts` (lines 474–669) create path performs customer lookup, pricing-tier resolution, and product existence check but never compares requested `qty` against `product.stock`. Two simultaneous `POST /buyer/orders` requests for the last unit of a product both return HTTP 201 Created. Effective stock goes negative.
- **Expected:** Order creation should validate that sufficient stock exists for each line item and reject the order with a `422 Unprocessable Entity` if stock is insufficient. Concurrent orders should be serialized so only one can claim the last unit.
- **Actual:** No stock check is performed at any point during order creation. Two concurrent orders for the last available unit both succeed with HTTP 201, resulting in overselling with negative effective stock.
- **Repro:**
  1. Set a product's stock level to 1.
  2. Fire `Promise.all([POST /buyer/orders, POST /buyer/orders])` simultaneously, each ordering 1 unit of that product.
  3. Both return HTTP 201 — both orders are confirmed.
  4. Inspect the product's stock — it has not been decremented, effective oversell confirmed.
- **Fix:** Before `order.create()`, for each line item assert `product.stock >= requestedQty` inside a locking transaction; throw `InsufficientStockException` if not met.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test using `Promise.all` that verifies only one of two concurrent orders for the last unit succeeds. Add a DB check constraint `CHECK (stock >= 0)` on the `Product` table to enforce non-negative stock at the database level.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

### RF-018 — No self-service password reset; operator reset sends no notification

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** L1A-004
- **Issue:** No `POST /auth/forgot-password` or `POST /auth/reset-password` endpoint exists. Users with password-only auth (no Google link) are permanently locked out without operator intervention. The existing `POST /users/:id/reset-password` operator endpoint does not email the account owner, enabling an operator to silently reset any user's password without the user knowing.
- **Expected:** Users should be able to self-service reset their password via an email-based flow. When an operator resets a user's password, the user should receive an immediate notification email informing them of the change.
- **Actual:** No self-service reset path exists. Users locked out of non-Google accounts are permanently blocked without operator intervention. Operator password resets are silent — the affected user receives no notification and has no way to know their password was changed.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as a buyer with no Google auth linked.
  2. Navigate to the login screen and attempt to find a "Forgot password" option — none exists.
  3. As an operator, call `POST /users/:id/reset-password` for that buyer.
  4. Confirm the buyer receives no notification email about the reset.
- **Fix:** Implement `POST /auth/forgot-password` (email → time-limited reset token, 15 min) and `POST /auth/reset-password` (token + new password). Send a notification email when any operator-initiated password reset occurs.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated test that calls the operator reset endpoint and asserts that a notification email is sent (via mock email service). Add a security audit checklist item: "All password mutation operations must trigger a user-facing notification."

### RF-019 — `completeStop` double-submit race produces duplicate DeliveryMutation rows

- **Severity:** P1 (accounting corruption)
- **Domain:** driver-pod / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W4-002, confirmed as accounting concern by L1-C; note L1-C initially rated P2 but impact on accounting aggregates warrants P1
- **Issue:** `routes.service.ts` (lines 975–987) reads stop status BEFORE the transaction. Two concurrent requests both pass the `stop.status !== COMPLETED` check before either commits. Both enter the transaction and both create `DeliveryMutation` rows. The `updateMany` filter is idempotent for status but the duplicate mutation rows inflate accounting aggregates.
- **Expected:** Completing the same stop twice concurrently should be idempotent — only one `DeliveryMutation` row should be created and the second request should return either a `409 Conflict` or a no-op `200`.
- **Actual:** Two concurrent complete-stop requests both pass the pre-transaction status check, both enter the transaction, and both create `DeliveryMutation` rows. Accounting totals are inflated by the duplicate records.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send two simultaneous `PATCH /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete` requests for the same stop.
  2. Both return HTTP 200.
  3. Inspect the `DeliveryMutation` table — two rows exist for the same stop, inflating accounting aggregates.
- **Fix:** Move the status check inside `tenantTransaction` and use a DB-level unique constraint or optimistic concurrency token (`version` field) on `RouteRunStop` to reject the second concurrent write.
- **Prevention:** Add a `@@unique([stopId])` constraint on `DeliveryMutation` to prevent duplicate rows at the DB level. Add a concurrency test that fires two simultaneous complete-stop requests and asserts exactly one `DeliveryMutation` row is created.

---

## P2 — Medium Priority

### RF-020 — OAuth redirect passes raw access + refresh tokens in URL query string

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** web-operator / API
- **Source:** L1A-005
- **Issue:** Both primary and legacy Google OAuth callbacks (`auth.controller.ts` lines 236–255 and 288–305) redirect to `${webUrl}/auth/callback?accessToken=...&refreshToken=...`. Both tokens appear in browser history, server access logs, CDN logs, and any analytics/APM tools that capture full URLs.
- **Expected:** OAuth tokens should never appear in URLs. The callback should use a server-side code exchange (short-lived one-time code) or place tokens in the URL fragment so they are not sent to servers or logged.
- **Actual:** Both access and refresh tokens are passed as plain URL query parameters, making them visible in browser history, server access logs, CDN logs, and any analytics tools that capture full referrer URLs.
- **Repro:**
  1. Initiate a Google OAuth login flow from the operator web app.
  2. Observe the redirect URL in the browser address bar after callback — it contains `?accessToken=...&refreshToken=...` in plain text.
- **Fix:** Replace query params with a short-lived one-time code (exchange server-side), or move tokens into the URL fragment (`#`). At minimum, the frontend must strip tokens from the URL immediately on arrival before any navigation or analytics fires.
- **Prevention:** Add a security-focused PR checklist item: "OAuth and auth callbacks must never place tokens in query parameters." Add an automated test that inspects the OAuth redirect URL and asserts it contains no `accessToken` or `refreshToken` query params.

### RF-021 — Redis throttler fails open on outage: all rate limits silently disabled

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** L1A-002, W1-003 cross-reference
- **Issue:** `redis-throttler.storage.ts` line 98: on Redis error, `increment()` returns `{ totalHits: 1, isBlocked: false }`, making every request appear to be the first in the window. All throttle limits (including the 30 req/min brute-force guard on `/auth/login`) silently disappear during a Redis outage.
- **Expected:** During a Redis outage, rate limiting should degrade gracefully — using an in-process fallback map rather than silently disabling all limits.
- **Actual:** Any Redis error causes the throttler to report every request as the first in the window (`totalHits: 1, isBlocked: false`). All rate limits including the brute-force guard on login are silently disabled for the duration of the outage.
- **Repro:**
  1. Stop the Redis service while the API is running.
  2. Rapidly fire more than 30 `POST /auth/login` requests in one minute.
  3. Observe all requests succeed with no throttling applied.
- **Fix:** On Redis error, use a per-process in-memory LRU fallback map to maintain approximate rate limits. Emit a metric/alert during Redis outages.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test that simulates a Redis failure and asserts throttling still blocks requests beyond the limit. Add an alert to the monitoring system that fires when the in-memory throttler fallback is activated.

---

### RF-022 — TenantStatusGuard fails open on DB error: suspended tenants can make API calls

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W1-003, confirmed by L1-A
- **Issue:** `tenant-status.guard.ts` (lines 95–100) returns `true` on DB exceptions. A DB error on a cache miss causes the request to pass through without suspension enforcement. Compounded by RF-021.
- **Expected:** When the DB is unreachable and no cached tenant status exists, the guard should return `503 Service Unavailable` rather than allowing the request through, so suspended tenants cannot use a DB outage as a bypass mechanism.
- **Actual:** On DB errors during tenant status lookup, the guard returns `true` (access granted). A suspended tenant can bypass enforcement any time the DB is slow or erroring, especially when combined with the Redis cache being down (RF-021).
- **Repro:**
  1. Suspend a tenant in the database.
  2. Simulate a DB error (e.g., block the DB connection) so the cache miss falls through to the DB lookup path.
  3. Attempt to call any protected API endpoint with that tenant's credentials.
  4. Observe HTTP 200 — the request is not blocked despite the tenant being suspended.
- **Fix:** Fail closed: if no cache entry exists and the DB is unreachable, return 503. If a cache entry exists, use it (even if stale) and log the DB error.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for `TenantStatusGuard` that mocks a DB error on cache miss and asserts the response is 503, not 200. Establish a code review rule: "All security guards must fail closed — a DB/cache error must never result in access being granted."

---

### RF-023 — DRIVER role can enumerate all credit notes in the tenant

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W1-001, revised P1→P2 by L1-A; **browser-confirmed by W18-004**
- **Issue:** `GET /credit-notes` is guarded by `JwtAuthGuard` only. `creditNotesService.findAllForUser()` scopes results only for `role === CUSTOMER`. A `DRIVER`-role JWT receives the full unfiltered tenant credit-note list (amounts, customer IDs, credit-note numbers).
- **Expected:** A driver-role JWT should receive `403 Forbidden` or an empty result when calling `GET /credit-notes`, as drivers have no business need to access financial credit note data.
- **Actual:** `GET /credit-notes` with a driver JWT returns HTTP 200 with the full tenant credit note list including amounts and customer identifiers.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a driver and obtain a driver JWT.
  2. Call `GET /credit-notes` with that JWT.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 and all tenant credit notes are returned.
- **Fix:** Add `DRIVER` branch to `findAllForUser()` returning empty results, or add `@Roles(OPERATOR, CUSTOMER)` to the endpoint.
- **Prevention:** Add a role-based access control (RBAC) matrix test suite that iterates over all endpoints and all roles, asserting that driver tokens cannot access financial endpoints. Include credit notes in the matrix.

### RF-024 — JWT claims not validated against DB in OAuth callback before redirect URL construction

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W1-002, confirmed P2 by L1-A
- **Issue:** `auth.controller.ts` (lines 288–305) legacy tenant Google callback constructs a redirect URL incorporating JWT-decoded claims without validating those claims against the actual user record from the database. A crafted token could inject values into the redirect.
- **Expected:** After decoding the JWT in the OAuth callback, all claims used to construct the redirect URL should be validated against the current database record to prevent injection from stale or crafted tokens.
- **Actual:** The redirect URL is built directly from JWT-decoded claims without any DB lookup or claim validation. If a token contains crafted values, those values flow into the redirect URL.
- **Repro:**
  1. Inspect the OAuth callback handler in `auth.controller.ts` at lines 288–305.
  2. Confirm there is no database fetch of the user record between JWT decode and redirect URL construction.
- **Fix:** After decoding, fetch the user from DB by `sub` claim, assert all relevant fields match the DB record, then construct the redirect URL from DB-sourced values only.
- **Prevention:** Establish a code convention: redirect URLs and sensitive responses must always be constructed from DB-fetched values, never from raw JWT claims. Add this as a security PR checklist item.

### RF-025 — Cron day-of-week logic uses server UTC; wrong firing day for non-UTC tenants

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** L1B-001, M-W3-C
- **Issue:** `order-templates.service.ts` (`@Cron("0 6 * * *")`) and `recurring-invoices.service.ts` (`@Cron(EVERY_DAY_AT_MIDNIGHT)`) both use `new Date()` (server UTC) for day-of-week resolution. For a UTC+12 tenant, 06:00 UTC is 18:00 the previous calendar day. Standing orders set for Monday fire on Sunday; `calcNextRunAt()` accumulates off-by-one-day errors for non-UTC tenants.
- **Expected:** Day-of-week resolution for standing orders and recurring invoices should use the tenant's configured local timezone, so a Monday order fires on Monday in the tenant's local time regardless of server UTC.
- **Actual:** All day-of-week calculations use server UTC. Tenants with UTC+ offsets see their standing orders and recurring invoices fire on the wrong calendar day.
- **Repro:**
  1. Configure a tenant with timezone UTC+12.
  2. Create a standing order template scheduled to fire on Monday.
  3. Observe the order is generated on Sunday (server UTC time).
- **Fix:** Store a timezone per tenant in `SystemConfig`. Resolve `today` using tenant timezone (via `date-fns-tz` or `luxon`) in both cron handlers and in `calcNextRunAt()`.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for `generateDailyOrders()` with a mocked UTC+12 tenant timezone, asserting the correct day-of-week is resolved. Add a `timezone` field to the `SystemConfig` model and enforce it is set before a tenant goes live.

### RF-026 — Invoice number race: standalone create path returns 500 under concurrency

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W10-002, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** `invoices.service.ts` standalone `createInvoice` path (line 350) calls `generateInvoiceNumber()` with no transaction client (`db = this.prisma`). Under concurrent invoice creation, two calls read the same last invoice number and both attempt to insert. `@@unique([tenantId, invoiceNumber])` on the schema (line 1208) prevents silent duplication but the second concurrent call crashes with Prisma unique constraint error → HTTP 500.
- **Expected:** Concurrent invoice creation requests should both succeed with unique invoice numbers. If a collision occurs at the DB level, the server should retry with the next available number rather than returning a 500.
- **Actual:** The second of two simultaneous `POST /invoices` requests crashes with a Prisma unique constraint error, resulting in HTTP 500 returned to the client.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send two simultaneous `POST /invoices` requests with valid payloads.
  2. One returns HTTP 201 with a new invoice; the other returns HTTP 500.
- **Fix:** Pass a transaction client into `generateInvoiceNumber()` from the standalone path (mirror the `createInvoiceFromOrder` pattern) and add retry-on-constraint-violation logic.
- **Prevention:** Add a concurrency integration test that fires 5 simultaneous `POST /invoices` requests and asserts all return 201 with unique invoice numbers. Centralize `generateInvoiceNumber()` to a single implementation that always requires a transaction client.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

### RF-027 — Credit note number race; concurrent credit notes can exceed invoice total (TOCTOU)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W10-003, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** `credit-notes.service.ts::nextCnNumber()` (lines 19–27) runs outside any transaction. The balance check (lines 52–55) and `creditNote.create` (lines 65–75) are also unprotected. Two concurrent credit notes for the same invoice can both pass the balance check before either commits, collectively exceeding the invoice total. Additionally, the CN number race surfaces as HTTP 500 (unique constraint `@@unique([tenantId, creditNoteNumber])` is present but no retry exists).
- **Expected:** Concurrent credit note creation for the same invoice should be serialized so the combined credit amount never exceeds the remaining invoice balance. If a credit note number collision occurs, the server should retry automatically rather than returning 500.
- **Actual:** Two concurrent credit note requests for the same invoice both pass the balance check before either commits, allowing total credits to exceed the invoice amount. Additionally, CN number collisions produce HTTP 500 with no retry.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice with a $100 remaining balance.
  2. Simultaneously fire two `POST /credit-notes` requests against that invoice, each for $80.
  3. Both may return 201 — combined credits ($160) exceed the invoice total ($100).
- **Fix:** Wrap the balance check and `creditNote.create` in a single `prisma.tenantTransaction()`. Implement retry-on-conflict for the CN number generation within the transaction.
- **Prevention:** Add a concurrency test that fires two simultaneous credit note requests summing to more than the invoice balance and asserts only one succeeds. Use `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the invoice row within the transaction to prevent TOCTOU.

### RF-028 — `voidInvoice()` not wrapped in transaction: race allows voiding partially-paid invoice

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W10-005, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** `invoices.service.ts::voidInvoice()` (lines 795–806) calls `findOneOrThrow` then `invoice.update` without a wrapping transaction. Between the status read and the void write, a concurrent payment can transition the invoice from SENT → PARTIAL. The update succeeds and voids a partially-paid invoice, leaving orphaned payment records against a VOID invoice.
- **Expected:** `voidInvoice()` should be wrapped in a transaction that re-reads the invoice status atomically with the void update, preventing a concurrent payment from creating an inconsistent state.
- **Actual:** A concurrent `recordPayment()` call between the `findOneOrThrow` and `invoice.update` calls in `voidInvoice()` can transition the invoice to PARTIAL before the void is written. The void still succeeds, leaving payment records orphaned against a VOID invoice.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send concurrent `POST /invoices/:id/void` and `POST /invoices/:id/payments` requests for the same SENT invoice.
  2. Observe the invoice is VOID while a payment record exists against it — orphaned payment.
- **Fix:** Wrap `voidInvoice()` in a `tenantTransaction()` mirroring the pattern used in `recordPayment()` (line 1019).
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test simulating the concurrent void + payment race and asserting that either the void or the payment fails (not both succeed leaving inconsistent state). Enforce a code review rule: all invoice status transitions must occur inside a transaction.

---

### RF-029 — `completeStop` (routes.service.ts) pre-transaction status check: duplicate DeliveryMutation rows

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W10-004, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** `routes.service.ts::completeStop()` reads stop status BEFORE the transaction (lines 975–980) and uses the pre-read `stop.orders` inside the transaction without re-reading. Two concurrent operator requests both pass the pre-transaction status check, both enter the transaction, and both create `DeliveryMutation` rows. (Contrast: `orders.service.ts::completeStop()` correctly re-reads the stop inside the transaction at line 1069.)
- **Expected:** Concurrent stop-completion requests for the same stop should be idempotent — only one `DeliveryMutation` row should be created regardless of how many concurrent requests arrive.
- **Actual:** Two concurrent requests both pass the pre-transaction status check and both create `DeliveryMutation` rows inside the transaction, resulting in duplicate accounting records.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send two simultaneous `PATCH /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete` requests via the operator routes path.
  2. Both return HTTP 200.
  3. Inspect the `DeliveryMutation` table — two rows exist for the same stop.
- **Fix:** Move the stop read and status check inside `tenantTransaction`. Mirror the `orders.service.ts` approach.
- **Prevention:** Add a unique constraint on `DeliveryMutation(stopId)` to prevent duplicate rows at the DB level. Add a concurrency test asserting that two simultaneous complete-stop calls produce exactly one DeliveryMutation row.

### RF-030 — Stop completion does not advance linked order status (`orderId: null` on run stops)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod / real-time
- **Surface:** API / mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W9-005
- **Issue:** Stop `d7523aeb` on run `140784f6` has `orderId: null`. The stop-completion handler in `routes.service.ts` cannot look up the linked order without `orderId`. Completing the stop leaves order status at `OUT_FOR_DELIVERY` rather than advancing to `DELIVERED`. Buyer and operator see stale status.
- **Expected:** Completing a delivery stop should automatically advance all linked orders to `DELIVERED` status, so buyers and operators see the updated status without manual intervention.
- **Actual:** Because `RouteRunStop.orderId` is `null`, the stop-completion handler cannot find the linked order. The order remains stuck at `OUT_FOR_DELIVERY` indefinitely after the stop is completed.
- **Repro:**
  1. Dispatch a run with stops linked to orders.
  2. Have the driver complete a stop.
  3. Check `GET /orders/:orderId` — status is still `OUT_FOR_DELIVERY`.
  4. Inspect the `RouteRunStop` record — confirm `orderId: null`.
- **Fix:** Ensure `RouteRunStop.orderId` is populated when stops are created from route stops that have associated orders. In the stop-completion handler, when `stop.orderId` is set and all stops for that order's customer are completed, advance the order to DELIVERED.
- **Prevention:** Add a NOT NULL constraint or application-level guard on `RouteRunStop.orderId` when created from an order-linked route. Add an integration test that creates a run from an order, completes the stop, and asserts the order status transitions to DELIVERED.

### RF-031 — `dispatch.tsx` loads 50 routes + all drivers with no pagination signal or skeleton

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** NEW-W7-A, confirmed by L1-C
- **Issue:** `dispatch.tsx` (lines 36–37) calls `useAdminRoutes({ limit: 50 })` and `useAdminDrivers()` (no limit) simultaneously on mount. No loading skeleton is shown. For large tenants this is O(N) upfront hydration contributing to the tab-freeze in RF-007.
- **Expected:** The dispatch screen should display a skeleton loader immediately on mount and progressively populate content as data loads, without blocking the UI thread.
- **Actual:** The dispatch screen fires two unbounded data fetches simultaneously on mount with no skeleton state, causing a visible freeze on tenants with many routes and drivers.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator on a tenant with 50+ routes and many drivers.
  2. Navigate to the dispatch tab.
  3. Observe a freeze or long blank render period with no loading indicator.
- **Fix:** Use paginated loading with a skeleton state; defer the driver list fetch until the route list has settled, or use virtualization.
- **Prevention:** Establish a UI pattern standard requiring all list screens to use skeleton loading states. Add a Lighthouse/performance CI check that flags any screen with Time-to-Interactive > 3s.

### RF-032 — Home screen "Routes today" section fetches all routes with no date filter

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-006a (W7/audit), F-005 merged, confirmed by L1-C
- **Issue:** `home.tsx` (line 59) calls `useAdminRoutes({ limit: 10 })` with no date parameter. The section is labeled "Routes today" but displays routes ordered by creation date regardless of date. The subtitle count (from `useOperatorRouteRuns({ date: today })`) is correct; the section label and card list contradict it.
- **Expected:** The "Routes today" section should display only routes scheduled for today, consistent with the subtitle count shown beneath it.
- **Actual:** The section shows the 10 most recently created routes regardless of date, which may include routes from prior days. The label "Routes today" is misleading.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator on a tenant with routes from multiple dates.
  2. View the home screen "Routes today" section.
  3. Observe routes from dates other than today appear in the list.
- **Fix:** Either filter the routes query by today's scheduled date, or rename the section to "Recent routes."
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the home screen data layer asserting that the routes query includes a `date: today` filter. Add a UI snapshot test that confirms the section label matches the filter applied.

### RF-033 — `/warehouse` deep link redirects to `/home`; direct URL navigation broken

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-010 (W7/audit), root cause refined by L1-C
- **Issue:** Navigating directly to `https://routeflowmobile-production.up.railway.app/warehouse` while authenticated redirects to `/home`. Other routes (`/dispatch`, `/orders`) navigate correctly. Most likely the route is not emitted in the Expo Router static export manifest or the Railway serve config does not forward all paths to the app shell.
- **Expected:** Authenticated users navigating directly to `/warehouse` via the browser address bar should land on the warehouse screen without a redirect.
- **Actual:** Navigating to `/warehouse` directly redirects to `/home`. Bookmarks and shared links to the warehouse screen are broken.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator.
  2. Paste `https://routeflowmobile-production.up.railway.app/warehouse` into the browser address bar.
  3. Observe redirection to `/home` instead of rendering the warehouse screen.
- **Fix:** Verify the Expo Router static export manifest includes `warehouse`; if not, ensure the route file is not accidentally excluded. Add a Railway `_redirects` rule or `--single-page-app` flag so all unknown paths serve the app shell.
- **Prevention:** Add a CI smoke test that navigates to each operator route via direct URL and asserts the correct screen renders (not a redirect). Standardize the Railway deploy config to always use SPA mode for all Expo web exports.

### RF-034 — Mobile-as-web renders as narrow phone-width column on desktop viewports

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-002 (W7/audit), confirmed P2 by L1-C
- **Issue:** On desktop viewports ≥1024px wide, the app renders as a narrow centered column (~800px). KPI cards, route lists, and order tables are phone-width on a 1440px monitor. No explicit "use mobile app" message is shown to desktop users.
- **Expected:** On desktop viewports, the operator app should either render in a responsive multi-column layout utilizing the available screen width, or display a clear banner directing users to the full desktop dashboard.
- **Actual:** The app renders as a narrow ~800px centered column on a 1440px monitor. All content is phone-width with large empty margins on both sides, significantly degrading usability for desktop operators.
- **Repro:**
  1. Open the production URL in a browser on a monitor with a viewport width of 1440px.
  2. Observe the app renders in a narrow centered column with wide empty gutters on both sides.
- **Fix:** Either implement responsive breakpoints in the Expo web build, or add a banner directing desktop users to the Next.js operator dashboard when it is deployed.
- **Prevention:** Add a viewport-responsive test using Playwright that captures screenshots at 1440px and 375px and flags regressions in layout. Set a design system breakpoint standard for desktop minimum at 1024px.

### RF-035 — Inconsistent tax base: line discounts reduce tax, order/invoice-level discounts do not

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-001, W3-007, confirmed by L1-B (merged — same root cause)
- **Issue:** Invoice tax is computed per-line (after line-level discount) then invoice-level discount is subtracted from the post-tax total. Order tax is applied to the full pre-discount subtotal (`orders.service.ts` lines 605–607). The two surfaces are inconsistent and potentially non-compliant with tax jurisdictions that require discount to reduce the tax base.
- **Expected:** Tax should be computed consistently across invoices and orders using a single documented policy — either all discounts (line-level and order-level) reduce the tax base, or none do.
- **Actual:** Tax is computed differently for invoices (line-level discounts reduce the tax base) and orders (order-level discounts do not reduce the tax base). The same transaction produces different tax amounts depending on which code path created it.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an order with a $100 subtotal, a 10% line-level discount, and a $10 order-level discount.
  2. Create an invoice for the same amounts.
  3. Compare the tax amounts — they differ due to the inconsistent tax base calculation.
- **Fix:** Establish a single written tax policy. Apply it uniformly across invoices and orders in both `invoices.service.ts` and `orders.service.ts`.
- **Prevention:** Extract a shared `calculateTax(subtotal, discount, taxRate)` utility function and use it in both services. Add a unit test asserting that the same input produces the same tax output regardless of whether the invoice or order code path is used.

### RF-036 — Invoice update stores stale total when only discount/shippingFee changes

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-004, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `PATCH /invoices/:id` with only `discount` or `shippingFee` (no `items`) persists new values without recomputing `total` or `taxAmount`. Stored total is stale. Currently guarded by DRAFT-only check but stale DRAFT data propagates to SENT state.
- **Expected:** Any change to `discount` or `shippingFee` on an invoice should trigger immediate recalculation of `total` and `taxAmount`, keeping stored values consistent at all times.
- **Actual:** The `discount` and `shippingFee` are updated in the database, but `total` and `taxAmount` remain at their stale previous values. The incorrect totals are then sent to customers when the invoice is published.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a DRAFT invoice with `total: $100`.
  2. Call `PATCH /invoices/:id` with `{ "discount": 50 }` and no `items`.
  3. Fetch the invoice — `total` is still `$100` rather than `$50`.
- **Fix:** Same as RF-012 fix — call `recalculateTotals()` helper from both update branches.
- **Prevention:** Enforce a single `recalculateTotals()` function as the only path to persist invoice totals. Add a test asserting both update branches call this function.

---

### RF-037 — Float arithmetic for tax/total: sub-cent drift stored in DB

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-002, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `orders.service.ts` (lines 605–607): `subtotal` and `getTaxRate()` return plain JS floats. Multiplication without `Math.round(result * 100) / 100` means `taxAmount` and `total` carry sub-cent trailing digits, causing order totals to diverge from invoice totals by a few cents.
- **Expected:** All stored monetary values should be rounded to 2 decimal places before persistence, so order totals and invoice totals for the same transaction always match exactly.
- **Actual:** Tax and total amounts are stored with sub-cent floating-point precision (e.g., `$10.000000000001`). Order totals and invoice totals for the same items can differ by a few cents due to accumulated rounding differences.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an order with a product priced at $9.99 and a tax rate of 7% (yields repeating decimal: $0.6993...).
  2. Inspect the stored `taxAmount` — it contains sub-cent trailing digits.
  3. Compare the stored `total` to the corresponding invoice total — they differ by a few cents.
- **Fix:** Apply `Math.round(x * 100) / 100` before persisting every monetary value in `orders.service.ts`. Audit `invoices.service.ts` for the same pattern.
- **Prevention:** Adopt a shared `Money.round(amount)` utility and enforce via ESLint that raw floating-point arithmetic results are never passed directly to Prisma `create`/`update` calls for monetary fields.

---

### RF-038 — Overpayment tolerance of $0.001 allows negative AR balance

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-009, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `invoices.service.ts` (lines 1038–1041) accepts payments within $0.001 of the remaining balance. A payment of `remaining + $0.0001` is accepted, creating an `alreadyPaid` value that exceeds `invoice.total` and results in negative AR.
- **Expected:** Payments exceeding the remaining invoice balance should be rejected with a `400 Bad Request`, or the excess should be automatically routed to an `AdvancePayment` record — never resulting in negative AR.
- **Actual:** A payment of `(remainingBalance + $0.0001)` is accepted with HTTP 200. The `alreadyPaid` value exceeds the invoice total, producing a negative AR balance that corrupts aging reports.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice for $100 with no payments made.
  2. Record a payment of `$100.0001`.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 — the invoice is marked PAID with `alreadyPaid > invoice.total`.
  4. Check AR aging — the invoice shows a negative outstanding balance.
- **Fix:** Use zero tolerance (reject if `payment > remaining`), or route any overpayment to an `AdvancePayment` record.
- **Prevention:** Add a DB check constraint `CHECK (alreadyPaid <= total)` on the Invoice model. Add a unit test asserting that a payment 1 cent over the balance is rejected.

---

### RF-039 — Order consolidation propagates stale subtotals without revalidation

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-011, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `orders.service.ts` (lines 248–257) — when merging orders, the source order's existing `subtotal` is carried forward without recalculation. If the source order has a unit-price/qty mismatch, the merged order inherits the incorrect subtotal.
- **Expected:** Order consolidation/merge should recalculate all line subtotals from `unitPrice × qty` to ensure the merged order always has a correct, consistent total.
- **Actual:** The source order's stale `subtotal` is copied verbatim into the merged order. Any data inconsistency in the source order's subtotal (e.g., from the RF-037 float drift) is propagated and compounded in the merged order.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an order and manually set an incorrect `subtotal` on a line item.
  2. Merge it with another order.
  3. Verify the merged order total is incorrect, inheriting the corrupted subtotal.
- **Fix:** Recalculate all line subtotals from `unitPrice × qty` immediately before merging.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the merge operation that introduces a subtotal mismatch in the source order and asserts the merged total is correctly recalculated. Add the same recalculation call whenever orders transition status.

### RF-040 — Standing orders use live catalog prices with no buyer notification

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API / mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W3-012, M-W3-B, L1-B
- **Issue:** `order-templates/order-templates.service.ts` (line 282) fetches live product price at generation time. If operator raises prices, the next auto-generated standing order silently uses the higher price. Buyers have no visibility. Behavior inconsistent with recurring invoices (which freeze prices at template creation).
- **Expected:** If prices change between standing order template creation and generation, the buyer should be notified of the price change before or when the order is auto-generated, so they can review and cancel if needed.
- **Actual:** The standing order is auto-generated at the new higher price with no buyer notification. The buyer is silently charged more than they agreed to in their template without any opportunity to cancel.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a standing order template for a product priced at $10.
  2. Operator raises the product price to $15.
  3. Wait for the scheduled standing order to fire.
  4. Observe the auto-generated order uses the $15 price with no buyer notification.
- **Fix:** Either freeze the price in the template at creation time, or send a buyer notification when generated-order prices differ from the last auto-order.
- **Prevention:** Add a price-change detection step in `generateDailyOrders()` that compares live prices to the template's recorded prices and sends a notification when they differ. Document the chosen price policy (live vs. frozen) clearly in the buyer-facing UI.

### RF-041 — Credit note sub-penny tolerance leaves $0.001 permanently unresolved

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-013, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `credit-notes.service.ts` (line 240) marks a credit note APPLIED when `amountUsed >= amount − $0.001`. Up to $0.001 of credit permanently remains unresolved with no cleanup mechanism. Residuals accumulate over time.
- **Expected:** A credit note should be marked APPLIED only when the full amount has been used. No residual balance should persist indefinitely.
- **Actual:** The tolerance allows up to $0.001 of credit to remain unapplied. Over time, many credit notes accumulate sub-penny residuals that are never resolved, distorting financial reports.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create and apply a credit note in an amount that results in `amountUsed = creditNote.amount - 0.0005` (within tolerance).
  2. The credit note is marked APPLIED, but `creditNote.amount - amountUsed = $0.0005` remains permanently unresolved.
- **Fix:** Set minimum credit note amount of $0.01 on creation; use exact-value comparison on the APPLIED threshold.
- **Prevention:** Add a `@Min(0.01)` validator on credit note amount. Add a periodic reconciliation job that reports any credit note with a residual balance older than 30 days. Add a unit test asserting the tolerance does not cause residual balances.

### RF-042 — Advance payment not auto-applied to future invoices; not visible to buyer

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / buyer-portal
- **Surface:** API / mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W3-017, L1-B
- **Issue:** `invoices.service.ts` (lines 1330–1342) — excess payment becomes an `AdvancePayment` record. This record is not auto-applied to subsequent invoices and is not surfaced on the buyer's statement screen (`/buyer/statement`). Buyers who overpay have no self-service path to understand where their money went.
- **Expected:** Overpayments should be automatically applied to the buyer's next invoice, or at minimum be clearly visible as an available credit balance on the buyer's statement screen.
- **Actual:** Overpayment creates an `AdvancePayment` record that sits idle — not applied to future invoices and invisible to the buyer. The buyer has no way to know they have a credit balance or how to use it.
- **Repro:**
  1. Overpay an invoice by $20.
  2. Navigate to the buyer statement screen (`/buyer/statement`).
  3. Observe the $20 advance payment balance is not shown.
- **Fix:** Implement auto-application logic when a new invoice reaches SENT status, or at minimum surface the balance on the buyer statement.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test that overpays an invoice and then creates a new invoice, asserting the advance payment is automatically applied. Add a buyer statement UI test asserting the advance payment balance is displayed.

### RF-043 — Invoice detail screen hardcodes "GST (10%)" label regardless of tenant tax rate

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W5-007, confirmed P2 by L1-B
- **Issue:** `apps/mobile/app/(customer)/invoices/[id].tsx` (line 73) renders `<Text>GST (10%): ...</Text>` as a string literal. The tax amount from the API is correct, but the label always shows 10% regardless of the configured rate (0%, 7%, 15%, etc.).
- **Expected:** The tax label on the invoice detail screen should dynamically reflect the actual tax rate applied to the invoice (e.g., "GST (15%)" for a 15% rate, or "VAT (7%)").
- **Actual:** The label always reads "GST (10%)" regardless of the configured tax rate. A buyer on a 15% tax tenant sees a misleading label while the underlying amount is correct.
- **Repro:**
  1. Set the tenant tax rate to 15%.
  2. Create an invoice and view it in the buyer portal mobile app.
  3. Observe the label reads "GST (10%)" while the tax amount reflects 15%.
- **Fix:** Compute `rate = Math.round((invoice.tax / invoice.subtotal) * 100)` client-side and interpolate into the label, or add a `taxRatePercent` field to the invoice API response.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI test that creates an invoice under different tax rate configurations and asserts the label reflects the correct rate. Ban hardcoded percentage strings in financial UI components via ESLint rule or code review checklist.
