### RF-096 — DRIVER role can read all orders in the tenant without route-assignment filter

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security / PII
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W11-006
- **Issue:** `GET /orders/:id` allows DRIVER role with only tenant isolation — no check that the order belongs to a stop on a run assigned to this driver. Any driver can read all tenant orders.
- **Expected:** DRIVER-role requests to `GET /orders/:id` should only succeed if the order is on a stop in a run currently assigned to the requesting driver.
- **Actual:** Any driver can read any order in the tenant by ID, exposing customer names, addresses, and order details for routes they are not assigned to.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a driver.
  2. Obtain an order ID that is not on any run assigned to this driver.
  3. Call `GET /orders/{orderId}` with the driver JWT.
  4. Observe HTTP 200 with the full order data.
- **Fix:** For DRIVER role, add a check that a `RouteRunStop` for this order exists on an active run assigned to the requesting driver.
- **Prevention:** Add a DRIVER-role integration test for the orders endpoint asserting that access to an unassigned order returns 403. Add route-assignment checks as a required pattern for all DRIVER-accessible order and customer endpoints.

### RF-097 — `GET /public/places/config` leaks Google Maps API key unauthenticated

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W11-008
- **Issue:** `public-places.controller.ts:34-39` is a no-auth endpoint that returns `{ apiKey: process.env.GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY }`. Any script can harvest the key and abuse it at tenant's billing expense.
- **Expected:** The Google Maps API key should never be returned to unauthenticated callers. At minimum, authentication should be required before the key is revealed.
- **Actual:** Any unauthenticated HTTP client can retrieve the raw Google Maps API key from this endpoint, enabling key abuse at the tenant's billing expense.
- **Repro:**
  1. In an unauthenticated browser or curl session, call `GET /api/v1/public/places/config`.
  2. Observe the response contains `{ "apiKey": "<google-maps-key>" }`.
- **Fix:** Require `JwtAuthGuard` on this endpoint, or return a short-lived session signature rather than the raw key.
- **Prevention:** Add a security CI test that calls all `/public/*` endpoints without authentication and asserts none of them return API keys or secrets. Use Google's API key restrictions (HTTP referrer restriction) to limit key abuse even if leaked.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

### RF-098 — Tax computed on pre-discount subtotal — over-collects tax on discounted invoices

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W12-A5
- **Issue:** Invoice tax computation applies rate to pre-discount subtotal. Correct: `tax = (subtotal - discount) * taxRate`. Example: $1000, 10% discount, 8% tax → over-collects $8.
- **Expected:** Tax should be computed on the post-discount subtotal: `tax = (subtotal - discount) * taxRate`, so buyers are only taxed on the amount they actually pay.
- **Actual:** Tax is computed on the full pre-discount subtotal. A $1,000 invoice with a 10% discount and 8% tax should charge $72 tax but currently charges $80 — over-collecting $8.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice with subtotal $1,000, discount $100, and tax rate 8%.
  2. Inspect the computed `taxAmount` — it reflects 8% of $1,000 ($80) rather than 8% of $900 ($72).
- **Fix:** Apply discounts to subtotal before computing tax.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the invoice tax calculation with a discount, asserting `taxAmount = (subtotal - discount) * taxRate`. Establish a documented tax calculation policy and enforce it via the shared `calculateTax()` utility (see RF-035).

---

### RF-099 — VOID payments counted as paid in AR aging; credit notes not netted

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W12-A6
- **Issue:** AR aging sums all payments without filtering `status != VOID`. Applied credit notes not subtracted from outstanding balance. Finance team makes collection decisions on incorrect data.
- **Expected:** AR aging should only count `CONFIRMED` payments and should subtract applied credit note amounts from the outstanding balance to show the true amount owed.
- **Actual:** VOID payments inflate the "amount paid" total, and unapplied credit notes are not netted from outstanding balances. Finance teams see incorrect AR data and may pursue collection on already-settled balances or miss genuinely overdue amounts.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice for $500 with one CONFIRMED payment of $200 and one VOID payment of $100.
  2. Query the AR aging report — the outstanding balance reflects only $300 ($500 - $200) but should be $300 (VOID payment should not reduce it).
  3. Apply a credit note of $50 — the AR aging does not subtract it.
- **Fix:** Filter payments to `status = CONFIRMED` only. Subtract applied credit note amounts from invoice outstanding balance in aging query.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the AR aging query asserting VOID payments are excluded and applied credit notes reduce the outstanding balance. Add a data integrity check job that compares AR aging totals against the payment ledger to detect discrepancies.

---

### RF-100 — `StockMovement.performedById` FK not nullified before user delete — latent 500

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** data integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W12-B5
- **Issue:** `deleteCustomer()` deletes the associated User record without first nullifying `StockMovement.performedById`. May cause FK violation 500 or silent cascade deletion of inventory history.
- **Expected:** Before deleting a user record, all FK references to that user (including `StockMovement.performedById`) should be nullified to preserve historical records while allowing the user deletion to proceed.
- **Actual:** The user is deleted without nullifying the FK. This either causes a 500 FK constraint violation or silently cascades to delete inventory history — depending on the Prisma schema's `onDelete` behavior.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create stock movements associated with a specific user.
  2. Delete the customer (and associated user) via `DELETE /customers/:id`.
  3. Observe either a 500 FK violation or the loss of stock movement history.
- **Fix:** `tx.stockMovement.updateMany({ where: { performedById: userId }, data: { performedById: null } })` before deleting user.
- **Prevention:** Create a pre-delete cleanup checklist for user deletion that enumerates all FK references to the User model and nullifies them. Add an integration test for customer deletion asserting StockMovement records persist with `performedById: null`.

### RF-105 — Messages controller has no ownership check — any user can read and post to any thread

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W13-008, W13-009
- **Issue:** Messages endpoints have `JwtAuthGuard` only. No `RolesGuard`, no participant ownership check. Any authenticated user (driver, customer, buyer) can read and post to any message thread.
- **Expected:** Message thread access should be restricted to participants (sender, recipient) of that thread, or at minimum scoped by role (e.g., operators see all tenant threads, buyers see only their own).
- **Actual:** Any authenticated user can read all message threads in the tenant and post to any thread regardless of whether they are a participant.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a driver.
  2. Obtain a message thread ID belonging to a buyer-operator conversation.
  3. Call `GET /messages/{threadId}` and `POST /messages/{threadId}` with the driver JWT — both succeed.
- **Fix:** Add participant check: `WHERE id = :id AND (senderId = :userId OR recipientId = :userId OR tenantId = :tenantId)`.
- **Prevention:** Add a participant-ownership test for the messages module asserting non-participant users receive 403. Add message thread participant checks as a mandatory pattern for all messaging endpoint PR reviews.

---

### RF-106 — File extension derived from user-controlled `originalname` → extension spoofing

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** upload-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W14-003
- **Issue:** `customers.service.ts:1864` and `products.service.ts:111` derive stored file extension from user-supplied `file.originalname`. Attacker uploads a file with `originalname="payload.svg"` but `Content-Type: image/jpeg`. MIME check passes; stored key gets `.svg` extension; `mime.lookup` on serving returns `image/svg+xml` → inline SVG execution.
- **Expected:** The stored file extension should always be derived from the server-validated MIME type, not from the user-supplied filename, so a renamed SVG cannot be stored as an image.
- **Actual:** The file is stored with the extension from `originalname`. An attacker who names their malicious SVG `payload.jpg` gets it past the MIME check, but names it `payload.svg` when the extension is later used for serving — resulting in inline SVG execution.
- **Repro:**
  1. Upload a file with `originalname="payload.svg"` and `Content-Type: image/jpeg`.
  2. The MIME check passes (accepts `image/jpeg`).
  3. The stored key uses `.svg` extension from `originalname`.
  4. Fetching the file returns `Content-Type: image/svg+xml` — browser executes embedded scripts.
- **Fix:** Derive extension from the validated MIME type (`mime.extension(file.mimetype)`), not from user-supplied filename.
- **Prevention:** Add a security test uploading a file with mismatched `originalname` extension and `Content-Type`, asserting the stored extension reflects the MIME type. Add an ESLint rule banning `file.originalname` usage in storage key generation.

---

### RF-107 — Storage keys not prefixed with `tenantId` — cross-tenant file access possible

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** upload-security / multi-tenancy
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W14-010
- **Issue:** Storage keys are `products/{productId}/{uuid}.ext` — no tenant prefix. In shared local storage or R2 bucket, if an attacker obtains another tenant's product UUID via an IDOR, they can access files for that product.
- **Expected:** All storage keys should include the `tenantId` as a prefix so files from different tenants are namespaced separately and cross-tenant access is structurally impossible.
- **Actual:** Storage keys use only resource-level UUIDs with no tenant prefix. An attacker who obtains a UUID from another tenant (via IDOR or guessing) can construct the key and access that tenant's files.
- **Repro:**
  1. As Tenant A operator, obtain a product UUID from Tenant B (e.g., via an IDOR in the products endpoint).
  2. Construct the storage key: `products/{tenantB-productId}/{uuid}.jpg`.
  3. Call `GET /uploads/products/{tenantB-productId}/{uuid}.jpg` — file served without tenant validation.
- **Fix:** Prefix all keys with `tenants/{tenantId}/`.
- **Prevention:** Add a storage key generation utility that always includes `tenantId` as the first path segment and enforce its use via a lint rule. Add an integration test asserting that Tenant A's file URL cannot be fetched by Tenant B's authenticated user.

---

### RF-108 — `mimetype.startsWith("image/")` filter passes SVG on product and customer document uploads

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** upload-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W14-007
- **Issue:** `products.controller.ts:104` and `customers.controller.ts:385` use `file.mimetype.startsWith("image/")` as the upload filter. This passes `image/svg+xml`. Combined with RF-106, an attacker can upload an SVG as a product image.
- **Expected:** The MIME type filter should use an explicit allowlist of safe raster image types, rejecting `image/svg+xml` and any other renderable format.
- **Actual:** The `startsWith("image/")` check passes `image/svg+xml`. Combined with RF-106's extension spoofing, attackers can upload a malicious SVG as a product image, enabling stored XSS via the product catalog.
- **Repro:**
  1. Upload a file with `Content-Type: image/svg+xml` containing a `<script>` tag to `POST /products/:id/images`.
  2. Observe HTTP 201 — the SVG is accepted and stored.
  3. The SVG is served inline, executing the embedded script in any user's browser.
- **Fix:** Use an explicit allowlist: `["image/jpeg", "image/png", "image/webp", "image/gif"]`.
- **Prevention:** Centralize the MIME allowlist in a shared constant and require it to be explicitly specified for each upload endpoint. Add an upload security test asserting `image/svg+xml` is rejected on all product and customer upload endpoints.

### RF-109 — Driver can read all customer returns in the tenant (RBAC gap — browser confirmed)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W18-003 (browser confirmed), W11-002 (code)
- **Issue:** `GET /api/v1/returns` returns HTTP 200 with the full tenant returns list to DRIVER-role JWT. Browser-confirmed: `ux_driver_a` token retrieves identical result to TENANT_ADMIN.
- **Expected:** DRIVER-role users should receive `403 Forbidden` on `GET /returns`. Drivers have no business need to see customer return records.
- **Actual:** A driver JWT returns the full tenant return list with HTTP 200, identical to what a TENANT_ADMIN receives — confirmed via live browser testing.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a driver (`ux_driver_a`).
  2. Call `GET /api/v1/returns` with the driver JWT.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 with the full tenant return list.
- **Fix:** Add `@Roles(Role.TENANT_ADMIN, Role.OPERATOR)` to `GET /returns`. The CUSTOMER path is correctly handled by `findAllForUser()`.
- **Prevention:** Add the returns list endpoint to the RBAC matrix test suite. Add a post-merge CI test that calls all financial list endpoints with a driver JWT and asserts all return 403.

### RF-110 — Stored XSS in customer `businessName` and order `notes` — raw HTML stored by API

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security / XSS
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W18-005, W18-006
- **Issue:** API stores raw HTML/JavaScript in `businessName` and `notes` with no sanitization. Browser-confirmed: `<img src=x onerror=alert('XSS-W18')>` stored verbatim. React Native Web frontend escapes correctly. Risk exists for PDF generators, email templates, admin panels, and native WebViews.
- **Expected:** Free-text fields like `businessName` and `notes` should be sanitized server-side to strip HTML tags before storage, preventing stored XSS in any rendering context (PDFs, email templates, WebViews).
- **Actual:** Raw HTML including `<script>` and event-handler attributes is stored verbatim. While the current React Native Web frontend escapes it correctly, any PDF generator, email template, or native WebView that renders this data would execute the XSS payload.
- **Repro:**
  1. Call `POST /api/v1/customers` with `{"businessName": "<img src=x onerror=alert('XSS-W18')>"}`.
  2. Observe HTTP 201 — the payload is stored verbatim.
  3. Fetch the customer record — the raw HTML is returned in the response.
- **Fix:** Add server-side HTML sanitization (strip-tags or NestJS `@Transform`) on all free-text fields (`businessName`, `contactName`, `notes`, `driverNote`).
- **Prevention:** Add a `@Transform(stripHtml)` decorator to all free-text string fields in all DTOs. Add a CI test that submits HTML payloads to free-text fields and asserts they are stripped in the response. Include stored XSS testing in the standard security audit checklist.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

---

### RF-111 — POD store (photos/signature) not persisted — lost on app crash or OS background-kill

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod / offline
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W19-003
- **Issue:** `podStore.ts` uses bare `create<PodState>()` with no `persist` middleware. Captured photos and signatures are held in memory only. OS background-kill between capture and stop submission permanently loses all evidence.
- **Note:** This is confirmed separately from RF-046 (POD photos lost on app crash); both findings describe the same root cause from different investigation paths. Treat as confirmed P2.
- **Expected:** Captured POD photos and signatures should persist to device storage so they survive app crashes and can be re-submitted after restart.
- **Actual:** All POD data is in-memory only. An OS background-kill after photo capture but before submission permanently destroys the evidence with no recovery path.
- **Repro:**
  1. Capture a POD photo and signature for a stop.
  2. Force-quit the driver app before submitting.
  3. Reopen the app — the POD data is gone.
- **Fix:** Add Zustand `persist` middleware backed by `AsyncStorage` to `podStore`. Clear on successful stop submission.
- **Prevention:** See RF-046 — same fix. Enforce `persist` middleware as mandatory for all Zustand stores in the driver app that hold actionable data. Add a test that saves POD data, simulates restart, and asserts data is recovered.

### RF-112 — Tenant code enumeration possible via login endpoint error messages

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W20-002
- **Issue:** `POST /auth/login` with a valid tenant code + wrong password returns a different error than with an invalid tenant code. Attacker can enumerate valid tenant codes by observing error message differences.
- **Expected:** All login failure scenarios (wrong tenant code, wrong username, wrong password) should return the identical error body `{"message":"Invalid credentials"}` with the same HTTP status code, giving no information about which field was incorrect.
- **Actual:** A valid tenant code paired with a wrong password returns a different error message than an invalid tenant code, allowing an attacker to confirm which tenant codes exist by comparing error responses.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `POST /auth/login` with a nonexistent tenant code — note the error message.
  2. Send `POST /auth/login` with a valid tenant code and wrong password — note the error message.
  3. Observe the two responses differ, confirming which tenant code exists.
- **Fix:** Return identical error messages for all login failure cases (`Invalid credentials` regardless of whether the tenant code, username, or password is wrong).
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that the login error response body and HTTP status code are byte-for-byte identical for invalid-tenant-code and valid-tenant-code-wrong-password scenarios. Add constant-time comparison for all credential fields to prevent timing-based enumeration as well.

---

### RF-113 — Email verification tokens can be replayed indefinitely — no single-use enforcement

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W20-003
- **Issue:** Email verification tokens are JWT-based with no database tracking (no `usedAt` flag or token invalidation table). A token can be used multiple times after the initial verification and remains valid until JWT expiry.
- **Expected:** An email verification token should be valid for only a single use. After the first successful verification, any subsequent submission of the same token should return `400 Bad Request` with `{"code":"TOKEN_ALREADY_USED"}`.
- **Actual:** The same email verification token can be submitted repeatedly and succeeds every time until the JWT's natural expiry, allowing replay by anyone who intercepts the token from an email link.
- **Repro:**
  1. Trigger an email verification flow and capture the token from the link.
  2. Submit the token via `POST /auth/verify-email` — observe success.
  3. Submit the same token again — observe success again (replay successful).
- **Fix:** Track token usage in the database. On successful verification, mark token as used and reject reuse.
- **Prevention:** Store a `verificationToken` hash in the `User` table and clear it on first successful use. Add an integration test that verifies a token twice and asserts the second call returns 400. Prefer short-lived (15-minute) tokens over long-lived JWTs for email verification to limit replay windows.

---

### RF-114 — Refresh token issuance not atomic — suspended users can get new tokens in race window

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W20-004
- **Issue:** The refresh token endpoint reads user status, verifies it, then issues new tokens in non-atomic steps. A concurrent user suspension or deletion during this window allows the suspended user to successfully receive new access/refresh tokens.
- **Expected:** If an operator suspends a user account, that user's ability to obtain new tokens should be revoked immediately. Any in-flight refresh request that overlaps with the suspension should fail with `401 Unauthorized`.
- **Actual:** Between the status check and token issuance in the refresh flow, a concurrent suspension can complete without affecting the token issuance path, allowing a suspended user to receive a new valid access token.
- **Repro:**
  1. As suspended user, send `POST /auth/refresh` with a valid refresh token.
  2. Concurrently (within milliseconds), an operator suspends the same user account.
  3. Due to the non-atomic check, the token issuance completes successfully and the suspended user receives a new valid access token.
- **Fix:** Wrap the status check and token issuance in an atomic transaction or use optimistic concurrency.
- **Prevention:** Use a database transaction that reads user status with `SELECT FOR UPDATE` and issues tokens only within that lock. Add an integration test that simulates concurrent suspension and refresh, asserting that at most one new token is issued and the suspended-user path returns 401.

---

### RF-115 — Cron-based standing orders fire at wrong local time for non-UTC tenants (DST variant)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / scheduling
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W20-007 (DST variant of RF-025)
- **Issue:** The cron scheduler has no DST awareness. During daylight saving transitions (e.g., spring-forward at 2:00am), a standing order scheduled for 06:00 local time in an affected timezone may fire twice or be skipped entirely on the transition day.
- **Expected:** Standing orders should fire at the correct local clock time on every day, including DST transition days, with exactly one execution per scheduled day.
- **Actual:** On DST spring-forward days, a standing order scheduled for an hour that is skipped by the clock transition is silently missed. On fall-back days, the same order may fire twice. Neither anomaly is logged or reported to the operator.
- **Repro:**
  1. Configure a tenant timezone to one that observes DST (e.g., `America/New_York`).
  2. Create a standing order scheduled for 02:30 local time.
  3. On the spring-forward transition day (clocks jump from 02:00 to 03:00), the standing order is skipped entirely with no alert.
- **Fix:** Use a timezone-aware scheduler (e.g., `node-cron` with timezone parameter, or `@nestjs/schedule` with timezone support). Handle DST edge cases explicitly.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test that mocks the clock to a DST transition moment and asserts standing orders are executed exactly once. Encode scheduled times as timezone-aware instants (using `luxon` or `date-fns-tz`) rather than bare cron expressions.

---

### RF-116 — Server downtime causes standing orders to be silently skipped — no recovery mechanism

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / scheduling
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W20-008
- **Issue:** The standing-order cron fires at a single scheduled time. If the server is down at that moment, the orders are never generated. There is no "missed fire" recovery mechanism and no operator alert.
- **Expected:** If the server restarts after a scheduled standing-order execution window was missed, the system should detect the gap, generate the missed orders, and notify the operator that catch-up generation occurred.
- **Actual:** If the server is down when a standing order cron fires, the orders are silently skipped. There is no recovery mechanism, no missed-fire log entry, and no operator notification. The operator discovers missing orders only when customers complain.
- **Repro:**
  1. Schedule a standing order to fire at a specific time.
  2. Shut down the API server before that time and restart it after the scheduled window has passed.
  3. Observe that no order was generated — no log, no alert, no catch-up execution.
- **Fix:** Implement a "catch-up" check on startup: compare last-fire timestamps against expected schedule and generate missed orders for up to 24h in arrears.
- **Prevention:** Store the last successful cron execution timestamp per standing order in the database. On server startup, run a catch-up pass for any execution windows missed in the last 24h. Add an alert (email or webhook) when catch-up generates orders. Add a startup integration test that seeds a missed execution and asserts catch-up fires correctly.

---

### RF-117 — Standing order template edits do not cascade to already-scheduled orders

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API / web-operator / mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W20-009
- **Issue:** Orders generated from standing order templates capture a denormalized snapshot of line items and prices at creation time. Editing the template's line items does not update already-generated (but not yet confirmed) orders. Behavior is undocumented — operators and buyers have no UI indication of whether an order reflects the current template or a stale snapshot.
- **Expected:** When a standing order template is edited, pending (unconfirmed) orders generated from that template should either be updated to reflect the new line items, or clearly marked in the UI as reflecting a stale snapshot with an option to regenerate.
- **Actual:** Editing a template's line items or prices has no effect on already-generated pending orders. Operators and buyers see these orders with no indication that they reflect stale data, leading to unexpected order contents and prices at delivery time.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a standing order template with 5 units of Product A at $10.
  2. The cron fires — a pending order is created with 5× Product A at $10.
  3. Edit the template to change Product A's quantity to 3 units.
  4. Inspect the pending order — it still shows 5 units at the old price, with no stale indicator.
- **Fix:** Document and surface the snapshot behavior clearly in the UI. Add a "regenerate from current template" action on draft standing orders. Alternatively, defer line-item resolution to order confirmation time.
- **Prevention:** Display a "Based on template — last synced [date]" badge on pending standing orders. Add a UI action to regenerate line items from the current template. Add a unit test asserting the pending order is flagged as stale after template edit.

---

### RF-118 — Template deletion fails when pending orders exist (Prisma Restrict FK error)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W20-010
- **Issue:** Deleting a standing order template fails with a Prisma FK constraint error when generated-but-unconfirmed orders reference it. The error is not handled and surfaces as a 500.
- **Expected:** Attempting to delete a standing order template that has pending orders should return `409 Conflict` with a user-friendly message listing the blocking orders, allowing the operator to handle them before deletion.
- **Actual:** `DELETE /order-templates/:id` throws an unhandled Prisma FK constraint error and returns HTTP 500 when pending orders reference the template. The operator receives a generic server error with no actionable information.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a standing order template and let it generate at least one pending (unconfirmed) order.
  2. Attempt to delete the template via `DELETE /order-templates/:id`.
  3. Observe HTTP 500 — Prisma FK constraint violation surfaces unhandled.
- **Fix:** Before deleting template, either: (a) null the `templateId` FK on referencing orders, or (b) return a user-friendly error listing the pending orders that must be addressed first.
- **Prevention:** Add a pre-delete check that queries for pending orders referencing the template and returns 409 with order IDs if any exist. Add a unit test asserting the endpoint returns 409 (not 500) when blocking orders exist. Catch Prisma FK constraint errors globally and map them to 409 responses.

### RF-119 — Settings page is a single flat form — tabbed layout (Users, Branding, Notifications) absent

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux / settings
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W16-001
- **Issue:** Settings renders as one flat form: Contact Phone, Address, Tax Rate, Notifications toggle. No tabs for Users, Branding, Invoicing details.
- **Expected:** The Settings screen should have tabbed navigation with at minimum: General (current flat form), Users, Branding, and Notifications tabs — matching standard SaaS product settings structure.
- **Actual:** Settings renders as a single flat form with Contact Phone, Address, Tax Rate, and a Notifications toggle. There are no tabs, and critical configuration surfaces like Users and Branding are entirely absent.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator and navigate to Settings.
  2. Observe a single flat form with no tabs.
  3. Navigate to `/settings/users`, `/settings/branding` — both return "Page could not be found."
- **Fix:** Implement full tabbed Settings layout. Users tab is especially critical (see RF-090).
- **Prevention:** Add Settings tab navigation to the release acceptance checklist. Add smoke tests for each tab route asserting they render a non-error page. Block merge of any settings-related feature PRs until the tab scaffold exists.

### RF-120 — Settings validation missing for ZIP and tax rate fields

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux / settings
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W16-002, W16-004
- **Issue:** ZIP field accepts non-numeric strings ("ABC"). Tax rate accepts out-of-range values (101, -1). Both save silently with success toast.
- **Expected:** The ZIP field should validate the format before saving and display an inline error for non-numeric or improperly formatted entries. The tax rate field should reject values outside 0–100% and display an inline error.
- **Actual:** Entering "ABC" in the ZIP field and -1 or 101 in the tax rate field both result in a success toast and the invalid values are saved to the database, silently corrupting tenant configuration.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to Settings as operator.
  2. Enter "ABC" in the ZIP field and click Save — observe success toast with no error.
  3. Enter "101" in the tax rate field and click Save — observe success toast with no error.
  4. Confirm the invalid values are persisted via API.
- **Fix:** Add ZIP validation regex (`/^\d{5}(-\d{4})?$/`). Add tax rate range validation (0–1 decimal or 0–100 percent). Show inline errors before saving.
- **Prevention:** Add class-validator decorators (`@IsPostalCode`, `@Min(0)`, `@Max(100)`) to the `UpdateTenantDto` so invalid values are rejected at the API layer even if the UI validation is bypassed. Add a form validation unit test for the Settings component covering both fields.

### RF-121 — Settings save toast fires without confirming PATCH request succeeded

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux / settings
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W16-011
- **Issue:** "Save changes" toast fires on button click regardless of whether the PATCH request completed. Network monitoring showed intermittent cases where `OPTIONS` preflight fired but no `PATCH` followed, yet toast still appeared.
- **Expected:** The success toast should only appear after receiving a 2xx HTTP response from the PATCH request. A network failure or 4xx/5xx response should display an error toast instead.
- **Actual:** The success toast fires immediately on button click, before the PATCH response is received. If the request fails silently, the operator believes settings were saved when they were not.
- **Repro:**
  1. Throttle the network to simulate a slow connection in browser DevTools.
  2. Click "Save changes" in Settings.
  3. Observe the success toast fires immediately — before the PATCH response arrives or even if the request is dropped.
- **Fix:** Gate success toast on confirmed 2xx response from the PATCH. Show error toast on 4xx/5xx.
- **Prevention:** Establish a UI pattern rule: success toasts must always be triggered inside the `.then()` or `onSuccess` callback of the API call, never on the button click handler. Add a UI test for Settings save that intercepts the PATCH and returns 500, asserting an error toast is shown (not a success toast).

### RF-122 — Settings Branding tab absent — no logo upload or color picker in UI

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux / settings
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W16-009
- **Issue:** No Branding tab in Settings. No logo upload, no color picker, no branding preview.
- **Note:** Logo upload endpoint exists at the API level and has critical security issues (RF-076). The UI absence means the dangerous SVG path is currently harder to reach, but it remains accessible via direct API calls.
- **Expected:** The Settings screen should have a Branding tab that allows operators to upload a logo (raster images only), pick a brand color, and preview how the branding will appear across the platform.
- **Actual:** No Branding tab exists. Operators cannot set a logo or brand color via the UI, and the dangerous logo upload API endpoint (RF-076) is still accessible via direct API calls.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator and navigate to Settings.
  2. Observe there is no Branding tab.
  3. Navigate directly to `/settings/branding` — "Page could not be found."
- **Fix:** Implement Branding tab with logo upload (PNG/JPG/WEBP only — reject SVG per RF-076 fix), color picker, and live preview.
- **Prevention:** Ensure the Branding tab ships at the same time as the RF-076 SVG security fix so the upload UI enforces the safe MIME allowlist from day one. Add a smoke test for the Branding tab asserting logo upload rejects SVG files.

## Additional P3 — Low Priority

### RF-123 — Buyer timestamp order numbers (13-digit) vs sequential for operator-created orders

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-006
- **Issue:** Orders placed through the buyer portal get timestamp-based numbers (`#ORD-1777431385833`). Operator-created orders use sequential format (`#ORD-00001`). Mixed formats appear on the same operator orders list.
- **Expected:** All orders regardless of origin (buyer portal or operator dashboard) should use the same sequential number format (`#ORD-00001`) for consistency across reporting, invoicing, and customer communication.
- **Actual:** Buyer-portal orders receive 13-digit timestamp-based numbers while operator-created orders use short sequential numbers. The operator orders list shows a jarring mix of both formats.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create one order as operator — it receives `#ORD-00001`.
  2. Place one order as a buyer — it receives `#ORD-1777431385833`.
  3. View the operator orders list — both formats appear in the same column.
- **Fix:** Use the same sequential number generator for buyer-portal orders as for operator-created orders.
- **Prevention:** Centralize order number generation in a single `generateOrderNumber()` utility used by all order creation paths. Add a test asserting buyer-portal orders receive sequential (not timestamp) numbers.

### RF-124 — No delivery timestamp on DELIVERED order detail in buyer portal

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-007
- **Issue:** DELIVERED order detail shows only order creation date. No `deliveredAt` timestamp.
- **Expected:** The DELIVERED order detail screen should display the actual delivery timestamp so buyers can confirm when goods were delivered.
- **Actual:** Only the order creation date is shown. The `deliveredAt` field is not displayed, leaving buyers unable to verify delivery time from their portal.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as a buyer with a DELIVERED order.
  2. Open the order detail screen.
  3. Observe only the order creation date — no delivery date or time is shown.
- **Fix:** Display `deliveredAt` field on DELIVERED orders in buyer portal.
- **Prevention:** Add `deliveredAt` to the buyer order detail component spec as a required field for DELIVERED-status orders. Add a UI test asserting the delivery timestamp is rendered on DELIVERED order detail.

### RF-125 — No PDF download action on invoice detail in buyer portal

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-008
- **Issue:** Invoice detail page has no "Download PDF" or "View PDF" CTA.
- **Expected:** The invoice detail screen in the buyer portal should include a "Download PDF" or "View PDF" button that fetches the invoice PDF for the buyer's records.
- **Actual:** The invoice detail page shows invoice line items and totals but provides no way to download or view the PDF. Buyers who need a PDF copy for their own accounting must request it from the operator directly.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as a buyer and navigate to an invoice detail screen.
  2. Observe there is no "Download PDF" or "View PDF" button.
- **Fix:** Add PDF download CTA on invoice detail page.
- **Prevention:** Add PDF download as a required acceptance criterion for the buyer invoice detail screen. Add a UI smoke test asserting the PDF download button is present and links to the correct endpoint on SENT/PAID invoices.

### RF-126 — Buyer portal dashboard stats show blank order count and unpaid amount

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-009
- **Issue:** Stats row shows "Orders | $0 Total Spend | Unpaid" but numeric values for "Orders" count and "Unpaid" are blank. Only Total Spend shows a value.
- **Expected:** The buyer dashboard stats row should display the total order count and the outstanding unpaid balance alongside the total spend — all three fields populated from the buyer profile API response.
- **Actual:** The "Orders" count cell and "Unpaid" balance cell are blank. Only "Total Spend" renders a value, making two of the three key dashboard stats non-functional.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as a buyer with at least one order and one unpaid invoice.
  2. Navigate to the buyer dashboard.
  3. Observe the stat cells for "Orders" count and "Unpaid" amount are empty.
- **Fix:** Bind orders count and unpaid balance from buyer profile API response to the correct stat cells.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI test for the buyer dashboard asserting all three stat cells render non-empty values when the buyer has orders and unpaid invoices. Add the dashboard stats to the buyer portal smoke test suite.

### RF-127 — Category filter pills go blank during search — labels cleared by search state change

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15-010
- **Issue:** Category filter pill labels are cleared when a search query is entered, leaving unlabeled pills that cannot be deselected or identified.
- **Expected:** Category filter pills should retain their labels at all times, including while a search is active, so buyers can see and toggle which category filters are applied.
- **Actual:** When a buyer types in the search field, category pill labels disappear. The pills remain as blank buttons that cannot be meaningfully identified or deselected.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to the buyer product catalog.
  2. Select a category filter pill — the label is visible.
  3. Type any character in the search field.
  4. Observe the category pill labels disappear, leaving blank buttons.
- **Fix:** Ensure category pill labels are not cleared by the search state change.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI test asserting that category pill labels remain visible while a search query is active. Decouple category label state from search query state in the component.

### RF-128 — Profile page shows raw TENANT_ADMIN enum string instead of "Administrator"

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W16-013
- **Issue:** The operator Profile page displays the raw database enum value `TENANT_ADMIN` as the user's role label rather than a human-readable string.
- **Expected:** The role label on the Profile page should display "Administrator" for `TENANT_ADMIN`, "Driver" for `DRIVER`, and "Operator" for `OPERATOR` — human-readable strings throughout the UI.
- **Actual:** The Profile page shows the raw enum value `TENANT_ADMIN` directly in the role label field, which is confusing and looks like a developer error to end users.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as TENANT_ADMIN (`ux_admin`).
  2. Navigate to the Profile page.
  3. Observe the role label reads "TENANT_ADMIN" instead of "Administrator."
- **Fix:** Map `TENANT_ADMIN` → "Administrator", `DRIVER` → "Driver" in all role display contexts.
- **Prevention:** Create a shared `roleLabel` utility function used by every role display in the UI and add it to the shared component library. Add a snapshot test asserting the Profile page renders "Administrator" for `TENANT_ADMIN` users.

---

### RF-129 — Offline queue has no retry backoff — immediately retries on weak connectivity

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** driver-pod / offline
- **Surface:** mobile-driver
- **Source:** W19-006
- **Issue:** Sync retries happen immediately on connectivity restore, draining battery and hammering server on flaky connections.
- **Expected:** Failed offline queue retries should use exponential backoff (starting at 1s, doubling up to a 30s cap) to avoid hammering the server and draining battery on flaky connections.
- **Actual:** The offline queue retries immediately and continuously on every connectivity restore event, regardless of how many prior failures have occurred. On a weak 2G signal that repeatedly connects and drops, this floods the server and drains battery rapidly.
- **Repro:**
  1. Queue several offline actions in the driver app.
  2. Enable a flaky network connection that toggles every few seconds.
  3. Monitor network requests — the app hammers the server with rapid retries on each brief connectivity window, with no delay between attempts.
- **Fix:** Implement exponential backoff (1s, 2s, 4s, 8s, max 30s) for retry attempts.
- **Prevention:** Add a retry backoff unit test asserting that the delay between consecutive retry attempts doubles up to the cap. Use a shared retry utility (e.g., `p-retry` or a custom exponential backoff hook) consistently across all queued action retry paths.

## Phase 3 Findings (RF-130–RF-138)

_Source: W17 (Finance sub-pages browser testing — 2026-04-30)_

---

### RF-130 — Finance home is AP-only; no AR Aging dashboard exists

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-001
- **Issue:** Finance home shows only AP metrics (vendor bills, expenses, suppliers). No AR Aging report (current / 1–30 / 31–60 / 61–90 / 90+ buckets) exists anywhere in the operator app. The More > Insights section only has "Analytics" (Revenue, top items, margins). The stat "79 Overdue invoices" links to a raw list, not an aging breakdown.
- **Expected:** The Finance screen should include an AR Aging report that buckets outstanding invoice balances into Current, 1–30, 31–60, 61–90, and 90+ day ranges, allowing operators to prioritize collections and identify at-risk customers.
- **Actual:** Finance home shows only AP metrics. The "79 Overdue invoices" stat links to a flat raw list with no aging breakdown. Operators have no dashboard-level visibility into how long receivables have been outstanding.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator and navigate to Finance.
  2. Observe the home screen shows only vendor bills, expenses, and supplier metrics.
  3. Navigate to More > Insights > Analytics — only Revenue, Top Items, and Margins are available; no AR Aging.
- **Fix:** Add an AR Aging report screen accessible from Finance or Insights.
- **Prevention:** Add AR Aging to the Finance screen acceptance criteria. Add a smoke test asserting the AR Aging screen renders with at least one aging bucket when overdue invoices exist.

---

### RF-131 — Date fields default to 2025-06-01 (11 months in the past)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-002
- **Issue:** Both the new vendor bill date field and the invoice "Record Payment" date field pre-populate with `2025-06-01` — ~11 months in the past relative to the 2026-04-30 audit date. Operators not noticing will record bills or payments with a stale date, corrupting AR/AP aging.
- **Expected:** Date fields for vendor bill creation and payment recording should default to today's date, so operators record financial events on the correct date without needing to manually change the pre-populated value.
- **Actual:** Both date fields default to `2025-06-01` — approximately 11 months in the past. Operators who do not notice the stale default will record vendor bills and payments with incorrect dates, corrupting AR/AP aging and audit history.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to Finance > Vendor Bills > New Bill.
  2. Observe the date field defaults to `2025-06-01`.
  3. Navigate to an invoice > Record Payment.
  4. Observe the payment date field also defaults to `2025-06-01`.
- **Fix:** Replace the hard-coded `2025-06-01` with `new Date().toISOString().split('T')[0]`.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI test asserting the date field default value equals today's date (within 1 day tolerance). Add a lint rule or code review checklist item banning hardcoded date strings in UI component default values.

---

### RF-132 — Date stored as UTC midnight renders 1 day earlier in local timezone

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-003
- **Issue:** Date-only inputs (e.g., `2026-04-30`) are parsed as UTC midnight (`T00:00:00Z`) and displayed in the local timezone, which shows the prior day for any timezone west of UTC. Affects vendor bill detail and expense detail display.
- **Expected:** A bill entered with date `2026-04-30` should display as `2026-04-30` regardless of the operator's local timezone. Date-only fields should never shift by a day due to UTC offset conversion.
- **Actual:** Entering `2026-04-30` stores it as `2026-04-30T00:00:00Z`. When displayed in a timezone like UTC-5, it renders as `2026-04-29` — one day earlier than entered. This corrupts vendor bill and expense dates for all operators outside UTC.
- **Repro:**
  1. Set the browser timezone to UTC-5.
  2. Create a vendor bill with date `2026-04-30`.
  3. View the bill detail — the date displays as `2026-04-29`.
- **Fix:** Parse YYYY-MM-DD strings without timezone conversion: `new Date(dateStr + 'T12:00:00')`, or use a date-only display utility. Same root cause as RF-137.
- **Prevention:** Create a shared `formatDateOnly(dateStr: string)` utility used in all date-only display contexts that avoids UTC conversion. Add a snapshot test comparing input date string to displayed string across multiple timezone offsets.

---

### RF-133 — "Mark received" button active on already-Received vendor bills (UI idempotency gap)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-004
- **Issue:** On a vendor bill with status Received, the "Mark received" button remains enabled. Clicking it shows "Bill marked as received." toast with no error — silently re-fires the API call. This is the UI counterpart of the API-level gap filed as RF-084 (no server-side idempotency guard on `receive()`).
- **Expected:** The "Mark received" button should be disabled or hidden on bills that already have status Received. A "Mark paid" button should be shown instead, matching the Received → Paid lifecycle step.
- **Actual:** The "Mark received" button remains enabled on already-Received bills. Clicking it fires the API call again, triggers the success toast, and (without the RF-084 API fix) doubles the stock increment.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a vendor bill and click "Mark received" — bill status becomes Received.
  2. While the bill status shows Received, click "Mark received" again.
  3. Observe a "Bill marked as received" success toast with no error — the API is called again.
- **Fix:** Conditionally disable/hide "Mark received" when `bill.status === 'RECEIVED'`. Show "Mark paid" (RF-134) instead.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI test asserting the "Mark received" button is disabled/hidden on Received bills and the "Mark paid" button is visible. Implement this fix atomically with the RF-084 API-level idempotency fix.

---

### RF-134 — No "Mark paid" transition for vendor bills; lifecycle terminates at Received

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** browser-ux / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-005
- **Issue:** The vendor bill lifecycle Draft → Received → Paid is incomplete. No "Mark paid" button exists on Received bills. The "Paid" filter tab always shows "No bills yet." No bill can ever reach Paid status via the UI.
- **Expected:** A Received vendor bill should have a "Mark paid" button that transitions it to Paid status, records a payment date and reference, and removes it from the outstanding AP balance. The "Paid" filter tab should show all paid bills.
- **Actual:** No "Mark paid" action exists in the UI. The vendor bill lifecycle is permanently stuck at Received — no bill can ever reach Paid status through normal UI operation. The "Paid" filter tab is always empty regardless of how many bills exist.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a vendor bill and mark it as Received.
  2. View the bill detail — no "Mark paid" button is present.
  3. Navigate to Finance > Vendor Bills > Paid tab — "No bills yet" always displays.
- **Fix:** Add a "Mark paid" button on bill detail when `bill.status === 'RECEIVED'`, wired to `PATCH /vendor-bills/:id` with `{ status: 'PAID' }`. Optionally prompt for payment date and reference number.
- **Prevention:** Add the complete vendor bill lifecycle (Draft → Received → Paid) to the release acceptance criteria and smoke tests. Add a UI test asserting the "Mark paid" button appears on Received bills and the Paid filter tab shows records after payment.

---

### RF-135 — Expenses have no status-change actions; all expenses stuck at Pending

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** browser-ux / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-006
- **Issue:** Expense detail only shows "Edit" and "Delete expense". No "Mark paid" or "Void" button exists, and the edit form has no Status field. All expenses created via the UI are permanently stuck in "Pending". Paid/Void statuses exist only for API-seeded records.
- **Expected:** Expense detail should include "Mark paid" and "Void" action buttons that allow operators to progress expenses through their lifecycle (Pending → Paid or Pending → Void), reflecting actual payment or cancellation.
- **Actual:** All UI-created expenses are permanently stuck at Pending status. There is no "Mark paid" or "Void" button, and the edit form has no Status field. Expenses cannot be closed via any UI action, making the expense management feature non-functional.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an expense via Finance > Expenses > New.
  2. View the expense detail — only "Edit" and "Delete expense" buttons exist.
  3. Click "Edit" — the form has no Status field.
  4. Observe the expense remains "Pending" with no path to "Paid" or "Void."
- **Fix:** Add "Mark paid" and "Void" action buttons to expense detail, wired to `PATCH /expenses/:id` with `{ status: 'PAID' | 'VOID' }`.
- **Prevention:** Add expense lifecycle (Pending → Paid, Pending → Void) to the release acceptance criteria. Add a UI test asserting the "Mark paid" and "Void" buttons are present on a Pending expense detail and that clicking them changes the status correctly.

---

### RF-136 — Duplicate invoice entries in Overdue filter list

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** browser-ux / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-007
- **Issue:** INV-000422 appears twice consecutively in the Overdue-filtered invoice list with identical fields (customer, amount, due date). Likely cause: JOIN on payments or line items without DISTINCT producing duplicate rows, or the frontend array being concatenated twice.
- **Expected:** Each invoice should appear exactly once in the Overdue filter list. No duplicate rows should be rendered regardless of how many payment or line-item records are associated with the invoice.
- **Actual:** INV-000422 appears twice in the Overdue list with identical customer, amount, and due date. Operators cannot distinguish a genuine duplicate from a rendering artifact, and the inflated count may cause double-collection attempts.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator and navigate to Finance > Invoices.
  2. Apply the Overdue filter.
  3. Observe INV-000422 appearing twice consecutively in the list.
- **Fix:** Add DISTINCT / deduplication by invoice ID to the overdue invoices query, or deduplicate by ID before rendering.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the overdue invoices query asserting no duplicate IDs appear in the result set for invoices with multiple payment records. Add a UI deduplication step as a safety net: `invoices = _.uniqBy(invoices, 'id')` before rendering.

---

### RF-137 — PDF due date renders 1 day later than in-app display (UTC vs local time)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-008
- **Issue:** INV-000426 shows "Due 4/13/2026" in-app but "Due Date: Apr 14, 2026" in the PDF — 1-day discrepancy in the opposite direction from RF-132. Together they confirm: dates are stored as `DATETIME` UTC midnight and converted inconsistently — in-app shows local time (Apr 13), PDF renderer shows UTC (Apr 14).
- **Expected:** The invoice due date should be identical in the in-app display and the PDF, regardless of timezone. The two rendering contexts should agree on the same calendar date.
- **Actual:** INV-000426 displays "Due 4/13/2026" in the app (local timezone conversion) but "Due Date: Apr 14, 2026" in the PDF (UTC). A buyer or operator comparing the app to the printed PDF sees conflicting due dates, creating potential disputes.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice with a due date of `2026-04-13` in a UTC-5 timezone.
  2. View the invoice detail in-app — due date shows "4/13/2026."
  3. Download the invoice PDF — due date shows "Apr 14, 2026" (UTC).
- **Fix:** Store invoice due dates as date-only strings (no time component), or standardize all display to UTC. Same root cause as RF-132.
- **Prevention:** Add a test that creates an invoice, renders the PDF, and asserts the due date string in the PDF matches the due date string in the API response. Standardize on a shared date formatting utility used by both the UI and the PDF renderer.

---

### RF-138 — "Staff & Drivers" company-code onboarding routes to Customer Portal

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth / browser-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W17-009
- **Issue:** On the onboarding sign-in page: select "Staff & Drivers" → enter tenant code → Continue → app navigates to `/customer-login` (Customer Portal) instead of `/login` (staff/operator login). Operators and drivers using this onboarding flow cannot log in through it.
- **Expected:** Selecting "Staff & Drivers" on the onboarding page and entering a tenant code should navigate to `/login` (the staff/operator login screen), not the customer portal.
- **Actual:** The "Staff & Drivers" onboarding path navigates to `/customer-login` (Customer Portal). Operators and drivers following the onboarding instructions are deposited on the wrong login screen and cannot access their accounts through this flow.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to the onboarding sign-in page.
  2. Select "Staff & Drivers," enter a valid tenant code, and click Continue.
  3. Observe the app navigates to `/customer-login` instead of `/login`.
- **Fix:** In the company-code Continue handler, navigate to `/login` for Staff & Drivers and `/customer-login` for Customer. The two paths must be mutually exclusive.
- **Prevention:** Add an onboarding routing test asserting that "Staff & Drivers" selection navigates to `/login` and "Customer" selection navigates to `/customer-login`. Include onboarding routing in the release smoke test suite.

## Phase 4 Findings (RF-139–RF-146)

_Sources: W24 (Phase 9 corner scenarios + Phase 10 security code audit — 2026-04-30); W22 (Phase 13 cross-role race conditions + Phase 10 security browser testing — 2026-04-30)_

---

### RF-139 — Invoice and order totals calculated with floating-point arithmetic; $0.01 rounding errors possible

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W24 / 9.A.1
- **Issue:** All invoice and order totals use native JS `number` (IEEE 754 double-precision float), not a decimal library. The DB stores financial columns as `Decimal(10,2)`, but Prisma returns JS `number` via `Number(...)` casts. Pattern in `invoices.service.ts:107–151, 569–603, 1519–1535` and `orders.service.ts:589–607`: `const lineSub = qty * item.unitPrice - (item.discount ?? 0)`. Classic $0.01 rounding errors accumulate on multi-line invoices. `applyPriceAdjustment()` never rounds before persisting the updated total.
- **Expected:** Invoice and order totals should be calculated using decimal-safe arithmetic so multi-line invoices always produce totals that match the sum of their line items to the cent, with no floating-point rounding errors.
- **Actual:** Floating-point accumulation errors cause multi-line invoice totals to diverge from the mathematically correct sum by $0.01 or more. For example, `0.1 + 0.2` in JS equals `0.30000000000000004`. On invoices with many line items, these errors compound and are persisted to the database without rounding.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice with 10+ line items where quantities and prices involve decimal values (e.g., 3× $3.33).
  2. Retrieve the invoice total — it may differ from the hand-calculated sum by $0.01.
  3. Verify the persisted `total` column in the database reflects the imprecise float.
- **Fix:** Round each line subtotal and final total: `Math.round(value * 100) / 100`. Or adopt `decimal.js` / `big.js` throughout the financial calculation layer.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test with a known multi-line invoice (designed to expose float errors) asserting the computed total exactly matches the expected value. Use `decimal.js` as the standard for all financial arithmetic and enforce it via a lint rule banning `*`, `+`, `-` on financial values without decimal wrapping.

---

### RF-140 — Discount stacking allows $0 invoice total with no confirmation

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API / web-operator
- **Source:** W24 / 9.A.2
- **Issue:** Three discount layers interact with no documentation or guard: (1) customer tier price, (2) line-item flat discount, (3) invoice-level flat discount. The guard `invDiscount <= subtotal` permits setting the discount equal to the subtotal, producing `total = $0 + shipping + tax`. With zero shipping and zero tax, an operator can silently zero out an invoice with no confirmation required and no alert that an invoice is being issued for free.
- **Expected:** Creating or saving an invoice with a $0 total (after all discount layers are applied) should require an explicit confirmation step, with a warning that the invoice will be issued for no charge.
- **Actual:** An operator can stack discounts to produce a $0 invoice and it is saved and sent silently — no warning, no confirmation, no audit note. This could be used to fraudulently zero out real invoices without detection.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice with subtotal $100.
  2. Set an invoice-level discount of $100 (equal to subtotal).
  3. Observe the invoice is saved and can be sent with a total of $0, no confirmation required.
- **Fix:** Add JSDoc documenting the discount-application order. Require a `confirmZeroTotal: true` flag before persisting a $0 invoice. Alert the operator in the UI.
- **Prevention:** Add a validation rule that returns 422 if the computed total is $0 without `confirmZeroTotal: true` in the request. Add an audit log entry when a $0 invoice is confirmed. Add a unit test asserting $0-total invoices are rejected without the confirmation flag.

---

### RF-141 — `bulkDeleteProducts()` destroys open order items and live invoice line items

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W24 / 9.C.1
- **Issue:** `products.service.ts:255–277`: the single-product `remove()` correctly checks for active order items and blocks deletion. `bulkDelete()` has **no such guard** — it directly deletes `OrderItem` rows (including items in PENDING/CONFIRMED/IN_PROGRESS orders) and `InvoiceItem` rows (including lines on SENT/PARTIAL invoices). Additionally, `clearAll()` at line 247 runs `TRUNCATE TABLE "Product" CASCADE` with zero safety checks.
- **Expected:** Bulk-deleting products should apply the same pre-deletion guard as single-product delete: if any selected product is referenced by an active order or open invoice, the entire bulk delete should be rejected with a descriptive error listing the blocking references.
- **Actual:** Bulk-deleting products silently destroys `OrderItem` and `InvoiceItem` rows for open orders and sent invoices, permanently corrupting financial records. Additionally, `clearAll()` runs a bare `TRUNCATE TABLE "Product" CASCADE` with no safety checks.
- **Repro:** Via API, bulk-delete a set of products that includes one referenced by an open PENDING order — the order's line items are silently destroyed.
- **Fix:** Mirror the single-delete pre-check in `bulkDelete()`: abort if any selected product has active order items or open invoice items. Restrict `clearAll()` to non-production environments via `NODE_ENV` guard.
- **Prevention:** Extract the "is product deletable?" check into a shared `assertProductDeletable(productId)` function called by both single and bulk delete paths. Add a unit test for `bulkDeleteProducts()` asserting it rejects when any product in the batch is referenced by an open order. Gate `clearAll()` with `if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') throw new ForbiddenException()`.

---

### RF-142 — `localhost` CORS bypass active in production; no Content-Security-Policy header

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W24 / 10.8
- **Issues:**
  1. `main.ts:82–116`: The CORS origin callback contains `if (/^http:\/\/localhost:\d+$/.test(origin)) return callback(null, true)`. This block runs in **all environments**, including Railway production. Any attacker with a page served from `http://localhost:<any port>` can make credentialed cross-origin requests to the production API.
  2. `app.use(helmet())` uses default options — Helmet does not set `Content-Security-Policy` by default, leaving the Swagger UI at `/api/docs` without a CSP.
- **Note:** `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` is NOT used — the allowlist pattern is otherwise correct.
- **Expected:** In production, only explicitly listed allowed origins should be permitted. The `localhost` bypass should be inactive in production environments. The Swagger UI should be served with a Content-Security-Policy header.
- **Actual:** The `localhost` CORS bypass is active in all environments including Railway production. Any page served from `http://localhost:<any port>` can make credentialed API requests to the production backend. No Content-Security-Policy is set on any route.
- **Repro:**
  1. From any local HTTP server (`http://localhost:1337`), send a credentialed CORS request to the production API with `Origin: http://localhost:1337`.
  2. Observe the response includes `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: http://localhost:1337` — the request is accepted.
- **Fix:** Wrap the localhost bypass in `if (process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production')`. Add a CSP via `helmet({ contentSecurityPolicy: { ... } })` on the Swagger route.
- **Prevention:** Add a CORS integration test that sends requests with `Origin: http://localhost:9999` and asserts the production environment returns `403` or omits the CORS header. Add CSP to the production Helmet configuration and verify with a security header scan in CI.

---

### RF-143 — Audit log captures actions but not field-level diffs on sensitive mutations

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W24 / audit log review
- **Issue:** `audit.interceptor.ts` logs every `POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE` with `tenantId`, `userId`, `action`, `entityType`, `entityId`, and `ip` — good baseline coverage. However, sensitive mutations (role escalation `DRIVER → TENANT_ADMIN`, password reset, mass delete, invoice void) log only the HTTP method + path with no before/after payload diff in `meta`. A role escalation attack (RF-073) would produce only `PATCH /users/:id` in the audit log with no indication of what changed.
- **Expected:** Audit log entries for sensitive mutations should include a `{ before, after }` diff in the `meta` field, enabling security teams to determine exactly what changed and by whom without querying the main database.
- **Actual:** All mutation audit entries record only `HTTP method + path`. A role escalation from DRIVER to TENANT_ADMIN is indistinguishable in the audit log from any other user profile update — the log shows `PATCH /users/:id` with no field-level diff.
- **Repro:**
  1. Execute a role escalation by calling `PATCH /users/:id` with `{"role":"OPERATOR"}`.
  2. Inspect the audit log entry — it contains only `{action:"UPDATE", entityType:"User", entityId:...}` with no `before`/`after` diff.
- **Fix:** For mutations on `User.role`, `User.password`, `Invoice.status`, and bulk-delete endpoints, include a `{ before, after }` diff in the audit log `meta` field.
- **Prevention:** Add an audit log integration test for role-change mutations asserting the log entry contains `meta.before.role` and `meta.after.role`. Define a "sensitive fields" list and enforce before/after diffing for those fields via code review checklist.

---

### RF-144 — No skip-one-occurrence for standing orders; template cannot pause a single date

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / scheduling
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W24 / 9.D.1
- **Issue:** `order-templates.service.ts::generateDailyOrders()` filters by `isActive` and `daysOfWeek` only. No `skipDates` field exists on `OrderTemplate`. The only way to prevent a specific occurrence is to deactivate or delete the entire template, which affects all future fires.
- **Expected:** Operators should be able to mark specific dates as skipped for a standing order template (e.g., skip the December 25 occurrence) without deactivating the entire recurring schedule.
- **Actual:** There is no skip-date mechanism. Preventing a single occurrence requires fully deactivating the template, which also cancels all future occurrences until the template is manually reactivated.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a weekly standing order template.
  2. Try to skip only the next occurrence without affecting subsequent ones — there is no UI action or API field to accomplish this.
  3. The only option is to deactivate the template and reactivate it after the skipped date has passed.
- **Fix:** Add `skipDates DateTime[]` to the `OrderTemplate` model. Filter in the cron: `NOT: { skipDates: { has: todayMidnight } }`. Expose skip-date management in the UI.
- **Prevention:** Add a cron unit test that seeds a template with a skip date matching today and asserts no order is generated for that date. Expose a "Skip next occurrence" action in the standing order management UI.

---

### RF-145 — Price change between browse and checkout not communicated to buyer

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-buyer / mobile-customer
- **Source:** W22 / 13.10 (browser-confirmed)
- **Issue:** The server correctly enforces live pricing — `POST /buyer/orders` ignores any `unitPrice` in the request body and always uses the current catalog price. However, if a product's price is updated by the operator between when the buyer last viewed the catalog and when checkout completes, the buyer is silently charged the new price with no warning. There is no `priceAdjusted` flag in the 201 response, no toast, and no line-level indicator in the order confirmation.
- **Expected:** If any product's price changed between cart-add and checkout, the order confirmation response should include a `priceAdjusted: true` flag and list the affected items, and the buyer portal should surface a clear "Prices were updated" notice before or after order placement.
- **Actual:** A buyer who adds Butter 500g at $5.49, then checks out after the operator changes the price to $50.00, is silently charged $50.00 with no warning. The 201 response contains no indication that prices differed from what the buyer saw.
- **Repro:** Buyer adds Butter 500g ($5.49) to cart. Operator changes price to $50.00. Buyer checks out — order creates successfully at $50.00 with no buyer-facing warning.
- **Fix:** When the server-resolved `unitPrice` differs from the price at cart-add time, include a `priceAdjusted: true` flag in the order response. The buyer portal should surface "Prices were updated for X item(s) before your order was placed."
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test that creates an order after a mid-browse price change and asserts the response contains `priceAdjusted: true` and lists the affected items. Consider a buyer-opt-in pattern: if prices changed by more than X%, require an explicit re-confirmation before finalizing the order.

---

### RF-146 — Frontend JS bundle hardcodes undocumented Railway API hostname

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** infrastructure
- **Surface:** web-buyer / mobile-customer
- **Source:** W22 / 10.7 (browser-confirmed)
- **Issue:** The frontend JS bundle (`entry-accc24f5ed58f1773525d07807cf3835.js`) hardcodes `https://routeflowapi-production-d504.up.railway.app` as the API base URL. The documented production URL is `routeflowapi-production.up.railway.app`. Both resolve to the same deployment today, but if the two Railway services ever diverge in code version or environment variables, the frontend will silently be pointed at the wrong backend. There is no runtime configuration mechanism to override this without a new build.
- **Expected:** The API base URL should be injected at build time via an environment variable (`EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL`) so it can be changed without a code modification, and a single canonical hostname should be used consistently.
- **Actual:** The API base URL is hardcoded in the compiled JS bundle. Changing the backend hostname requires a new build and deploy. The hardcoded hostname differs from the documented production URL, creating confusion about which backend the frontend is actually talking to.
- **Repro:**
  1. Open browser DevTools on the production frontend.
  2. Search the JS bundle for `routeflowapi` — find the hardcoded undocumented hostname.
  3. Compare to the documented production URL — they differ.
- **Fix:** Move API base URL to `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` (or equivalent build-time env var). Verify both hostnames currently point to the same Railway service, and consolidate to a single canonical hostname.
- **Prevention:** Add a CI check asserting no hardcoded hostname strings matching `*.railway.app` appear in the source code. Use `EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL` consistently and document it in the `.env.example` file.
