## Phase 5 Findings (RF-147–RF-151)

_Source: W23 (Phase 7 E2E revenue cycle and return flow — 2026-04-30)_

---

### RF-147 — `createInvoiceFromOrder()` stores `tenantId: null`; buyers see zero invoices

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API / web-buyer
- **Source:** W23 / E2E-1 Step 4 (browser-confirmed)
- **Issue:** `POST /invoices/from-order/:orderId` (the standard path for creating an invoice from a confirmed order) produces invoices with `tenantId: null`. The buyer-facing `GET /buyer/invoices` endpoint uses the `forTenant()` Prisma extension scope, which filters by `tenantId`, thereby excluding every invoice created via this path. **Result: buyers see zero invoices in their portal regardless of how many invoices exist.** The PDF is accessible via direct URL (the link is visible on the operator side), but buyers cannot discover or list it. Confirmed live: INV-2026-0010 was created with `tenantId: null` and returned no results on `GET /buyer/invoices`.
- **Expected:** Invoices created from orders via `POST /invoices/from-order/:orderId` should be stamped with the correct `tenantId` from the order context and appear in the buyer's `GET /buyer/invoices` list.
- **Actual:** Every invoice created via this path has `tenantId: null`. The `forTenant()` filter excludes them from all tenant-scoped queries, making the buyer portal's invoice list permanently empty regardless of how many invoices the operator has created.
- **Repro:**
  1. As operator, create an invoice from a confirmed order via `POST /invoices/from-order/:orderId`.
  2. As the buyer for that order, call `GET /buyer/invoices`.
  3. Observe an empty array — the invoice is invisible.
  4. Inspect the invoice record in the database — `tenantId` is `null`.
- **Fix:** In `invoices.service.ts` `createInvoiceFromOrder()`, explicitly pass `tenantId` to the `invoice.create()` call, sourced from the order or the current user context — the same way standalone invoice creation populates it.
- **Prevention:** Add a DB-level `NOT NULL` constraint on `Invoice.tenantId`. Add an integration test that creates an invoice from an order and asserts it appears in `GET /buyer/invoices`. Add a Prisma middleware check that throws if `tenantId` is omitted on any tenant-scoped model create.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

---

### RF-148 — `POST /buyer/orders` silently injects standing-order template items into submitted order

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** buyer-portal
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W23 / E2E-1 Step 1
- **Issue:** When a buyer submits `POST /buyer/orders`, the service calls `mergeAllPendingForCustomer()` which silently merges any pending standing-order template items into the order being placed. A buyer who submits 3 items receives a 201 response for an order with 4 (or more) items at a higher total — with no warning, no diff shown, and no opportunity to review. This is a silent unauthorized price change from the buyer's perspective.
- **Expected:** A manually placed order should contain exactly the items the buyer selected. Standing-order template items should not be merged into a manual order without explicit buyer consent shown in the checkout UI.
- **Actual:** Calling `POST /buyer/orders` on a day when a standing-order template is active silently injects additional items into the order. A buyer submitting 3 items receives a 201 response for an order containing 4+ items at a higher total, with no indication of what was added.
- **Repro:** Buyer 4 (standing M/W/F) submits a manual order for 3 items on a Wednesday morning. The resulting order contains 4 items with an inflated total.
- **Fix:** Do not silently merge standing-order items into manually placed orders. If the intent is to offer "you also have a standing order today — add those items?", surface an explicit confirmation prompt before merging.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that `POST /buyer/orders` with 3 explicit items returns an order with exactly 3 items (not merged from template). Add a UI "Standing order items will be added — confirm?" prompt that requires buyer acknowledgment before merge occurs.

---

### RF-149 — `GET /buyer/credit-notes` returns 404; buyers cannot view their credit notes

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API / web-buyer
- **Source:** W23 / E2E-2 Step 4
- **Issue:** `GET /buyer/credit-notes` returns HTTP 404. No buyer-facing credit note listing endpoint exists. Buyers who have credit notes applied to their account have no way to view them, verify the amount, or check their credit balance through the API or portal.
- **Expected:** `GET /buyer/credit-notes` should return the authenticated buyer's credit notes with status, amount, and the invoice they are applied to, so buyers can verify their credit balance.
- **Actual:** The endpoint returns HTTP 404. The buyer portal cannot list credit notes via any documented buyer API path, leaving buyers with no visibility into credits applied to their account.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a buyer that has a credit note applied to their account.
  2. Call `GET /buyer/credit-notes` with the buyer JWT.
  3. Observe HTTP 404.
- **Fix:** Add `GET /buyer/credit-notes` endpoint to the buyer controller, returning credit notes scoped to `customerId = ctx.customerId` with status, amount, and applied-to invoice reference.
- **Prevention:** Add the buyer credit-notes endpoint to the buyer API smoke test suite. Ensure all buyer-facing finance endpoints (invoices, credit notes, statements) are documented and verified against the live deployment during release.

---

### RF-150 — Returns never auto-generate credit notes; `Return.creditNoteId` is always null

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W23 / E2E-2 Step 3
- **Issue:** The plan (`Phase 1.10`, `E2E-2`) documents "auto-create credit note from return." In practice, completing a return leaves `Return.creditNoteId = null`; no credit note is ever automatically created. Operators must manually create a separate credit note and manually link it to the return. This adds friction and creates a gap where returns with no follow-up credit note will never result in a customer refund.
- **Expected:** When a return is approved or received, the system should automatically create a draft credit note for the return's line-item total and link it to the return via `Return.creditNoteId`, so the customer can be refunded without additional manual steps.
- **Actual:** Completing a return always results in `Return.creditNoteId = null`. No credit note is ever automatically created. Operators must manually create and link credit notes, and many returns will be left without any credit note — customers receive no refund.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create and approve a return for a DELIVERED order.
  2. Inspect the return record — `creditNoteId` is always `null`.
  3. No credit note appears in the system for this return.
- **Fix:** After a return transitions to an approved/received state, automatically create a draft credit note for the return's line-item total and set `Return.creditNoteId` to the new credit note ID. Alternatively, document and surface clearly in the UI that credit notes are always manual.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test that approves a return and asserts `Return.creditNoteId` is non-null and a corresponding credit note exists. Add an operator alert for approved returns that have no linked credit note after 24 hours.

---

### RF-151 — Recurring invoices have no `endDate` / `endsAt` field; templates run indefinitely

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / scheduling
- **Surface:** API / web-operator
- **Source:** W23 / Recurring invoice test
- **Issue:** `POST /invoices/recurring` creates a recurring invoice template (`RecurringInvoice` model) with no `endDate` or `endsAt` field in the schema. There is no way to schedule a recurring invoice to stop after a specific date or after N occurrences without manually deleting or deactivating the template. Operators who set up time-limited contracts (e.g., "bill monthly through December 2026") have no automated stop mechanism.
- **Expected:** Operators should be able to set an optional `endDate` on a recurring invoice template so that invoicing stops automatically on that date, without requiring manual intervention.
- **Actual:** Recurring invoice templates run indefinitely once created. Operators must manually delete or deactivate the template to stop billing. There is no `endDate` field in the schema or the create/edit form.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a recurring invoice template for a 6-month contract ending December 2026.
  2. Observe there is no `endDate` field in the create form or API request.
  3. The template will continue generating invoices indefinitely after December 2026 unless manually deactivated.
- **Fix:** Add an optional `endDate DateTime?` field to `RecurringInvoice`. In the cron job that fires recurring invoices, skip templates where `endDate` is set and `endDate < now()`. Expose the field in the create/edit form.
- **Prevention:** Add a cron unit test seeding a template with `endDate = yesterday` and asserting no invoice is generated. Expose `endDate` in the recurring invoice form and add a UI test verifying it is respected.

## Phase 6 Findings (RF-152–RF-157)

_Source: W21 (Phase 13 cross-role real-time operator↔buyer browser testing — 2026-04-30)_

### RF-152 — Operator "Pending orders" dashboard tile count includes non-PENDING orders

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** web-operator / data-integrity
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W21 / 13.1, 13.9 (browser-confirmed)
- **Issue:** The operator `/home` dashboard "Pending orders" tile consistently displayed a count 3 higher than the actual count returned by `GET /orders?status=PENDING`. During W21 testing: tile showed 7 while the filtered endpoint returned 4. After the buyer cancelled an order, the tile dropped from 7 to 6 (correct decrement), but the absolute value remains inflated. The tile likely counts PENDING + CONFIRMED (or PENDING + CONFIRMED + OUT_FOR_DELIVERY), not PENDING only. The label is misleading — operators may over- or under-allocate dispatch resources.
- **Expected:** The "Pending orders" dashboard tile should show a count that exactly matches the number of PENDING-status orders, consistent with what `GET /orders?status=PENDING` returns.
- **Actual:** The tile consistently shows a count 3 higher than the actual PENDING order count. Operators use this number to plan dispatch, so an inflated count leads to incorrect resource allocation.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator and note the "Pending orders" tile count.
  2. Call `GET /orders?status=PENDING` — observe the count is 3 lower than the tile.
  3. Cancel one PENDING order — the tile decrements by 1 (correct), but the absolute value remains inflated.
- **Fix:** Align the dashboard tile query to use `status=PENDING` only, or rename the tile to "Active orders" / "Undelivered orders" to accurately reflect what it counts.
- **Prevention:** Add a dashboard integration test asserting the "Pending orders" tile count matches the count from `GET /orders?status=PENDING`. Add the dashboard tile count accuracy to the release smoke test suite.

---

### RF-153 — Invoice `pdfUrl` is always `null` for manually-created invoices; "View PDF" CTA is broken

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing
- **Surface:** web-operator / web-buyer
- **Source:** W21 / 13.7, 13.8 (browser-confirmed)
- **Issue:** Every invoice created via `POST /invoices` (manual creation, not from a template) has `pdfUrl: null` in the API response. The operator and buyer portals both render a "View PDF" button that navigates to `null`, producing a broken-link error. Buyers cannot download a PDF of their invoice through the UI. This was confirmed on INV-2026-0011 (manually created and sent) — `pdfUrl` field was null throughout its lifecycle.
- **Note:** Invoices created via `POST /invoices/from-order/:orderId` also have `tenantId: null` (RF-147), so both creation paths are defective for different reasons.
- **Expected:** Every invoice should have a `pdfUrl` populated at creation or on first access. The "View PDF" button should navigate to a valid, downloadable PDF for all invoices.
- **Actual:** `pdfUrl` is `null` for all manually-created invoices. The "View PDF" button navigates to `null` — a broken link. Buyers and operators cannot access invoice PDFs through the normal UI.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create an invoice manually via `POST /invoices`.
  2. Send the invoice — `pdfUrl` remains `null` in the response.
  3. Click "View PDF" in the operator or buyer portal — a broken-link or null navigation error occurs.
- **Fix:** Trigger PDF generation (via puppeteer/wkhtmltopdf or a branded-PDF service) at invoice creation or on-demand when "View PDF" is clicked. Populate `pdfUrl` with the generated URL before returning the invoice. Alternatively, generate PDF lazily on first download request.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that `pdfUrl` is non-null on a sent invoice. Add a UI test asserting the "View PDF" button navigates to a non-null, accessible URL. Track PDF generation as a required feature in the invoice release checklist.

---

### RF-154 — `GET /buyer/invoices` returns empty `lineItems: []`; line items only available on detail endpoint

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API / web-buyer
- **Source:** W21 / 13.7, 13.8 (browser-confirmed)
- **Issue:** The buyer invoice list endpoint (`GET /buyer/invoices`) returns invoice summaries with `lineItems: []` for all invoices. To see which products were invoiced, a buyer must call the detail endpoint (`GET /buyer/invoices/:id`) for each invoice. This is not a page size issue — the list returns the field as an empty array, not a paginated sub-resource. Buyer portals that render line-item previews in the list view (e.g., "2× Multigrain Bread, 1× Protein Bars…") will always show empty.
- **Expected:** The buyer invoice list should either include truncated line-item previews (e.g., first 3 items with a count) or the UI should transparently call the detail endpoint when a line-item summary is needed. Either way, buyers should see what products are on each invoice.
- **Actual:** `GET /buyer/invoices` returns `lineItems: []` for every invoice. The buyer cannot see any product details from the invoice list view — they must open each invoice individually to see what was invoiced.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a buyer with invoices that have line items.
  2. Call `GET /buyer/invoices` — all invoices have `lineItems: []`.
  3. Call `GET /buyer/invoices/:id` — the detail endpoint correctly returns line items.
- **Fix:** Include line items in the list response (possibly truncated to first 3 with a count), or document the two-endpoint pattern explicitly so the UI calls the detail endpoint when line items are needed.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting `GET /buyer/invoices` returns non-empty `lineItems` for invoices that have line items. Document whether the list or detail endpoint is the source of truth for line items in the buyer API reference.

---

### RF-155 — Invoice API schema inconsistencies: wrong field names in `POST /invoices`, `PATCH /invoices/:id/status` returns 404

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** API / developer-experience
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W21 / 13.8
- **Issues:**
  1. `POST /invoices` expects `items[].qty` but the Swagger/frontend sends `lineItems[].quantity` — response is a validation error: "property lineItems should not exist", "items.0.property quantity should not exist". The correct field names are not documented in-app.
  2. `PATCH /invoices/:id/status` returns HTTP 404 (no such route). The correct invoice status transition endpoint is `POST /invoices/:id/send` (for SENT) and `POST /invoices/:id/void` (for VOID). This is inconsistent with `PATCH /orders/:id/status` which correctly handles order status changes.
- **Expected:** Invoice creation should use consistent field names (`lineItems[].quantity` matching the orders API pattern) and status transitions should follow the same `PATCH /:id/status` pattern used by orders.
- **Actual:** `POST /invoices` requires `items[].qty` (not `lineItems[].quantity`), causing validation errors for any client using the orders field naming convention. `PATCH /invoices/:id/status` returns 404 — the endpoint does not exist.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `POST /invoices` with `lineItems: [{productId: "...", quantity: 2}]` — receive 400 validation error.
  2. Send `PATCH /invoices/:id/status` with `{"status":"SENT"}` — receive 404.
- **Fix:** Standardize field names across all resource creation endpoints (`items[].qty` vs `lineItems[].quantity`). Either implement `PATCH /invoices/:id/status` (following the orders pattern) or update the Swagger docs to document the send/void endpoints explicitly.
- **Prevention:** Add an API schema consistency check to CI that compares field names across equivalent resource endpoints (orders vs invoices vs returns). Add integration tests for invoice creation and status transitions using the documented field names.

---

### RF-156 — Possible cross-tenant invoice data leak: `affa` tenant invoices visible in `ux-audit` tenant filter

- **Severity:** ~~P0~~ CLEARED (prior data contamination — not an isolation failure)
- **Domain:** auth-security / data-integrity
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W21 / Scenario 13.8 observation
- **Issue:** While testing the Sent invoice filter for tenant `ux-audit-1777265477001`, the operator's invoice list contained invoices from "Blue Dreamz" (a product associated with the `affa` tenant — product name "Bam Bam AAA Flower"). These records should not be visible to any user of the `ux-audit` tenant. If confirmed, this is a hard tenant isolation failure allowing operators of one tenant to read financial records of another tenant.
- **Caveat:** The `affa` tenant data may have been inadvertently seeded into the `ux-audit-1777265477001` test environment through prior QA operations that violated the "no affa data" rule. However, the more dangerous interpretation — that `forTenant()` scoping is failing on the invoice list query — must be ruled out.
- **Verification steps:** (1) Log in as `ux_admin` on `ux-audit-1777265477001`, call `GET /api/v1/invoices?status=SENT` and check whether any `tenantId` values in the response differ from `8ee7bbf5-991b-41b1-adcb-4a6c20981401`. (2) Search for "Blue Dreamz" or "Bam Bam" customers under `ux-audit` tenant — if they exist, it is prior data contamination, not a leak; if not, it is a P0 tenant isolation failure.
- **Resolution:** Verified by W26 — the "Blue Dreamz" invoices belong to a customer that was seeded into the `ux-audit` tenant during prior QA operations that violated the "no affa data" rule. All `tenantId` values in the invoice list response match `8ee7bbf5-991b-41b1-adcb-4a6c20981401`. The `forTenant()` scope is working correctly. No real isolation failure exists.
- **Expected:** Operators should only see invoices belonging to their own tenant in all filtered views.
- **Actual (at time of finding):** Invoices with `affa` tenant product names appeared in the `ux-audit` filtered view — caused by data contamination, not code defect.
- **Repro:** N/A — finding cleared. The contaminated data explains the observation.
- **Fix (if confirmed):** Audit the `invoices.service.ts` `findAll()` query to ensure `forTenant()` scope is applied unconditionally, including when status filters are applied.
- **Prevention:** Enforce strict QA data isolation rules: never seed data from one tenant's credentials into a different tenant's environment. Add a post-QA-run cleanup script that verifies all seeded records belong to the correct `tenantId`. Add a data contamination check to the QA environment setup that asserts no cross-tenant `tenantId` values exist.

## Phase 7 Findings (RF-157–RF-166)

_Source: W27 (Upload Fuzzing & API Abuse — 2026-04-30)_
_Environment: `https://routeflowapi-production.up.railway.app/api/v1` | Tenant: `ux-audit-1777265477001`_

### RF-157 — Stored XSS via SVG upload: product images and customer tax-documents

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** security / upload
- **Surface:** API / web-operator / web-buyer
- **Source:** W27 / F19
- **Issue:** `POST /products/:id/images` and `POST /customers/:id/tax-documents` accept SVG files containing embedded `<script>` tags and serve them back with `Content-Type: image/svg+xml`. Browsers execute JavaScript from inline `image/svg+xml` URLs. Any authenticated operator or buyer can upload a malicious SVG, then share the stored URL to execute arbitrary JavaScript in any other authenticated user's browser session. Product images are especially high-risk because they render across both the operator dashboard and the buyer shop.
- **Evidence:** SVG payload `<script>window._xss_w27=1</script>` uploaded to product `f31568b9-99bd-4a86-ae76-85f2b7da7704` and customer `e1f685de-9e84-4215-8c11-44a2a0304cae` returned HTTP 201 and was served back as `image/svg+xml` with the script tag intact. Three live XSS payloads currently stored in the `ux-audit` tenant require cleanup.
- **Expected:** SVG files should be rejected at the upload validation stage on all endpoints. Only safe raster image formats (JPEG, PNG, WEBP) should be accepted as product images or customer documents.
- **Actual:** SVG files with embedded `<script>` tags are accepted, stored, and served inline as `image/svg+xml`. Any authenticated user who navigates to the product catalog or customer detail has the malicious script executed in their browser, enabling session token theft across all user roles.
- **Repro:** `POST /api/v1/products/:id/images` multipart with a `.svg` file containing `<script>alert(1)</script>` → HTTP 201 → fetch the returned URL → browser executes the script.
- **Fix:** (1) Block SVG uploads at the MIME type / extension level on all upload endpoints. (2) If SVG is a business requirement, sanitize using DOMPurify server-side before storage. (3) Add `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` response headers to the `/uploads/*` static serving path. (4) **Immediate:** delete the three stored SVG XSS payloads from the ux-audit tenant before any further testing.
- **Prevention:** Add a MIME type allowlist constant (`["image/jpeg","image/png","image/webp"]`) used by all upload endpoints. Add an upload security test asserting SVG uploads return 400 on all affected endpoints. Add `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` as a default header on the uploads serving path.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

---

### RF-158 — Negative product price accepted when submitted as string decimal

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / API
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / API Abuse — Negative Numerics
- **Issue:** `PATCH /products/:id` with `{"pricePerUnit": -999}` (number) is correctly rejected with HTTP 400. However, `{"pricePerUnit": "-1.00"}` (string) returns HTTP 200 and stores the negative price in the database. The `@IsDecimalString()` decorator validates format but not minimum value. A negative price propagates to order totals and invoices, producing negative invoice amounts.
- **Evidence:** `PATCH /products/:id {"pricePerUnit":"-1.00"}` → HTTP 200. Subsequent `GET /products/:id` confirms `pricePerUnit: "-1.00"`. Order created with that product had a negative total.
- **Expected:** `PATCH /products/:id` with any negative price value (whether numeric or string) should return HTTP 400. All product prices must be non-negative.
- **Actual:** String decimal values like `"-1.00"` bypass the `@Min(0)` validation (which only applies to numeric types) and are accepted, stored, and propagated to order totals, producing negative invoice amounts.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `PATCH /products/:id` with `{"pricePerUnit": "-1.00"}` (string) — receive HTTP 200.
  2. Create an order with that product — the order total is negative.
  3. Generate an invoice from the order — the invoice shows a negative amount.
- **Fix:** Add `@Min(0)` or a custom validator on the `pricePerUnit` field that enforces `parseFloat(value) >= 0` for string decimal inputs. Alternatively, parse the value server-side and validate the numeric range before saving.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for product price validation with string decimal negative values asserting HTTP 400. Add a shared `@IsPositiveDecimalString()` custom decorator that validates both format and minimum value, and use it on all price fields in all DTOs.

---

### RF-159 — `DELETE /customers/:id` permanently destroys the record with no confirmation and no soft-delete

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** data-integrity / security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / HTTP Method Confusion
- **Issue:** A single authenticated `DELETE /customers/:id` call permanently removes the customer record from the database with no confirmation parameter required, no soft-delete flag, and no audit log entry. The endpoint is not guarded against CSRF or accidental UI invocation. Any operator with valid credentials can silently destroy a customer's entire order history, invoice records, and contact data in one request. Confirmed: customer `dff6f7ac-2381-4322-80d8-43ba89e996a9` was permanently deleted during testing.
- **Expected:** Deleting a customer with financial history should be blocked with a 409 error. All deletion actions should require explicit confirmation and produce an audit log entry. A soft-delete (archive) should be the default, with hard delete restricted to super-admins.
- **Actual:** A single `DELETE /customers/:id` API call permanently and silently destroys the customer and all associated records with no safeguard, no confirmation, and no audit log entry.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as an operator.
  2. Send `DELETE /customers/:id` with no additional parameters.
  3. Receive HTTP 200 — the customer, all orders, and all invoices are permanently destroyed with no warning or audit entry.
- **Fix:** (1) Implement soft-delete by adding an `isDeleted: Boolean @default(false)` flag and filtering deleted records from all queries rather than hard-deleting. (2) Require a confirmation query parameter (e.g., `?confirm=true`) and/or a separate `POST /customers/:id/archive` action. (3) Write a deletion audit log entry before performing any destructive operation. (4) Block deletion if the customer has open orders or unpaid invoices.
- **Prevention:** Add a pre-delete guard checking for financial history returning 409 if found. Implement soft-delete as the default deletion mechanism across all tenant-scoped resources. Add an integration test asserting `DELETE /customers/:id` returns 409 for customers with financial history.

---

### RF-160 — No rate limiting on `POST /auth/login` — brute-force is unrestricted

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / Rate Limit Check
- **Issue:** Sending 15 rapid `POST /auth/login` requests with an incorrect password all return HTTP 401 with no throttling, lockout, or CAPTCHA challenge. An attacker can attempt unlimited password guesses against any known username. Combined with the fact that usernames follow a predictable pattern (`ux_admin`, `ux_driver_a`, etc.), credential brute-force is fully viable.
- **Note:** The API uses Redis for Socket.IO pub/sub. Redis is already in the stack and Throttler/rate-limit middleware for NestJS could be added with minimal effort.
- **Expected:** After 5 failed login attempts from the same IP within 15 minutes, the login endpoint should return `429 Too Many Requests` and require a waiting period or CAPTCHA before further attempts.
- **Actual:** 15+ rapid failed login attempts all return HTTP 401 with no throttling. Credential brute-force attacks are completely unrestricted on the production API.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send 15 rapid `POST /auth/login` requests with a known username and incorrect passwords.
  2. All 15 return HTTP 401 immediately — no throttling, no lockout, no delay.
- **Fix:** Apply NestJS `@nestjs/throttler` with a Redis store to `POST /auth/login`, limiting to (e.g.) 5 failed attempts per IP per 15 minutes, with exponential back-off. After N failures, require email-based OTP or CAPTCHA. Log all failed login attempts with IP and username for alerting.
- **Prevention:** Add rate limiting to the login endpoint as part of the security baseline and verify it in a CI integration test. Add failed login alerting (e.g., send an email alert when 10+ failures occur in 5 minutes for the same username). Test rate limit behavior in the security smoke test suite.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 2**

---

### RF-161 — Unhandled 500 when non-image bytes are uploaded with `image/*` MIME type (`sharp` crash)

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** API / upload
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / F18b, F19 (expense endpoint)
- **Issue:** Uploading a PDF file labeled as `image/jpeg`, or an SVG labeled as `image/jpeg`, causes `sharp` (the image processing library) to throw an unhandled exception, resulting in HTTP 500 "Internal server error" being returned to the caller. No stack trace is leaked in the response body, but the server-side error is unhandled and may leave temporary files or memory buffers unreleased.
- **Expected:** Uploading non-image bytes with an `image/*` MIME type should return HTTP 400 "The uploaded file is not a valid image" — a user-friendly error — rather than crashing with a 500.
- **Actual:** Uploading PDF bytes with `Content-Type: image/jpeg` causes the `sharp` library to throw an unhandled exception and the server returns HTTP 500. Temp files may be left unreleased.
- **Repro:** `POST /bookkeeping/expenses/:id/receipt` with PDF bytes and `Content-Type: image/jpeg` → HTTP 500.
- **Fix:** Wrap all `sharp` processing calls in a try/catch. On failure, return HTTP 400 "The uploaded file is not a valid image." Clean up any temp file before throwing. Add magic-byte validation before passing files to `sharp` (read first 4 bytes and check against known image signatures).
- **Prevention:** Add a global exception filter for `sharp` errors that returns 400 with a user-friendly message. Add an upload test that submits PDF bytes as `image/jpeg` and asserts HTTP 400 (not 500). Add a temp file cleanup step in all upload error paths.

---

### RF-162 — Zero-byte files silently accepted on all upload endpoints

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** API / upload
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / F16
- **Issue:** Uploading a 0-byte file to any upload endpoint (`/expenses/:id/receipt`, `/products/:id/images`, `/customers/:id/tax-documents`) returns HTTP 201 with a stored URL. The stored URL serves an empty body (200 OK, 0 bytes). Operators viewing these "attachments" see nothing; a blank receipt or product image is stored as if it were valid content.
- **Expected:** Uploading a 0-byte file should return HTTP 400 "The uploaded file is empty" before any storage or processing occurs.
- **Actual:** 0-byte files are accepted and stored with HTTP 201. The stored URL returns an empty response body. A receipt or product image stored this way is invisible when opened, silently corrupting the attachment record.
- **Repro:**
  1. Upload a 0-byte file to `POST /products/:id/images`.
  2. Receive HTTP 201 with a stored URL.
  3. Fetch the stored URL — it returns 200 OK with an empty body (0 bytes).
- **Fix:** Reject files with `size === 0` before calling any storage or processing code. Return HTTP 400 "The uploaded file is empty."
- **Prevention:** Add file size validation as the first step in the upload pipeline, before any MIME or content checks. Add an upload test asserting 0-byte files return 400 on all upload endpoints. Add `minSize: 1` to the Multer configuration globally.

---

### RF-163 — No maximum-length validation on `CreateCustomerDto` string fields

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** API / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / Oversized String Fields
- **Issue:** `POST /customers` with a `businessName` containing 10,001 characters returns HTTP 201 and stores the full string. The same applies to `contactName`, `email`, `phone`, and `notes` fields. No `@MaxLength()` decorator is applied. Arbitrarily long strings can bloat the database, break UI rendering (overflowing labels, overflowing PDF columns), and cause index performance degradation.
- **Evidence:** Customer `9e7d17d3-b5b4-4d76-ae9f-b691c469ca3b` created with a 10,001-character `businessName` (stored successfully).
- **Expected:** String fields like `businessName` should be validated to a maximum length (e.g., 255 characters) and return HTTP 400 if exceeded, preventing database bloat and UI rendering issues.
- **Actual:** A 10,001-character `businessName` is accepted and stored with HTTP 201. UI components and PDF renderers that attempt to display this value will overflow or truncate unpredictably.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `POST /customers` with `{"businessName": "A".repeat(10001)}`.
  2. Receive HTTP 201 — the full 10,001-character string is stored.
  3. View the customer in the UI — the label overflows or truncates with no user-visible error.
- **Fix:** Add `@MaxLength(255)` (or appropriate business limits) to all string fields in `CreateCustomerDto`, `UpdateCustomerDto`, and equivalent DTOs for products, orders, and invoices.
- **Prevention:** Add a `@MaxLength` decorator audit to all DTO PR reviews. Add a validation test asserting that `businessName` fields exceeding 255 characters return 400. Apply max-length validation globally to all string DTO fields using a shared base validator.

---

### RF-164 — No maximum order quantity cap — qty 999,999 accepted, creating a $25.6M order

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / Negative Numerics
- **Issue:** `POST /orders` with `items[0].qty = 999999` returns HTTP 201 and creates an order with a total of $25,664,510.89. There is no server-side cap on line-item quantity. A buyer or operator making a data-entry error (or a malicious actor) can create orders with financially absurd totals that distort AR aging reports, payment records, and analytics.
- **Evidence:** Order `6af05a85-7fb3-479b-ae6f-53fc6bff2a0e` with total $25,664,510.89 persists in the ux-audit tenant. (Also note: an anomalous order `ORD-1777431385834` with total $25,664,510.89 was observed by W28, created during the W9 concurrent test — same root cause.)
- **Expected:** Line-item quantity should be capped at a sensible business limit (e.g., 9,999 units) and orders exceeding a total value cap (e.g., $1,000,000) should require explicit operator override.
- **Actual:** A quantity of 999,999 is accepted and persists as a $25.6M order with no validation error, distorting all AR aging, analytics, and payment records.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `POST /orders` with `{"items":[{"productId":"...","qty":999999}]}`.
  2. Receive HTTP 201 with a $25,664,510.89 total.
  3. The order appears in AR aging and analytics, inflating all aggregate totals.
- **Fix:** Add `@Max(9999)` (or an appropriate business limit) to `qty` in `CreateOrderItemDto`. Consider also adding a per-order total cap (e.g., reject orders > $1,000,000) with a clear error message.
- **Prevention:** Add a validation test asserting `qty > 9999` returns 400. Add a per-order total cap validation asserting orders over $1,000,000 return 422 without an explicit override flag. Add an anomaly detection alert for orders that exceed 10× the customer's average order value.

---

### RF-165 — No rate limiting on authenticated API endpoints

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security / performance
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / Rate Limit Check
- **Issue:** 35 rapid `GET /products` requests all returned HTTP 200 with no throttling. Authenticated endpoints are not rate-limited, leaving the API open to scraping, enumeration, and denial-of-service via high-volume reads. An authenticated buyer could enumerate the entire product catalog (including cost prices if exposed), and an operator could flood analytics endpoints.
- **Expected:** Authenticated API endpoints should apply per-user rate limits (e.g., 300 requests/minute for read endpoints, 60/minute for mutations) and return HTTP 429 with a `Retry-After` header when exceeded.
- **Actual:** 35 rapid authenticated requests all return HTTP 200 — no 429, no delay, no throttle. The API can be scraped at unlimited speed by any authenticated user.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate and send 35 rapid `GET /products` requests in a loop.
  2. All 35 return HTTP 200 — no throttling of any kind.
- **Fix:** Apply `@nestjs/throttler` globally with a Redis store, with separate rate-limit buckets for read endpoints (e.g., 300 req/min) and write/mutation endpoints (e.g., 60 req/min). Unauthenticated endpoints should have a tighter limit.
- **Prevention:** Add rate limiting as a global NestJS guard and verify it in a CI integration test asserting 429 is returned after the configured threshold. Document rate limits in the API reference and surface them in 429 error messages.

---

### RF-166 — No file content validation for PDF uploads — corrupt bytes silently stored

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** API / upload
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W27 / F17
- **Issue:** Uploading 50 random bytes as `corrupt.pdf` with `Content-Type: application/pdf` to the expense receipt endpoint returns HTTP 201. The corrupt data is stored and served back as `application/pdf`. The PDF viewer in the expense detail will fail silently or show an error when the attachment is opened. No magic-byte validation is performed on PDF uploads.
- **Expected:** Uploading corrupt or non-PDF bytes with `Content-Type: application/pdf` should return HTTP 400 "The uploaded file is not a valid PDF", verified by checking the file's magic bytes against the PDF signature.
- **Actual:** 50 random bytes submitted as a PDF are stored with HTTP 201 and the stored URL serves back the corrupt bytes as `application/pdf`. The expense PDF viewer will fail silently when the attachment is opened.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `POST /bookkeeping/expenses/:id/receipt` with 50 random bytes and `Content-Type: application/pdf`.
  2. Receive HTTP 201 with a stored URL.
  3. Fetch the URL — it returns the corrupt bytes as `application/pdf`.
- **Fix:** Read the first 5 bytes of any `application/pdf` upload and verify they match the PDF magic number (`%PDF-`). Reject with HTTP 400 if the signature does not match. Apply equivalent magic-byte checks to all file type categories accepted by upload endpoints.
- **Prevention:** Create a shared `validateFileMagicBytes(buffer, expectedSignature)` utility used by all upload handlers. Add an upload test asserting corrupt PDF bytes return 400. Add magic-byte checks to the file upload security audit checklist.

## Phase 8 Findings (RF-167–RF-170)

_Source: W25 (Driver Dispatch and Real-Time QA — 2026-04-30)_
_Environment: `https://routeflowmobile-production.up.railway.app` | API: `https://routeflowapi-production.up.railway.app/api/v1`_

### RF-167 — `GET /route-runs/my-runs` returns 404 for all drivers regardless of assignment state

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** driver-pod / dispatch
- **Surface:** API / mobile-driver
- **Source:** W25 / Test 13.3, 13.12
- **Issue:** `GET /route-runs/my-runs` returns HTTP 404 `{"message":"Route run not found"}` for every driver, regardless of whether runs are assigned to them or what status those runs are in. This is the primary endpoint the driver app uses to fetch its active route. Driver A (userId `4db3d83b`, driverProfileId `d79a6f4a`) had two assigned runs (one SCHEDULED, one IN_PROGRESS); both were confirmed via direct `GET /route-runs/:id`, yet `GET /route-runs/my-runs` still returned 404.
- **Note:** This is distinct from RF-001 (tenantId null on run creation). Even with tenantId correctly set on existing runs, the `my-runs` endpoint is broken. The driver route page in the browser (`/route`) displays the route correctly, suggesting the web app uses a different endpoint or falls back to a direct ID lookup.
- **Expected:** `GET /route-runs/my-runs` should return all runs assigned to the authenticated driver that are in SCHEDULED or IN_PROGRESS status, with HTTP 200.
- **Actual:** The endpoint returns HTTP 404 for all drivers, regardless of assigned run state. The driver mobile app cannot fetch its active route via this endpoint.
- **Repro:** Authenticate as `ux_driver_a`. `GET /api/v1/route-runs/my-runs` → HTTP 404. Confirm driver has assigned runs via operator `GET /api/v1/route-runs` → both runs with `driverId` matching the driver exist and are SCHEDULED/IN_PROGRESS.
- **Fix:** Audit the `route-runs` controller for route ordering — `@Get("my-runs")` may be declared after `@Get(":id")`, causing the router to treat `my-runs` as an ID parameter (this is the same class of bug as the `my-stats` issue fixed in MEMORY.md). Move `@Get("my-runs")` before `@Get(":id")` in the controller.
- **Prevention:** Add a CI integration test that authenticates as a driver with an assigned run and asserts `GET /route-runs/my-runs` returns HTTP 200 with the expected run. Add static route ordering linting to flag `@Get("my-runs")` declarations that appear after parameterized routes.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 2**

---

### RF-168 — Stop completion does not consistently update CONFIRMED orders to DELIVERED

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** driver-pod / dispatch / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W25 / Test 13.11
- **Issue:** After cancelling route run `140784f6`, it was found that Stop 1's CONFIRMED orders (`b3565cc1`, `514b08ee`) were never transitioned to DELIVERED despite the stop having been marked COMPLETED before cancellation. Stop 2's and Stop 3's orders (which were DELIVERED or OUT_FOR_DELIVERY) were correctly preserved. The root cause identified is that older runs lack "delivery mutation records" — internal records that drive the order status transition logic. Runs created via newer code paths update correctly; legacy or older runs do not.
- **Implication:** Delivered orders may remain stuck in CONFIRMED status indefinitely, causing operators to see phantom CONFIRMED orders that were actually delivered. AR aging and route reconciliation become unreliable.
- **Expected:** Marking a stop as COMPLETED should unconditionally transition all orders on that stop from CONFIRMED to DELIVERED, regardless of whether the run was created via an old or new code path.
- **Actual:** Orders on stops from older runs remain stuck in CONFIRMED status even after the stop is marked COMPLETED. These phantom CONFIRMED orders distort the operator's active order count and AR aging.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a route run via an older code path (without delivery mutation records).
  2. Mark a stop as COMPLETED via `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete`.
  3. Inspect the orders on that stop — they remain in CONFIRMED status rather than transitioning to DELIVERED.
- **Fix:** Audit `routes.service.ts` `completeStop()` to ensure it unconditionally writes the order status transition to DELIVERED for all orders on the stop, regardless of whether a "delivery mutation record" exists. Remove the dependency on pre-existing mutation records.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test that completes a stop and asserts all orders transition to DELIVERED unconditionally. Eliminate the dependency on "delivery mutation records" as a prerequisite for order status transitions.

---

### RF-169 — `PATCH /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId` returns HTTP 500 Internal Server Error

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod / API
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W25 / Test 13.5
- **Issue:** `PATCH /route-runs/{runId}/stops/{stopId}` with `{"status":"DELIVERED"}` returns HTTP 500 `{"statusCode":500,"message":"Internal server error"}`. The correct stop completion endpoint is `POST /route-runs/{runId}/stops/{stopId}/complete`, which works correctly. The PATCH route crashes — likely due to a missing handler implementation or an unhandled exception in the route-run stop update path.
- **Expected:** `PATCH /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId` should either update the stop correctly and return 200, or return 405 "Method Not Allowed" if PATCH is intentionally unsupported for this resource.
- **Actual:** The endpoint returns HTTP 500 "Internal server error" for any valid PATCH request, indicating an unhandled exception in the route-run stop update path.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `PATCH /route-runs/{runId}/stops/{stopId}` with `{"status":"DELIVERED"}` and valid run/stop IDs.
  2. Receive HTTP 500 — the server crashes on this input.
- **Fix:** Either implement the PATCH handler to correctly update stop status, or return HTTP 405 "Method Not Allowed" if PATCH is intentionally unsupported. Never return HTTP 500 for predictable user input.
- **Prevention:** Add a test asserting `PATCH /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId` returns either 200 or 405 — never 500. Add a global NestJS exception filter that catches unhandled errors in route handlers and returns 500 with a safe message while logging the stack trace server-side.

---

### RF-170 — No cash payment collection API endpoint exists for route run stops

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod / finance-pricing
- **Surface:** API / mobile-driver
- **Source:** W25 / Test 13.6
- **Issue:** The driver app displays a "Cash" tab in the bottom navigation for collecting cash payments at stops. However, no API endpoint for recording a cash payment at a route-run stop was discoverable. All attempted paths returned 404: `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/payment`, `/collect-payment`, `/cash`, `POST /payments`, and `POST /route-runs/:runId/cash-collection`. Cash collection is a core driver workflow — drivers who collect cash have no way to record it, making end-of-day cash reconciliation impossible via the app.
- **Expected:** Drivers should be able to record cash, card, or cheque payments collected at a stop via `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/payment`, with the payment recorded against the stop's invoice and visible immediately in the operator's finance dashboard.
- **Actual:** No payment collection endpoint exists for route run stops. The "Cash" tab in the driver app has no functional backend. All attempted payment endpoint paths return 404.
- **Repro:**
  1. As a driver, complete a delivery stop.
  2. Attempt to record a cash payment via `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/payment`.
  3. Receive HTTP 404 — the endpoint does not exist.
- **Fix:** Implement `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/payment` with body `{amount, method: "CASH"|"CARD"|"CHEQUE", notes?, geolocation?}`. Record the payment against the stop's associated invoice(s) and trigger the invoice status update. Emit a Socket.IO event so the operator's finance dashboard updates in real time.
- **Prevention:** Add cash payment collection to the driver app release acceptance criteria. Add an integration test for the payment endpoint asserting the payment is recorded and the invoice status updates. Block the "Cash" tab from shipping until the backend endpoint exists.

## Phase 9 Findings (RF-171–RF-175)

_Source: W28 (Phase 9 Corners: Search/Sort, Clock, Bulk Ops, Multi-Address, Driver — 2026-04-30)_
_Environment: `https://routeflowapi-production.up.railway.app/api/v1` | Tenant: `ux-audit-1777265477001`_

### RF-171 — Server-side sort completely unsupported on `/orders` — `sortBy` param rejected with 400

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** API / operator-ux
- **Surface:** API / web-operator
- **Source:** W28 / 9.H
- **Issue:** `GET /orders?sortBy=createdAt&order=asc` returns HTTP 400 `{"message":"property sortBy should not exist"}`. The `sortBy` and `order` query parameters are whitelisted out of the orders DTO via strict validation. No server-side sorting is possible on the `/orders` endpoint. Operators cannot sort their order list by Total, Date, Status, or Customer name — any sort UI is client-side only and limited to the current page of results.
- **Also affected:** `GET /orders?format=csv` returns 400 (no export support); `GET /orders?status=PENDING&sortBy=total&order=desc` fails due to `sortBy` rejection regardless of other valid params.
- **Expected:** `GET /orders?sortBy=createdAt&order=desc` should return orders sorted by creation date, descending. The endpoint should support at least `createdAt`, `total`, and `status` as sort fields.
- **Actual:** Any `sortBy` or `order` parameter is rejected with HTTP 400, making server-side sort impossible. Operator order list sort is client-side only, limited to the current page.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `GET /orders?sortBy=createdAt&order=asc` — receive HTTP 400 `{"message":"property sortBy should not exist"}`.
  2. Send `GET /orders?status=PENDING&sortBy=total&order=desc` — also returns 400.
- **Fix:** Add optional `sortBy` and `order` query parameters to `GetOrdersDto` with an allowlist of sortable columns (`createdAt`, `total`, `status`, `customerName`). Apply `.orderBy({ [sortBy]: order })` in the Prisma query. Validate that `sortBy` is one of the allowed fields to prevent injection.
- **Prevention:** Add a sort-parameter integration test for the orders endpoint asserting `sortBy=createdAt&order=desc` returns HTTP 200 with results in the correct order. Add server-side sort support as a standard feature for all list endpoints (orders, invoices, customers, products).

---

### RF-172 — `POST /orders` upserts existing PENDING order instead of creating a new one

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** order-management / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W28 / 9.L (BUG-2); also observed in W21/13.1 (buyer portal) and W23
- **Issue:** When `POST /orders` is called for a customer that already has a PENDING order, the API returns the existing order's ID and updates that order rather than creating a new one. Ten consecutive `POST /orders` calls for the same customer all returned the same order ID (`d204cea4-6b6f-4abf-ac46-51d3c4956da6`). Only one order was created. This is caused by a `mergeAllPendingForCustomer()` mechanism (also referenced as RF-148 in the buyer portal context). Operators cannot create multiple simultaneous orders for the same customer if one is already PENDING.
- **Business impact:** An operator taking a second phone order from a customer while the first is still PENDING will silently overwrite the first order's line items. Items from the first order are lost with no warning.
- **Expected:** `POST /orders` should always create a new order, returning a new unique order ID, even if the customer has existing PENDING orders.
- **Actual:** Every `POST /orders` call for a customer with a PENDING order returns the same existing order ID and merges the new items into it. Ten consecutive calls produced only one order.
- **Repro:**
  1. Ensure a customer has a PENDING order.
  2. Send `POST /orders` for that customer with different items.
  3. Observe the response contains the same order ID as the existing PENDING order — no new order is created.
- **Fix:** Remove or make optional the `mergeAllPendingForCustomer()` upsert behavior on the operator-side order creation path. Upsert behavior may be intentional for the buyer portal self-service flow but should not apply to operator-created orders. Add a `forceNew: true` flag or simply create a new order unconditionally when the request comes from the operator.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that two consecutive `POST /orders` calls for the same customer return two different order IDs. Document the merge behavior explicitly in the API reference and require opt-in (`merge: true`) rather than opt-out.

---

### RF-173 — Invalid dates (e.g., Feb 29 in a non-leap year) accepted with HTTP 201 and stored as `null`

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** API / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W28 / 9.K
- **Issue:** `POST /orders {"requestedDeliveryDate": "2026-02-29"}` returns HTTP 201 with `requestedDeliveryDate: null` in the response. Feb 29, 2026 does not exist (2026 is not a leap year). The date parser silently coerces the invalid date to `null` instead of returning HTTP 400 with a validation error. Operators who mistype a delivery date receive no feedback that their date was rejected.
- **Also observed:** `POST /orders {"requestedDeliveryDate": "2026-04-30T23:30:00Z"}` also stores as `null` (a separate time-zone parsing issue: the date parser may be stripping the time component and coercing to null on invalid parse).
- **Expected:** Submitting an invalid date string (e.g., `2026-02-29` in a non-leap year) should return HTTP 400 "Invalid date: '2026-02-29'" with a clear validation error. Valid dates should be stored correctly.
- **Actual:** Invalid dates are silently coerced to `null` and stored without any error response. The operator receives HTTP 201 and believes the date was set, but it is actually null in the database.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send `POST /orders` with `{"requestedDeliveryDate": "2026-02-29"}`.
  2. Receive HTTP 201 with `requestedDeliveryDate: null` in the response.
  3. The operator order detail shows no delivery date, with no indication the submitted date was invalid.
- **Fix:** Validate all date inputs server-side using `new Date(value)` and check `isNaN(result.getTime())`. Return HTTP 400 "Invalid date: 'XXXX-XX-XX'" if the date cannot be parsed. Apply to all date fields in all DTOs.
- **Prevention:** Add a custom `@IsValidDate()` class-validator decorator that rejects non-existent calendar dates. Add validation tests for common invalid date strings (`2026-02-29`, `2026-13-01`, `not-a-date`). Apply date validation consistently across all order, invoice, and expense DTOs.

---

### RF-174 — `GET /invoices?status=OVERDUE` returns 0 results — UI "Overdue" tab completely broken

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / operator-ux
- **Surface:** API / web-operator
- **Source:** W28 / 9.K
- **Issue:** "OVERDUE" is not a valid value in the invoice status enum. The correct way to query overdue invoices is `GET /invoices?isOverdue=true`, which correctly returns overdue invoices (3 confirmed during testing). However, the operator's invoice list "Overdue" tab fires `GET /invoices?status=OVERDUE`, which always returns an empty array. The Overdue filter is therefore completely non-functional — it always shows "No invoices." The operator dashboard correctly displays the overdue count (using the `isOverdue` computed field), creating a confusing discrepancy where the dashboard says "3 Overdue" but clicking through to the Overdue tab shows nothing.
- **Note:** `dueDate = today` is treated as already overdue (`isOverdue: true`) with no grace period, which may be intentional but should be confirmed with product.
- **Expected:** The Overdue tab in the invoice list should display all invoices where `isOverdue: true`, matching the count shown on the operator dashboard.
- **Actual:** The Overdue tab fires `GET /invoices?status=OVERDUE` which always returns 0 results (OVERDUE is not a valid status value). The operator sees "No invoices" while the dashboard simultaneously shows "3 Overdue."
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator — dashboard shows "3 Overdue" invoices.
  2. Navigate to Finance > Invoices > Overdue tab.
  3. Observe "No invoices" — the tab is always empty regardless of actual overdue invoices.
- **Fix:** (1) In the invoice list component, change the Overdue tab's API call from `?status=OVERDUE` to `?isOverdue=true`. (2) Alternatively, add "OVERDUE" as a valid status alias in the backend that maps to `WHERE isOverdue = true`. Option 1 is simpler and safer.
- **Prevention:** Add a UI integration test asserting the Overdue tab count matches the dashboard overdue count. Add an API test asserting `GET /invoices?isOverdue=true` returns the same set as the dashboard overdue metric.

---

### RF-175 — Customer addresses always stored as `addressType=BILLING` regardless of label

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** customer-management / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W28 / 9.M
- **Issue:** When creating a customer with a `addresses` array containing an entry labelled "Shipping" (or `label: "shipping"`), the stored record has `addressType: "BILLING"`. The address type is not inferred from the label field. There appears to be no way to create an address with `addressType: "SHIPPING"` through the current API. As a result, dispatch cannot distinguish between billing and shipping addresses on multi-address customers — all addresses are treated as billing, and the delivery stop may use the wrong address.
- **Expected:** An address submitted with `addressType: "SHIPPING"` should be stored with `addressType: "SHIPPING"`. Dispatch logic should use the SHIPPING address for route stop coordinates, not the BILLING address.
- **Actual:** All addresses are stored with `addressType: "BILLING"` regardless of the submitted `addressType` value. Drivers delivering to multi-address customers may be routed to the billing address instead of the delivery address.
- **Repro:** `POST /customers` with `{"addresses": [{"label": "Shipping", "street": "...", "addressType": "SHIPPING"}]}` → stored address has `addressType: "BILLING"`.
- **Fix:** Ensure `addressType` from the request body is respected and persisted. If `addressType` is not provided, infer from `label` (e.g., if label contains "ship" → SHIPPING, "bill" → BILLING, otherwise default to BILLING). Update `CreateCustomerAddressDto` to accept and validate the `addressType` enum field.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that an address submitted with `addressType: "SHIPPING"` is stored and returned with the correct type. Add address type handling to the route planning logic acceptance criteria to ensure shipping addresses are preferred over billing addresses for delivery stops.

## Phase 10 Findings (RF-177–RF-179)

_Source: W29 (Permission Matrix, E2E-5 Dispatch Corners, Console Audit — 2026-04-30)_
_Method: Chrome browser + browser JavaScript — all API calls via `fetch()` in browser JS console_

### RF-177 — Driver role can read `GET /returns` — HTTP 200 instead of 403

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** auth-security / returns
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W29 / Permission Matrix
- **Issue:** A driver-authenticated token receives HTTP 200 from `GET /returns` and receives actual return records (confirmed: returned 1 record with `id: bb19da5d`, reason: DAMAGED). All other operator-only endpoints (`GET /orders`, `GET /customers`, `GET /invoices`, `GET /users`, `GET /drivers`) correctly return 403 for drivers. The returns endpoint is missing its role guard.
- **Expected:** `GET /returns` should return HTTP 403 Forbidden for DRIVER-role tokens. Drivers have no business need to see the global returns list; they should only see return-related information via their route-run stop endpoint.
- **Actual:** DRIVER-role tokens receive HTTP 200 with actual return records from `GET /returns`. All other operator-only list endpoints correctly return 403 for drivers — the returns endpoint is the anomaly.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as a driver.
  2. Call `GET /returns` with the driver JWT.
  3. Observe HTTP 200 with return records (confirmed: 1 record with reason DAMAGED returned).
- **Fix:** Add `@Roles('OPERATOR', 'TENANT_ADMIN')` guard to the `GET /returns` controller handler in `returns.controller.ts`. Drivers should only see returns associated with their own stops via the route-run stop endpoint, not the global returns list.
- **Prevention:** Add `GET /returns` to the RBAC permission matrix test suite. Add a post-merge CI test that calls all financial list endpoints with a driver JWT and asserts all return 403. Review all endpoints in the returns controller for missing role guards.

---

### RF-178 — Out-of-order stop completion not enforced — any stop completable in any sequence

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** driver-pod / dispatch
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W29 / E2E-5; W28 / 9.O (GAP-3 confirmed)
- **Issue:** `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete` succeeds (HTTP 201) regardless of the stop's position in the route. Completing stop 4 before stops 1, 2, 3 is allowed. Completing stop 2 before stop 1 is allowed. No sequential enforcement exists. This allows drivers to record deliveries in any order, making route reconciliation inaccurate and allowing POD fabrication (mark a future stop as delivered before visiting the customer).
- **Expected:** Completing a stop should be rejected if any earlier stop in the route sequence has not yet been completed, returning HTTP 422 "Cannot complete stop N before stop N-1 is completed."
- **Actual:** Any stop can be marked as completed in any order regardless of sequence position. A driver can mark stop 4 as delivered before visiting stops 1, 2, or 3, enabling fabricated POD records.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a route run with 4 stops in sequence order.
  2. Call `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stop4Id/complete` (stop 4, skipping stops 1-3).
  3. Observe HTTP 201 — the out-of-sequence completion is accepted.
- **Fix:** In `routes.service.ts` `completeStop()`, check that all stops with a lower `sequence` (or `position`) value are already COMPLETED before allowing the current stop to be completed. Return HTTP 422 "Cannot complete stop N before stop N-1 is completed" if out-of-order.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that completing a stop out of sequence returns HTTP 422. Add route sequence enforcement as a mandatory invariant in the driver dispatch architecture documentation.

---

### RF-179 — Route run status does not auto-transition to `IN_PROGRESS` when stops are completed on a SCHEDULED run

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** driver-pod / dispatch
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W29 / E2E-5
- **Issue:** When stops are completed on a run that is still in SCHEDULED status (which is itself a separate bug — RF-003), the run's own status remains SCHEDULED indefinitely. It does not auto-transition to IN_PROGRESS. Operators viewing the fleet/dispatch dashboard see the run as SCHEDULED while stops are being completed, giving a false picture of driver activity. The correct behavior is: first stop completion should set run status to IN_PROGRESS; last stop completion should set it to COMPLETED.
- **Expected:** Completing the first stop on a SCHEDULED run should atomically transition the run to IN_PROGRESS. Completing the final stop should transition it to COMPLETED.
- **Actual:** The run status remains SCHEDULED even as stops are completed. Operators see a SCHEDULED run on the dispatch dashboard while deliveries are actively in progress, making real-time fleet visibility unreliable.
- **Repro:**
  1. Create a route run in SCHEDULED status.
  2. Complete the first stop via `POST /route-runs/:runId/stops/:stopId/complete`.
  3. Check the run status — it remains SCHEDULED rather than transitioning to IN_PROGRESS.
- **Fix:** In `routes.service.ts` `completeStop()`: (1) if the run is SCHEDULED when the first stop is completed, transition it to IN_PROGRESS; (2) if all stops are now COMPLETED/SKIPPED, transition the run to COMPLETED. These status transitions should be atomic with the stop completion in the same transaction.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting that completing the first stop of a SCHEDULED run returns the run in IN_PROGRESS status. Add another asserting all stops complete transitions the run to COMPLETED.

## Phase 11 Findings (RF-176, RF-180–RF-184)

_Source: W26 (E2E-3/4/6/7: Standing Orders, Recurring Invoices, Concurrent Edits, Auth — 2026-04-30)_
_Method: Chrome browser + browser JavaScript — all API calls via `fetch()` in browser JS console_

---

### RF-176 — Missing `X-Tenant-Slug` header on login issues a `tenantId: null` JWT that bypasses tenant isolation

- **Severity:** P0
- **Domain:** auth-security / tenant-isolation
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W26 / E2E-7 auth edge cases (browser JavaScript fetch)
- **Issue:** `POST /auth/login` called without the `X-Tenant-Slug` header but with valid `ux_admin` credentials returns HTTP 200 with a JWT carrying `{tenantId: null, tenantSlug: null, role: "TENANT_ADMIN", isAdmin: true}`. The `forTenant()` Prisma extension scopes all queries with `WHERE tenantId = ?`. A null tenantId in the WHERE clause matches all database rows that have `tenantId IS NULL` — which includes all records created before RF-001's tenantId backfill and any records created by other broken creation paths (RF-147, etc.). W26 used this null-tenant token to successfully enumerate 20 products, 20 customers, 20 orders, and 14 users belonging to two completely foreign tenants (`0e74ddf9`, `584e28dc`), none of which belong to the `ux-audit` tenant (`8ee7bbf5`).
- **Expected:** `POST /auth/login` without the `X-Tenant-Slug` header should return HTTP 400 "tenantCode is required." A JWT with `tenantId: null` should never be issued. The `forTenant()` scope should throw an exception if `tenantId` is null rather than generating a permissive WHERE clause.
- **Actual:** Login without a tenant slug issues a valid JWT with `tenantId: null`. Using this token, W26 enumerated data from two foreign tenants — a confirmed multi-tenant isolation failure.
- **Repro:** `POST /api/v1/auth/login` with valid credentials and NO `X-Tenant-Slug` header → HTTP 200 → token with `tenantId: null` → `GET /products`, `GET /customers` → returns foreign tenant data.
- **Fix:** (1) Make the tenant slug mandatory on login — return HTTP 400 "tenantCode is required" if the header/body field is missing or invalid. (2) Never issue a JWT with `tenantId: null`. (3) In `forTenant()`, if `tenantId` is null, throw an exception rather than generating a WHERE clause that matches all null rows. (4) Run a backfill to populate tenantId on all null rows that were legitimately created (RF-001 fix should have done this; verify it ran). (5) Add an integration test asserting that login without a tenant slug returns 400.
- **Prevention:** Add an integration test asserting login without `X-Tenant-Slug` returns 400. Add a guard in `forTenant()` that throws `InternalServerErrorException` if called with a null tenantId. Add a DB-level `NOT NULL` constraint on all tenant-scoped tables. Add a security test asserting no endpoint returns cross-tenant data when called with a null-tenantId token.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 1**

---

### RF-180 — `GET /buyer/standing-orders` returns 404 — standing orders buyer portal is completely broken

- **Severity:** P1
- **Domain:** order-management / buyer-portal
- **Surface:** API / mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W26 / E2E-3
- **Issue:** The buyer portal "Standing Orders" page (accessible via More → Standing Orders in the buyer app) calls `GET /buyer/standing-orders` which returns HTTP 404. The backend route does not exist. The standing orders page always shows "No standing orders." even for buyers who have active standing order templates. W26 confirmed: Buyer 4 (`ux_buyer4`) has a confirmed standing order template (orders with notes "Auto-generated from standing order: UX Weekly Protein & Almonds" exist in the database), but the buyer cannot see or manage their templates through the portal.
- **Note:** The correct operator-side endpoint appears to be `GET /recurring-invoices` (not `/invoices/recurring`), suggesting similar naming inconsistency may apply to the buyer route. The standing order template data is present — only the buyer-facing API route is missing.
- **Expected:** `GET /buyer/standing-orders` should return the authenticated buyer's active standing order templates with HTTP 200, allowing buyers to view, pause, and manage their recurring orders.
- **Actual:** The endpoint returns HTTP 404. The buyer standing orders page always shows "No standing orders" regardless of how many active templates exist for that buyer.
- **Repro:**
  1. Authenticate as Buyer 4 who has an active standing order template.
  2. Navigate to More > Standing Orders in the buyer portal.
  3. The page shows "No standing orders" — calls `GET /buyer/standing-orders` → 404.
- **Fix:** Implement `GET /buyer/standing-orders` (or `GET /buyer/order-templates`) that returns the authenticated buyer's standing order templates, scoped by `customerId`. Expose pause/skip/delete operations as well.
- **Prevention:** Add the buyer standing-orders endpoint to the buyer portal release acceptance criteria and smoke test suite. Ensure all buyer portal feature screens are connected to a working backend endpoint before being shown in the navigation.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 2**

---

### RF-181 — Concurrent writes to the same resource cause silent data loss — no optimistic concurrency control

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W26 / E2E-6
- **Issue:** `Promise.all([PATCH /products/:id {price:5.99}, PATCH /products/:id {price:7.99}])` — both concurrent PATCH requests return HTTP 200 with no conflict indication. The final stored price is non-deterministic (whichever write reached the database last). One client's write is silently discarded. No ETag, no `version` field, no `If-Match` header support, no last-write-wins indication in the response. This affects any resource that can be edited simultaneously by two operators (products, orders, customers, invoices).
- **Expected:** A PATCH request that conflicts with a concurrent write should return HTTP 409 Conflict with a message indicating the resource was modified since it was last fetched, so the client can fetch the latest version and retry.
- **Actual:** Both concurrent PATCH requests return HTTP 200. One write silently overwrites the other with no indication to either client. The final state is non-deterministic.
- **Repro:**
  1. Send two concurrent `PATCH /products/:id` requests with different `price` values using `Promise.all()`.
  2. Both return HTTP 200 — one write is silently lost.
  3. The final price reflects whichever write won the DB race.
- **Fix:** Implement optimistic concurrency control (OCC): (1) Add a `version: Int @default(1)` field to mutable resources. (2) Increment `version` on every write. (3) Require clients to send the current `version` in PUT/PATCH requests via an `If-Match` header or body field. (4) If the stored version doesn't match, return HTTP 409 Conflict. Alternatively, implement pessimistic locking via Redis for high-contention resources like product prices.
- **Prevention:** Add a concurrency integration test that sends two simultaneous PATCH requests and asserts one returns 409. Add OCC to all mutable resource models as part of the architecture standard. Add an ETag response header to all PATCH responses so clients can track the current version.

---

### RF-182 — No recurring invoice UI — correct endpoint is `/recurring-invoices` but no UI surface exposes it

- **Severity:** P2
- **Domain:** finance-pricing / operator-ux
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W26 / E2E-4
- **Issue:** The operator invoice list at `/invoices` has no Recurring tab and no "Create Recurring Invoice" button. Navigating directly to `/invoices/recurring` redirects to `/home`. The correct backend API endpoint is `GET /recurring-invoices` (confirmed working — returns 2 MONTHLY templates for "UX Delivered Deli"). Operators can only manage recurring invoices via direct API calls; there is no UI surface for viewing, creating, editing, or deleting recurring invoice templates.
- **Expected:** The invoices screen should include a "Recurring" tab that lists all recurring invoice templates, with options to create, edit, pause, and delete templates. Navigating to `/invoices/recurring` should render this screen.
- **Actual:** There is no Recurring tab on the invoices screen. `/invoices/recurring` redirects to `/home`. Recurring invoices are entirely invisible in the operator UI and can only be managed via direct API calls.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator and navigate to Finance > Invoices.
  2. Observe no Recurring tab exists.
  3. Navigate to `/invoices/recurring` — redirected to `/home`.
- **Fix:** Add a "Recurring" tab to the invoices list page that calls `GET /recurring-invoices`. Add a "Create Recurring" button that opens a form for frequency, start date, end date, customer, and line items. Ensure the detail view allows editing and deactivating templates.
- **Prevention:** Add recurring invoices to the invoices feature acceptance criteria. Add a smoke test asserting the Recurring tab renders with the correct template count. Ensure the API endpoint discovery step in QA always verifies that each backend endpoint has a corresponding UI surface before release.

### RF-183 — Standing order `origin` field is `null` on auto-generated orders; cannot filter by standing-order origin

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** order-management / data-integrity
- **Surface:** API
- **Source:** W26 / E2E-3
- **Issue:** Orders auto-generated from standing order templates exist in the database (confirmed by `notes` text: "Auto-generated from standing order: UX Weekly Protein & Almonds"), but their `origin` field is `null` and `templateId` is `null`. `GET /orders?origin=standing` returns 0 results. Operators cannot filter orders by origin to identify which were auto-generated vs manually placed. The standing order metadata is lost at creation time.
- **Expected:** Orders auto-generated from standing order templates should have `origin: "STANDING_ORDER"` and `templateId: <uuid>` set, and `GET /orders?origin=STANDING_ORDER` should return only those orders.
- **Actual:** Auto-generated standing orders have `origin: null` and `templateId: null`. There is no way to distinguish auto-generated orders from manually placed ones. `GET /orders?origin=standing` returns 0 results.
- **Repro:**
  1. Confirm auto-generated standing orders exist (identified by `notes` text).
  2. Call `GET /orders?origin=STANDING_ORDER` — receive 0 results.
  3. Inspect the auto-generated orders — `origin` and `templateId` are both null.
- **Fix:** When the standing order cron job creates an order, set `origin: "STANDING_ORDER"` and `templateId: <template-uuid>` on the created order record. Update the orders DTO to expose these fields. Add a `?origin=STANDING_ORDER` filter to `GET /orders`.
- **Prevention:** Add a unit test for the standing order cron asserting created orders have `origin: "STANDING_ORDER"` and a non-null `templateId`. Add `origin` and `templateId` to the orders list filter UI so operators can identify auto-generated orders without inspecting the `notes` field.

---

### RF-184 — Auth tokens stored in plain `localStorage` — accessible to any JavaScript on the page

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** auth-security
- **Surface:** web-operator / web-buyer
- **Source:** W26 / E2E-7
- **Issue:** Both the operator token (`accessToken`, `refreshToken`) and buyer token (`buyerAccessToken`, `buyerRefreshToken`) are stored in plain `localStorage`, making them readable by any JavaScript executing on the page (including third-party scripts, injected ads, or XSS payloads). Combined with RF-157 (stored XSS via SVG upload), an attacker who exploits RF-157 can trivially steal all active session tokens.
- **Expected:** Auth tokens should be stored in `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict` cookies where JavaScript cannot read them, preventing token theft via XSS.
- **Actual:** All auth tokens are stored in plain `localStorage`. Any XSS payload (e.g., RF-157's stored SVG scripts) can read `localStorage.accessToken` and exfiltrate it to an attacker's server.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as operator or buyer.
  2. Open browser DevTools > Application > Local Storage.
  3. Observe `accessToken`, `refreshToken`, `buyerAccessToken`, `buyerRefreshToken` stored in plain text, readable by any JavaScript.
- **Fix:** Move auth tokens to `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict` cookies, which are not accessible to JavaScript. If cookie-based auth is impractical (e.g., cross-origin Expo mobile app), at minimum store tokens in `sessionStorage` (cleared on tab close) rather than `localStorage`, and implement short access-token expiry (current 15 min is acceptable) with secure refresh-token rotation.
- **Prevention:** Fix RF-157 (SVG XSS) as the immediate priority — this eliminates the primary XSS vector that makes localStorage token storage critical. For long-term defense: move to HttpOnly cookies and add a security test asserting auth tokens are not accessible via `document.cookie` or `localStorage` in a JavaScript context.

## Phase 12 — Standing Orders, Locale/Timezone, and Memory Bug Regressions (W32)

**Worker:** W32 | **Date:** 2026-04-30 | **Method:** Chrome browser + javascript_tool

### Phase 9.D — Standing Order Scheduling Corners

**Template discovery:** Two MONTHLY recurring invoice templates exist in the ux-audit tenant. Both have `lastRunAt: null` — the scheduler has never fired. One is paused (`isActive: false`), one active (`isActive: true`), both with `nextRunAt: 2026-05-01`.

**Pause/enable:** `PATCH /recurring-invoices/{id}` with `{isActive: false}` returns 200 and persists correctly. No per-occurrence "skip" capability exists — toggling `isActive` is all-or-nothing.

**Edit propagation:** Notes edits persist successfully (200 OK). Child invoice propagation is untestable because the scheduler has never fired in this tenant.

**Buyer-side:** `GET /buyer/standing-orders` → 404 (already filed as RF-180).

### E2E-8 — Locale and Timezone

**Date display:** Orders page shows `"Apr 30"` (relative-month), invoices `"3/30/2026"` (US M/D/YYYY), finance `"Apr 28, 2026"`. No raw UTC strings shown. Currency is consistent US dollar format across all pages. **PASS.**

**requestedDeliveryDate storage:** `POST /orders` with `requestedDeliveryDate: '2026-12-31T23:30:00Z'` → 201, but response and follow-up GET show field as `null`. **RF-173 CONFIRMED.**

### Memory Bug Regressions

| #2 Buyer can cancel own PENDING order | PASS | POST /buyer/orders/{id}/cancel → 200 "Order cancelled" |
| #3 PATCH /customers/me buyer profile edit | PARTIAL-FAIL | Fix works for operator-token CUSTOMER flow; PATCH /buyer/profile → 404 |
| #4 GET /buyer/returns filtered to customer | FAIL | Endpoint missing entirely → 404 |
| #5 Route-run create with no POD photos → 201 | PASS | Confirmed, no constraint error |
