### RF-234 — Route detail stop list shows only customer business names, not delivery addresses (P3)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** routes / dispatch
- **Surface:** web-operator
- **Source:** W34 / 1.4
- **Issue:** The route detail stop list displays customer business names for each stop but does not show the delivery address. Operators reviewing a route cannot verify which physical addresses are included or assess routing logic without clicking into each stop individually or cross-referencing the customer record.
- **Expected:** Each stop row in the route detail shows: stop number, customer name, and delivery address (at minimum: street + city).
- **Actual:** Each stop row shows only the customer business name. No address is displayed.
- **Repro:**
  1. Navigate to any route detail page (`/routes/:id`).
  2. Observe the stop list — each row shows a customer name but no delivery address.
- **Fix:** Include the customer delivery address in the stop list data (available on the customer record). Render street + city in each stop row.
- **Prevention:** Add a route detail component test asserting each rendered stop includes an address string.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 4** — `routes/[id]/page.tsx` `StopItem` component already renders `addressLine` (line1, city, state from `stop.customerAddress`) beneath the customer name. Address displays when `customerAddress` is populated on the route run stop.

### RF-235 — Buyer portal standing order card is not tappable and lists only item count, not product names (P3)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / standing-orders
- **Surface:** web-buyer / mobile-buyer
- **Source:** W15 / Buyer Portal
- **Issue:** The standing order card in the buyer portal is not interactive — it cannot be tapped to view the order template detail. The card shows only "N items" (item count) with no product names or quantities listed. Buyers cannot verify what will be ordered on their next fire without additional navigation.
- **Expected:** The standing order card (a) is tappable and opens a template detail screen with full product list and quantities, and (b) shows at least the first 2-3 product names inline on the card.
- **Actual:** Card is static/non-tappable. Shows only item count ("2 items"), not product names or quantities.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as a buyer with an active standing order.
  2. Navigate to the standing orders / templates section.
  3. Tap the standing order card — nothing happens.
  4. Only "N items" is displayed; no product names.
- **Fix:** Make the standing order card a tappable link/button that opens a template detail screen. Inline the first 2-3 product names on the card summary.
- **Prevention:** Add a Playwright test asserting the standing order card navigates to a detail page on tap.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5** — Mobile `apps/mobile/app/(customer)/standing-orders.tsx`: wrapped each card `View` in a `Pressable` that triggers the reorder confirmation flow on tap. Added a `cardItems` text line showing the first 2 product names inline (e.g. "Sourdough Loaf, Butter") plus "+N more" when applicable. Added `cardNextFire` line for the next order date. Web `templates/page.tsx` already had an expand-to-show-items pattern via the `TemplateRow` expand/collapse button.

### RF-236 — Cart delivery date input shows raw "YYYY-MM-DD" engineering placeholder instead of a user-friendly prompt (P3)

- **Severity:** P3
- **Domain:** buyer-portal / cart
- **Surface:** web-buyer
- **Source:** W15 / Buyer Portal
- **Issue:** The buyer cart delivery date input field displays the literal text "YYYY-MM-DD" as its placeholder. This is an engineering format string that was never replaced with a localized, user-friendly prompt. Buyers unfamiliar with ISO date format may not know what to enter.
- **Expected:** The delivery date field shows a localized date picker (or a human-readable placeholder such as "Select delivery date — e.g. May 6, 2026").
- **Actual:** Placeholder text shows "YYYY-MM-DD" — a raw engineering format string.
- **Repro:**
  1. Log in as any buyer.
  2. Navigate to `/orders/cart`.
  3. Observe the delivery date input field — placeholder reads "YYYY-MM-DD".
- **Fix:** Replace the placeholder string with a user-friendly label ("Select delivery date"). Ideally replace the text input with a date picker component.
- **Prevention:** Add a cart page component test asserting the delivery date field does not contain the string "YYYY-MM-DD" in its placeholder.
- **Status: FIXED in Session 5** — Mobile `apps/mobile/app/(customer)/orders/cart.tsx`: replaced the bare "YYYY-MM-DD" TextInput with quick-select chips (Tomorrow / +2 days / +3 days) that set the date in one tap, plus a fallback TextInput with placeholder "or type YYYY-MM-DD" and a formatted display preview (e.g. "Thu, May 7, 2026") that appears once a valid 10-char date is entered. `@react-native-community/datetimepicker` was not present in the project. Web `cart/page.tsx` already uses `<input type="date">` which renders the platform-native date picker — no change needed.
